## Goal

Implement the canonical append-only session-event contract, its tamper-evident integrity protocol, and the PII-column indirection — providing the register-and-append-path infrastructure every other plan emits into.

## Scope

This plan covers the `EventEnvelope` contract and version semantics, the 159-event taxonomy registry across 20 categories per [Spec-006 §Event Type Summary](../specs/006-session-event-taxonomy-and-audit-log.md#event-type-summary) (140 per the 2026-07-02 Spec-006 B1 amendment, +1 `pty.control_changed` per the 2026-07-06 B4 amendment, +15 with the `mcp_governance` category per the 2026-07-22 B18 amendment, +2 (`agent.provider_switched`, `agent.provider_switch_failed`) under `session_lifecycle` per the 2026-08-26 same-agent provider-switch amendment, and +1 (`session.peer_invocation_set`) under `session_lifecycle` per the 2026-08-26 Spec-030 peer-invocation amendment — closed into contracts by T1.10, Plan-016 T1.13, and Plan-030 T4.2 respectively; the §Event Type Summary canonical count), append-only persistence with BLAKE3 hash chain and Ed25519 signatures over RFC 8785 JCS canonical bytes, Merkle anchor emission into the shared `event_log_anchors` table (metadata only per [ADR-017](../decisions/017-shared-event-sourcing-scope.md)), the PII-column indirection pattern (`pii_payload` ciphertext + `pii_ciphertext_digest`), replay reads, live subscriptions, and compaction to audit stubs. It also owns the cross-cutting `sourceEpoch` + `sourcePosition` epoch-attribution payload carrier pair (the CP-004-12 registration, T1.9 — stamped and consumed by Plan-004's rollback legs; registered here because the typed payload-field shape lives in this plan's `event.ts` contract surface).

Plan-006 is the canonical emitter of the `event_maintenance` and `audit_integrity` categories (6 event types total). It is not the emitter of the remaining 18 categories — see §Event Taxonomy Coverage for the ownership boundary.

## Non-Goals

- Full timeline UI rendering (Plan-013)
- Metrics and dashboard implementation (Plan-020)
- Emission of events owned by other plans: `session.*` lifecycle (Plan-001 — except the two `session.goal_*` events, which are Plan-016-owned per the census row below: the goal set/clear RPC surface, 2026-07-02 campaign; and the three B18 provider-surface observations, Plan-005-normalized), `runtime_node.*` and `session.clock_*` (Plan-003 / Plan-015), `dispatch.*` (Spec-024 implementation), `participant.*` purge trigger (Plan-022; Plan-006 emits only the `event.shredded` audit artifact, recording the Path-1 crypto-shred), `policy_bundle.*` (V1.1 Cedar runtime bundle loader), the five `mcp.*` governance events ([Plan-028](./028-mcp-server-configuration-and-governance.md), 2026-07-22 B18), `user.message` (the Plan-004/B9 queue-submit surface), and the B18 provider-surface `run.*`/`usage.*` additions (Plan-005 normalizer per the census rows below)
- Crypto-shred fan-out orchestration (Plan-022 owns the three-path orchestrator; Plan-006 provides the append path and emits `event.shredded` for the Path-1 crypto-shred, emitted after Path 3 per Spec-022 ordering)
- Cedar policy bundle runtime loading (V1.1 per [ADR-012](../decisions/012-cedar-approval-policy-engine.md) §Decision — V1 compiles policies into the daemon image at build time)

No event type, category, error code, or wire method is minted.

Integrity protocol invariants ([Spec-006 §Integrity Protocol](../specs/006-session-event-taxonomy-and-audit-log.md#integrity-protocol), [Spec-022 §Signature Safety Under Shred](../specs/022-data-retention-and-gdpr.md#signature-safety-under-shred)) are load-bearing — changes to canonical-form fields or field ordering require an ADR-018 MINOR envelope bump and cannot be retrofitted to already-signed rows.

Target paths below assume the canonical implementation topology defined in [Container Architecture](../architecture/container-architecture.md).

## Target Areas

The `events/` subdirectory does not exist in the contracts package; the top-level co-location pattern (Plan-001 baseline: 16 categories + 3-variant union + factory + registry) is the canonical layout. Also exports the cross-cutting `SourceEpochSchema` / `SourcePositionSchema` epoch-attribution carrier pair with their payload-key consts, the `withEpochStamp` pairing helper, and the `ORIGIN_POSITION_STUB_KEY` stub-projection key const (T1.9, the CP-004-12 registration); T1.11/T1.12 (minted by the 2026-08-01 T4.10 targeted readiness-audit delta) extend the same file with the six exported Plan-006-emitted payload schemas + their `SessionEventSchema` union arms and the five imported `runtime_node.*` union arms.

the session's uploaded anchors stay emitter-only-verifiable until then), retry the typed authorization refusal (`runtimenode.permission_denied`) on that same backoff (pre-admission timing is its ordinary cause — the attempt can fire the moment the session materializes locally, so it converges when the control plane admits the membership; the pre-decoupling delivery-release choreography died with the machinery); the retry loop's lifetime is the locally-materialized session's lifetime — terminal on success, conflict, or local teardown, parked (never terminated) on the floor refusal

### Bootstrap Wiring

The bullets above name the file each service lives in. This subsection names who **constructs** it in production as of the Phase-3 ship (2026-08-04), so a reader cannot mistake a shipped module for a running one. Deferred wiring is named with the task that owns it; nothing here is aspirational.

- **`packages/runtime-daemon/src/bootstrap/index.ts` is the daemon's only composition root, and it wires no Plan-006 service.** As shipped it loads the Spec-027 secure-defaults config and exposes the pre-bind assertion Plan-007 gates listener binds on (I-007-1); it constructs nothing from `events/`. The T3.1/T4.2 events wiring described in the `bootstrap/index.ts` bullet above is **T4.2's edit (F-006-HALT-04)** and has not been dispatched. Every Phase-3 daemon service consequently has its only in-tree construction sites under `__tests__/`.
- **`EventLogService` (T3.1)** — production construction is T4.2's. Its `haltSource` dependency is optional and defaults to the vacuous `NeverHaltedIngestHaltSource`, a deliberate **fail-OPEN** default: until T4.2 passes the `IngestHaltRegistry`, no halt is observable and every session is admitted. Its `piiEncryptor` is likewise composition-root-injected per CP-006-1.
- **`IngestHaltRegistry` (T3.1)** — the production halt-state carrier, with no production construction site. Its halted set is populated by T4.2's key-reuse observer in the registry → service → observer construction order that same T4.2 edit owns.
- **`Compactor` (T3.2)** — `tick()` has **no production caller**: the never-during-active-runs precondition belongs to the idle scheduler that would own the call, and no such scheduler exists. Two further gates sit behind that one. The pass probes the daemon-scope sentinel session's signing key before mutating any row and refuses with zero rows mutated when it is unresolvable, so **compaction stays inert until CP-006-7 provisions that key** — which makes the compactor a SECOND consumer of the sentinel-key provisioning precondition, beside T4.2's `key_reuse_detected` emitter. Its optional `rollbackAttributionSource` separately defaults to the vacuous current-attribution source until Plan-004 T3.14 supplies the real one.
- **`MerkleAnchorService` (T3.3)** — no production construction site. Its only in-tree caller is the compactor's anchor-before-compaction force-fire through the injected anchor seam; the upload leg depends on the deferred credential provider below, so the durable partition queue drains no earlier than Tier 5.
- **`SchemaMigrationEmitter` (T3.4)** — no production construction site, and **no batch-boundary seam is bound**: the daemon migration runner's `applyMigrations` takes the database handle alone and exposes no batch-finish hook, so the primary path (`emitBatchCompletion`) and the reconcile fallback (`reconcileOnStartup`) are both caller-invoked surfaces awaiting the same composition root T4.2 opens.
- **`DaemonCredentialProvider` (T3.3, CP-006-13)** — an interface plus one in-tree implementation that refuses every mint with a Tier-5 deferral. The real implementation and its composition-root wiring land with Plan-018's Tier-5 PASETO work (decomposed 2026-08-15 as Plan-018 T5.4–T5.7, with T5.6's presented-token arm registered on this declaration — NS-62); until then every live credentialed leg (anchor upload, T4.2's roster read, T4.10's registrar, Plan-024 T-024-3B-3's lease publisher, and Plan-014's relay upload/fetch-mint calls — CP-014-5; the seam's full consumer set is live-plus-forward-declared per CP-006-13) is unreachable by construction rather than by flag.
- **Control-plane `event-anchors/` (T3.3) — the one production wiring that HAS landed.** `createEventAnchorRouter` is mounted into the namespaced control-plane router on Plan-008's `host.ts`, discharging CP-006-2, and `EventLogAnchorStore` is constructed there over that host's placeholder `Querier` whose `query` / `exec` / `transaction` each throw the Tier-5 deferral. So `eventanchor.upload` is registered and reachable, and a gate-passing call throws at the storage boundary — the same posture as every sibling procedure on that host, not a Plan-006-specific gap.

## Data And Storage Changes

- Implement read/write semantics for `session_events` columns forward-declared by Plan-001 per [Plan-001 §Cross-Plan Forward-Declared Schema](./001-shared-session-core.md#cross-plan-forward-declared-schema): `monotonic_ns`, `prev_hash`, `row_hash`, `daemon_signature`, `participant_signature`, `pii_payload`, plus the payload-embedded `pii_ciphertext_digest` field.
- Add `session_events.pii_participant_id` (TEXT, nullable) — the durable PII owner stamp. Plan-006-CREATED via T3.1's additive migration rather than forward-declared by Plan-001, so it is deliberately absent from the list above; it carries the same participant id T2.4's codec signs into `payload` under `PII_PARTICIPANT_ID_PAYLOAD_KEY`, giving I-006-2-12's read-side check a durable copy to hold the signed claim to. NULL on every row with no `pii_payload`.
- Add `session_events.content_payload` (BLOB, nullable) — the durable encrypted home for **machine-authored** prose: the assistant message body, the reasoning-update body, and tool-call arguments / result / error bodies (2026-08-30, §6 node NS-94). Plan-006-CREATED via Phase 3B's additive migration, like `pii_participant_id` and unlike the Plan-001 forward-declared list above; the ordinal is deliberately unpinned here (`0NNN-content-payload.ts`, the `received_from_node_id` placeholder idiom) because ordinals are claimed in merge order and one is already in flight. It is a **sibling of `pii_payload`, not a widening of it**: sealed AES-256-GCM under the **session**-scoped content key rather than a participant's, so the two columns partition one row's text by authorship and erasing a person never requires erasing a session. Excluded from the canonical bytes, committed to by the payload-embedded `contentCiphertextDigest`, and carrying **no owner-stamp sibling column** — `session_id` is already a canonical signed member, so the key's binding is signed already and a second column would restate it. NULL on every row with no machine-authored body, and NULL **by construction** on every `audit_integrity` / `event_maintenance` row. Canonical definition at [Local SQLite Schema §Session Events](../architecture/schemas/local-sqlite-schema.md#session-events-plan-001-extended-by-plans-006-008-015), which is the build-time authority: change the doc first, then mirror it into the migration.
- Add shared `event_log_anchors` table (Postgres) — columns `session_id`, `node_id`, `start_sequence`, `end_sequence`, `merkle_root`, `root_signature`, `anchored_at`. Metadata only; the control plane does **not** receive session event payloads per [ADR-017](../decisions/017-shared-event-sourcing-scope.md).
- Add shared `daemon_signing_public_keys` table (Postgres, T4.10 — the 2026-07-29 CP-006-7 amendment) — columns `session_id`, `node_id`, `public_key`, `registered_at`, `PRIMARY KEY (session_id, node_id)`: the register-once daemon verification-key roster external verifiers resolve via `runtimenode.signingkeyroster`. Deliberately **no participant FK** — outside the [Spec-022 §Shred Fan-Out](../specs/022-data-retention-and-gdpr.md#shred-fan-out) Path-2 `REFERENCES participants(id)` closure, so the key survives participant erasure for the audit-retention lifetime of the anchors it verifies. Canonical DDL at [shared-postgres-schema.md §Daemon Signing Public Keys (Plan-006 — Verification-Key Roster)](../architecture/schemas/shared-postgres-schema.md#daemon-signing-public-keys-plan-006--verification-key-roster).
- Extend local `session_snapshots` with replay-cursor state and a flag indicating whether a range contains compacted regions.
- **No `session_events_shared` table.** Per `ADR-017 §Decision`, V1 ships Option B (per-daemon local logs). Cross-participant events are distributed by the relay as pairwise-encrypted payloads and appended to each receiving daemon's local log with that daemon's own per-session sequence number.
- See [Local SQLite Schema](../architecture/schemas/local-sqlite-schema.md) and [Shared Postgres Schema](../architecture/schemas/shared-postgres-schema.md) for canonical column definitions.

## API And Transport Changes

- Define `EventEnvelope` v1.0 with `.version = "1.0"` at emit per [Spec-006 §EventEnvelope Version Semantics](../specs/006-session-event-taxonomy-and-audit-log.md#eventenvelope-version-semantics) — semver `"MAJOR.MINOR"` per [ADR-018 §Decision #1](../decisions/018-cross-version-compatibility.md#decision); producer-set at emit time per §Decision #2; immutable per [Spec-006 §EventEnvelope Version Semantics](../specs/006-session-event-taxonomy-and-audit-log.md#eventenvelope-version-semantics) (Immutability) + `ADR-018 §Decision` #6 (upcaster chain on read, never log rewrite) — rewriting `.version` would break the hash chain and signatures, which both commit to canonical bytes including `.version`.
- Register `EventReadAfterCursor`, `EventReadWindow`, and `EventSubscription` in the shared client SDK and daemon or control-plane contracts.
- Register typed `VERSION_FLOOR_EXCEEDED` and `VERSION_CEILING_EXCEEDED` in [Error Contracts](../architecture/contracts/error-contracts.md) before the first Plan-001 emitter lands, per `ADR-018 §Decision` #10 (pre-Plan-001 registration mandate; below-floor write behavior defined in §Decision #4).
- See [API Payload Contracts](../architecture/contracts/api-payload-contracts.md) for typed schemas.

- `event_maintenance`: `schema.migrated`, `event.compacted`, `event.shredded`

Plan-006 provides the append-path infrastructure, canonical envelope, and integrity protocol that all other emitter plans write into; it does not emit their category entries.

**V1 vs V1.1 emission scope (`policy_events`).** Per [ADR-012 §Decision](../decisions/012-cedar-approval-policy-engine.md#decision), V1 compiles Cedar policies into the daemon image at build time and evaluates them from V1 in the **resident, signature-verified `@cedar-policy/cedar-wasm` evaluator** (§Scope By Phase); only runtime **policy-bundle** loading into that already-resident evaluator is V1.1 — the WASM evaluator itself is not deferred. The `policy_bundle.loaded` / `policy_bundle.rejected` types are registered in the V1 taxonomy so the registry is complete from V1 forward, but their emitter ships in V1.1 as part of the bundle loader. Plan-006 implements the type registration only; a V1.1 plan owns the bundle-loader emission surface.

## Integrity Protocol

Plan-006 implements the tamper-evidence protocol defined in [Spec-006 §Integrity Protocol](../specs/006-session-event-taxonomy-and-audit-log.md#integrity-protocol).

### Canonical Serialization

Per [Spec-006 §Canonical Serialization Rules](../specs/006-session-event-taxonomy-and-audit-log.md#canonical-serialization-rules):

- Canonicalization: [RFC 8785 JSON Canonicalization Scheme (JCS)](https://datatracker.ietf.org/doc/html/rfc8785). The canonicalizer is shared with [Spec-024](../specs/024-cross-node-dispatch-and-approval.md)'s `request_body_hash` so the daemon runs one canonicalization rule across integrity and dispatch.
- Hash function: [BLAKE3](https://github.com/BLAKE3-team/BLAKE3-specs/blob/master/blake3.pdf) for both the hash-chain digest and the `pii_ciphertext_digest`.
- Signature scheme: [RFC 8032 §5.1 — Ed25519](https://datatracker.ietf.org/doc/html/rfc8032#section-5.1).
- Fields included (the canonical set — membership only; serialized order is mandated by RFC 8785 §3.2.3 UTF-16 code-unit lex-sort of member names, NOT the order listed here): `id`, `sessionId`, `sequence`, `occurredAt`, `category`, `type`, `actor`, `payload`, `correlationId`, `causationId`, `version`.
- Present-but-null fields are serialized as `null`, not omitted — "absent" vs "present-but-null" are wire-distinguishable.
- `occurredAt` is RFC 3339 UTC with millisecond precision (`YYYY-MM-DDTHH:MM:SS.sssZ`) so ordering is byte-stable.
- `pii_payload` is NOT in the canonical form. Events with non-NULL `pii_payload` embed `pii_ciphertext_digest` inside `payload` (and `payload` IS canonical) so the signature commits to a one-way digest of the ciphertext.

### Hash Chain

Every `session_events` row:

- `prev_hash` = `row_hash` of the immediately-prior row for the same `session_id` (genesis row uses 32 zero bytes as `prev_hash`).
- `row_hash` = `BLAKE3(prev_hash || canonical_bytes(row))`.

Verification: recompute `canonical_bytes(row)`, recompute `BLAKE3(prev_hash || canonical_bytes(row))`, compare to the stored `row_hash`. Mismatch halts replay at the offending sequence and surfaces `audit_integrity_failed` with `failureMode = 'hash_mismatch'`.

### Ed25519 Signatures

- `daemon_signature` = `Ed25519(daemon_signing_key, canonical_bytes(row))`.
- `participant_signature` = `Ed25519(participant_signing_key, canonical_bytes(row))` — optional, populated for participant-authoritative events (e.g., approval decisions per [Spec-012](../specs/012-approvals-permissions-and-trust-boundaries.md)).
- Verification uses the `NodeId`-resolved public key from the session participant roster per [Security Architecture §Audit Log Integrity](../architecture/security-architecture.md#audit-log-integrity).

### Merkle Anchor Emission

Per [Spec-006 §Anchoring Cadence](../specs/006-session-event-taxonomy-and-audit-log.md#anchoring-cadence):

- Anchors fire on the earlier of `ANCHOR_INTERVAL_EVENTS = 1000` events OR `ANCHOR_INTERVAL_SECONDS = 300` seconds since the previous anchor.
- Anchor payload = `(session_id, node_id, start_sequence, end_sequence, merkle_root, root_signature, anchored_at)` — metadata only.
- `root_signature` = `Ed25519(daemon_signing_key, anchor_claim)` — same daemon key used for row signatures, over the RFC 8785 canonicalization of the five-member anchor claim `{endSequence, merkleRoot, nodeId, sessionId, startSequence}` per `Spec-006 §Anchoring Cadence` (2026-08-11 amendment, Codex PR #323 round 2 — previously `merkle_root` alone, which left the coordinates a coverage test runs against unsigned; pre-first-release, no production anchors exist, and the shipped T3.3 signer takes the preimage update in the immediate follow-up code PR this plan's §Notes 2026-08-11 record names — landed 2026-08-12, PR #324).
- Anchors are uploaded to Postgres `event_log_anchors`; no session event payloads are sent to the control plane per ADR-017.
- Partition tolerance: if control-plane upload fails, anchors queue locally and flush on reconnect without re-signing. Local hash chain and signatures provide tamper-evidence without the anchor tier; anchors provide external cross-observer consistency.

## PII Columns

Plan-006 owns the read/write semantics for the `session_events.pii_payload BLOB` column forward-declared in Plan-001, and — since 2026-08-30 — for its machine-authored sibling `session_events.content_payload BLOB` and the `session_content_keys` table that holds its wrapped sealing key, both of which this plan creates rather than inherits (§Content Payload Column below).

### Encrypt-Then-Digest-Then-Sign Order

The write-path order is LOAD-BEARING for [Spec-022 §Signature Safety Under Shred](../specs/022-data-retention-and-gdpr.md#signature-safety-under-shred). Reversing any step breaks the safety proof — a signature directly over `pii_payload` ciphertext would leave signed bytes on disk after a shred, enabling length and structure attacks.

1. Extract PII fields from the in-memory event object (user messages, file paths, code snippets per [Spec-022 §PII Payload Column Pattern](../specs/022-data-retention-and-gdpr.md#pii-payload-column-pattern)).
2. Encrypt PII under the participant's AES-256-GCM key (key resolved from `participant_keys.encrypted_key_blob`; table owned by Plan-022 per `Plan-001 §Cross-Plan Forward-Declared Schema`). Output: `pii_ciphertext` bytes.
3. Compute `pii_ciphertext_digest = BLAKE3(pii_ciphertext)`.
4. Embed **two** members into the `payload` field: `pii_ciphertext_digest` (which bytes the row holds) and `pii_participant_id` (whose key sealed them — the owner stamp, per I-006-2-12).
5. Canonicalize the envelope (per §Canonical Serialization above). `pii_payload` is excluded; both embedded members are included because they live inside `payload`. The asymmetry is deliberate: excluding the ciphertext protects the shred, while the stamp is an identifier no shred erases and discloses no new CLASS of datum, `actor` being a canonical member already carrying participant ids (I-006-2-05's scope clarification).
6. Compute `row_hash = BLAKE3(prev_hash || canonical_bytes)` and `daemon_signature = Ed25519(canonical_bytes)`.
7. Persist the row: `payload` (carrying the digest and the stamp), `pii_payload` (ciphertext), `pii_participant_id` (the durable stamp column, byte-equal to the signed claim), `row_hash`, `daemon_signature`, `participant_signature?`.

The single write path is `pii-indirection.ts`; callers MUST NOT construct `pii_payload` bytes by any other route.

### Read Path

- `pii_payload IS NULL` — no decryption; return payload verbatim.
- `pii_payload` non-NULL and decryption key available — load ciphertext, decrypt under the participant AES-256-GCM key, merge decrypted PII back into the payload for the authorized caller.
- `pii_payload` non-NULL and key absent (crypto-shred has occurred; `participant_keys` row DELETEd in Plan-022 Path 1) — return the envelope + payload (including `pii_ciphertext_digest`) with the participant's PII partition replaced **as a unit** by a single `<pii-shredded>` marker at one reserved payload key, never field by field: the PII field _names_ are themselves inside the ciphertext the reader can no longer open, so per-field marking would demand a schema the shred just made unknowable. Signature and hash verification remain valid because canonical bytes exclude `pii_payload`. This partition-level projection is a **contract, not shipped production behavior** — T3.5's shred-safety suite pins it (marker at one reserved key, plus the negative property that no plaintext byte survives the projection) against a suite-local reader; the production read path that implements it is T4.3's `replay-service.ts`, which has not been dispatched.

### Content Payload Column (Machine-Authored Prose)

`content_payload` is built by Phase 3B and reuses the Encrypt-Then-Digest-Then-Sign order above **step for step**, substituting only the key and the embedded members: step 2 seals under the session content key — a random 32-byte AES-256 DEK generated once per session and held wrapped in `session_content_keys`, never derived from the master key — with AAD = `session_id || event_id`; step 3 computes `contentCiphertextDigest = BLAKE3(content_ciphertext)`; step 4 embeds **one** member rather than two, the digest, because the owner stamp's job is done by `session_id`, already canonical and already signed. Steps 1, 5, 6, and 7 are unchanged, and a row carrying both columns runs the order once over both partitions rather than twice — one canonicalization per row (I-006-2-06) is not weakened by a second ciphertext.

Two write-path rules are inherited deliberately, not merely by analogy:

- **Sole write path.** `pii-indirection.ts` is extended to seal this column too, and remains the only route by which either ciphertext is constructed; callers MUST NOT produce `content_payload` bytes anywhere else. Housing both codecs in one module is the point — it is the module the digest-embedding and canonicalization order lives in, and a second sealing module would be a second place for that order to drift.
- **`audit_integrity` and `event_maintenance` carry no `content_payload`.** The same discriminated-union type on `RawEventInput` that makes `pii_payload` unconstructible for those categories (I-006-2-07) makes this column unconstructible for them, and for the same reason: [§Audit Integrity Invariant](#audit-integrity-invariant) makes those categories never-compacted and never-shredded, so a destroyable body on such a row would be a body no mechanism in this plan may ever destroy.

**Read path.** Three cases, mirroring §Read Path above with one difference that matters — and in **none** of them is `payload` mutated. The reader returns a **`HydratedSessionEvent`**: the verified event exactly as its signature covers it, paired with the closed two-arm `content` union T3.6 declares. (1) `content_payload IS NULL` — `content` takes the **unavailable** arm with reason `absent`; there was never a body. (2) Non-NULL, and the session content key unwraps from `session_content_keys` and opens the ciphertext — `content` takes the **available** arm carrying the plaintext body beside the `contentLength` / `contentTruncated` the signed payload already states. (3) Non-NULL and **unopenable** — the daemon master key is unavailable, the wrapped key row is missing, or the ciphertext fails its AEAD tag — `content` takes the **unavailable** arm with the reason that distinguishes those cases; it never fabricates an empty body, because an empty body reads as "the assistant said nothing" and that is a different and false claim. Case (3) is exactly the condition the canonical-transcript fold reports as `'turn_content_unavailable'` ([Spec-006 §Session Lifecycle](../specs/006-session-event-taxonomy-and-audit-log.md#session-lifecycle-session_lifecycle)); there is no fourth, post-shred case, because no per-participant shred reaches this column. **Merging the body into `payload` is prohibited, not merely undesirable** (2026-08-30, Codex PR #383 round 3, correcting this paragraph's own earlier wording): the five `SessionEventSchema` variants are strict and declare no body member, so a merge would either add an undeclared member that fails validation or make an already-verified payload differ from the bytes its signature covers. T3.8's `content-read.ts` is bound by the same rule.

**Bound.** The sealer enforces `CONTENT_PAYLOAD_PLAINTEXT_MAX = 262144` bytes of UTF-8 plaintext, truncating an over-bound body at a codepoint boundary and setting `contentTruncated: true` in `payload` while `contentLength` keeps the **pre-truncation** length. It truncates rather than refusing: refusing the append would drop the turn, which is a worse and less honest outcome than storing a prefix that says it is one. No sentinel character is written into the text. The fold reports the prefix as `'turn_content_truncated'`.

## Shred Fan-Out Cross-References

Plan-006 participates in the Path 1 → Path 2 → Path 3 fan-out defined in [Spec-022 §Shred Fan-Out](../specs/022-data-retention-and-gdpr.md#shred-fan-out):

- **Plan-006 emits `event.shredded` (`Spec-006 §Event Maintenance (event_maintenance)`)** recording the Path-1 crypto-shred (SQLite `participant_keys` row DELETE) — but **emitted after Path 3 completes**, per the [Spec-022 §Shred Fan-Out](../specs/022-data-retention-and-gdpr.md#shred-fan-out) ordering, so the "shredded" marker never precedes the Path-3 diagnostic-bucket flush — carrying `{participantId, affectedSessionIds[], piiPayloadsCleared, shredReason}`. The event has no PII content and is retained indefinitely.
- **`participant.purged` emission discipline.** Plan-022 owns the emit trigger for `participant.purged` (`Spec-006 §Participant Lifecycle (participant_lifecycle)`), but the timing is constrained by [Spec-022 §Ordering And Atomicity](../specs/022-data-retention-and-gdpr.md#ordering-and-atomicity): Plan-022 MUST NOT emit `participant.purged` until all three paths complete. Plan-006 provides the append path and does not short-circuit this ordering.
- **Partial-shred recovery.** On per-path failure, `participant.purge_requested` remains the most recent durable state per `Spec-022 §Fallback Behavior`. Plan-006's append path refuses to record a `participant.purged` until Plan-022's orchestrator reports all three paths complete; idempotent retries are safe because all three paths are idempotent per `Spec-022 §Ordering And Atomicity`.

## Audit Integrity Invariant

Events in the `audit_integrity` category (`audit_integrity_verified`, `audit_integrity_failed`, `key_reuse_detected`) **and** the `event_maintenance` category (`schema.migrated`, `event.compacted`, `event.shredded`) are subject to a shared invariant:

> **These events are never compacted. These events are never crypto-shredded.**

The invariant is declared symmetrically in [Spec-006 §Audit Integrity](../specs/006-session-event-taxonomy-and-audit-log.md#audit-integrity-audit_integrity) and [Spec-006 §Event Maintenance](../specs/006-session-event-taxonomy-and-audit-log.md#event-maintenance-event_maintenance). Rationale:

- Compacting `audit_integrity_failed` would destroy the record that a chain break was detected — a verifier could not distinguish "no failure occurred" from "the failure record was compacted away."
- Crypto-shredding `audit_integrity_*` events would defeat the tamper-evidence guarantee the protocol is designed to provide.
- Compacting `schema.migrated` would destroy the audit trail of a non-reversible schema change.
- Shredding `event.shredded` would be self-referential — the audit stub of the shred operation must survive indefinitely.

Plan-006 enforces the invariant at three layers:

- `compactor.ts` excludes events where `category IN ('audit_integrity', 'event_maintenance')` from compaction candidates and logs a contract test failure if ever a row from those categories is passed into the compaction selector.
- `pii-indirection.ts` refuses to write `pii_payload` for events in either category (these categories have `pii_payload = NULL` by construction — their content carries no participant PII).
- Shred fan-out Path 1 SQL selector explicitly excludes these categories from its shred scope — the rows the fan-out counts as PII-bearing, NOT columns it rewrites, Path 1 destroying the participant's key and overwriting no column (defense-in-depth; redundant with the previous rule because these rows' `pii_payload` is always NULL, but prevents regressions if a future event type is mis-categorized).

## Cross-Version Compatibility Surface

Plan-006 owns the `EventEnvelope` contract's integration with [ADR-018](../decisions/018-cross-version-compatibility.md):

- `.version` format `"MAJOR.MINOR"` per `ADR-018 §Decision` #1.
- Producer-set at emit per §Decision #2; never copied from received events.
- Unknown-MAJOR events persisted as version stubs retaining full canonical bytes per §Decision #5 (read-side behavior per §Decision #9 accept-and-stub); the row's `.version` stays the producer's original — version-stubbing never rewrites the canonical row.
- Unknown-MINOR optional fields and enum values preserved verbatim for future upcasting per [Spec-006 §EventEnvelope Version Semantics](../specs/006-session-event-taxonomy-and-audit-log.md#eventenvelope-version-semantics) + `ADR-018 §Decision` #8 (MINOR additive-only) composed with §Decision #6 (upcaster chain on read).
- MAJOR-mismatch at session join produces typed `VERSION_FLOOR_EXCEEDED` (below `session.min_client_version`) or `VERSION_CEILING_EXCEEDED` (above session's highest supported MAJOR) per §Decision #4 + §Decision #10.
- Event-type registry extensions within the same envelope MAJOR are additive-only per §Decision #8 — event-type renames are explicitly forbidden because readers dispatch on the name.

## Implementation Steps

Plan-006 ships in **four phases**, each phase = one PR. The phase boundaries align with the audit-derived dispatch graph (see [`docs/operations/plan-implementation-readiness-audit-runbook.md`](../operations/plan-implementation-readiness-audit-runbook.md)).

**Phase 1 — Contracts** (T1.1 – T1.12; T1.11/T1.12 minted by the 2026-08-01 T4.10 targeted readiness-audit delta, un-dispatched additions to the shipped T1.1–T1.10 set). Widen `packages/contracts/src/event.ts` in place per Plan-001's contracts package layout: `EventCategory` literal union to 19 entries; enumerate all 141 `SessionEventType` literals (the T1.1–T1.2 baseline; T1.10 — the 2026-07-22 B18 census closure — then widens to 20 entries / 156 literals, the B1→B4→B18 chain's terminal state); author the named `EventEnvelopeSchema`; bind `CapabilityDetails` typed wrapper for `runtime_node.capability_declared` / `runtime_node.capability_updated` payloads (closes Plan-005 CP-005-5); cross-link version-bound errors to Spec-006 + ADR-018; widen api-payload-contracts.md Plan-006 §EventEnvelope block from 16 to 19 categories; confirm envelope-to-`session_events`-column bijection; author the `EVENT_DISPOSITION_BY_KIND` normalized-kind disposition registry — keyed over a closed `NormalizedEventKind` literal union for compile-time totality — + its exhaustiveness test (set-equality of registry keys against the `NORMALIZED_EVENT_KINDS` census array in both directions — never a bare `size === 35` check); register the cross-cutting `SourceEpochSchema` / `SourcePositionSchema` epoch-attribution carrier pair with their payload-key consts, the `withEpochStamp` pairing helper, and the `ORIGIN_POSITION_STUB_KEY` stub-projection key const (T1.9, the CP-004-12 registration); author the six exported Plan-006-emitted payload schemas + their `SessionEventSchema` union arms and roster entries (T1.11, the docket-7 resolution); register the five Plan-003-authored `runtime_node.*` payload variants into the union (T1.12, the CP-003-1 leg (a) discharge).

**Phase 2 — Crypto Protocol Core** (T2.1 – T2.5, T2.7 — T2.6 was deleted 2026-07-26; the id is retired, not reused, and the reasoning is in `Plan-006 §Progress Log`). RFC 8785 JCS canonicalizer with UTF-16 lex-sort field ordering (`Spec-006 §Canonical Serialization Rules` amended in this PR to describe field membership and lex-sort serialization); BLAKE3 hash-chain + Ed25519 signer whose `verifyRow` fails closed on the 64-zero-byte `daemon_signature` placeholder (`signature_placeholder`, never a retro-sign — the replacement for the deleted T2.6); golden-vector contract tests over both the canonicalizer and the signer (RFC 8785 Appendix B Table 1 number-serialization vectors + project-specific edge cases); PII indirection codec enforcing encrypt → digest → embed → canonicalize → sign sole-write-path; post-shred signature-verification property test; **T2.7 — `DaemonSigningKeySource` interface + `OsKeystoreSealedDaemonSigningKeySource` implementation** (user-ratified 2026-05-28; self-contained signing-key custody using OS-keystore-managed master key + sealed BLOB persisted in the new local `daemon_signing_keys` SQLite table per ADR-004 SQLite-local-state boundary — corrected from the pre-Codex-T4-review draft that mis-located the column on shared-Postgres `sessions`).

**Phase 3 — Persistence + Maintenance** (T3.1 – T3.5). Append-path service writing the forward-declared integrity columns + Plan-022 Path 1 shred callback; compactor with three triggers (50K events / 90 days / 500MB) + audit-stub format + three-layer category-exclusion enforcement + new `session_events.retention_class` discriminator column (typed column over JSON-field probe); Merkle-anchor service with earlier-of-1000-events-or-300s cadence + new `pending_anchor_uploads` table for durable partition queue (Design A — SQLite table with `UNIQUE(session_id, node_id, start_sequence, end_sequence)`); schema-migration emitter on `AFTER_MIGRATE_OPERATION_FINISH` batch boundary with callback-seam primary path + reconcile-on-startup fallback (hybrid resolution); cross-task contract-test suite + end-to-end shred-safety regression (`Plan-006 §Test And Verification Plan` acceptance gate).

**Phase 4 — Read-Side + SDK + Desktop Stub** (T4.1 – T4.10). Three-check integrity verifier (chain → signature → anchor) plus per-row stub-signature and scalar-binding re-verification for compacted rows and a per-row stored-`occurred_at` canonical-form check on BOTH retention classes, emitting fifteen of the 16-value `failureMode` enum per `Spec-006 §Audit Integrity (audit_integrity)` (the sixteenth mode, `signing_key_slot_conflict`, is T4.10's registrar-side conflict alarm — in the shared enum, outside the verifier's emission set; post-amendment for the post-compaction integrity protocol — `anchor_missing_for_compacted_range` + `anchor_signature_invalid` + `stub_signature_invalid` + `stub_scalar_mismatch` are additive-MINOR extensions per `ADR-018 §Decision` #8 — plus the 2026-07-26 `signature_placeholder` fail-closed unsigned-row mode per `Spec-006 §Integrity Protocol`, plus the three 2026-07-27 signed-but-unchecked-binding modes consuming Phase 2's exported predicates: `occurred_at_not_canonical` from T2.1's `isCanonicalOccurredAt` per I-006-2-10, `pii_ciphertext_digest_unbound` from T2.4's `isCiphertextDigestBound` per I-006-2-11, and `pii_owner_stamp_unbound` from T2.4's `isPiiOwnerStampBound` per I-006-2-12); `key-reuse-observer.ts` enforcing `refuse_on_rotation` Sigstore-precedent invariant and halting ingest from every locally-hosted `(session_id, node_id)` identity the colliding key appears under (I-006-4-03's fail-closed-on-both-identities rule, scoped to local authority at Codex PR #278 round 3); `replay-service.ts` for `EventReadAfterCursor` + `EventReadWindow` honoring `retentionClass: 'audit_stub'` for compacted regions; new `session_snapshots.{has_compacted_ranges, compacted_range_count}` columns (existing `as_of_sequence` already carries cursor-state semantics; only the compaction-incidence flags are added); `EventSubscription` replay-then-live stream consuming `LocalSubscriptionProducer<EventEnvelope>` with sequence-monotonicity gap-detection in the SDK; Zod replay-shape schemas mirrored from `docs/architecture/contracts/api-payload-contracts.md §Plan-006 — Session Event Taxonomy`; client SDK `eventClient.ts` typed entry-points registered under Plan-007 namespace as `event.readAfterCursor` / `event.readWindow` / `event.subscribe` per CP-006-4; desktop `<CompactedStubSegment>` narrow-scope renderer (Plan-013 Non-Goal compliance); end-to-end integration tests; post-attach daemon public-key registration + resolution procedures over the new control-plane `daemon_signing_public_keys` table (T4.10 — CP-006-7 leg B per CP-003-5, the `NodeId`-keyed resolution surface external verifiers resolve against; added by the 2026-07-29 amendment, attachment-bound at round 5 — the register request presents the attach response's server-minted `attachmentId`).

The per-task `Provides` / `Depends` / `IdempotencyClass` / inline-cite block follows in §Tasks. Each task names its file path, exported symbols, intra-phase + cross-plan dependencies, idempotency classification per [Spec-015 §Idempotency Classes and Recovery Behavior](../specs/015-persistence-recovery-and-replay.md#idempotency-classes-and-recovery-behavior), and Spec-006 / ADR-018 cite anchors. The full audit-trail rationale for each ratification (drift class, options considered, hardened-mode recommendation) is surface-forwarded into this plan body + the [api-payload-contracts.md §Plan-006](../architecture/contracts/api-payload-contracts.md#plan-006--session-event-taxonomy) doc-mirror per AGENTS.md Surface-Forward-Then-Delete; transient working artifacts under `.agents/tmp/research/plan-readiness-audit/plan-006/` are gitignored and deleted post-merge.

- Contracts: See [API Payload Contracts](../architecture/contracts/api-payload-contracts.md) for typed schemas this plan consumes.

### Co-Tier Phase Ordering Precondition

Plan-005 and Plan-006 are co-tier (both Tier 4) in the cross-plan DAG. The Plan-006 Phase 1 contract surface **imports** types Plan-005 Phase 1 ships:

- `DriverCapabilityFlag` (literal union)
- `NormalizedProviderToolMetadata` (the post-`.default()` `z.output` of `ProviderToolMetadataSchema`; `CapabilityDetails.tools` imports the normalized shape because event payloads cross the persistence boundary)

Both are exported from `packages/contracts/src/provider-driver.ts` by Plan-005 Phase 1 (T1.1 – T1.4). Until that file lands on `develop`, Plan-006 Phase 1 T1.4 cannot compile. The ordering is therefore: **Plan-005 Phase 1 PR → Plan-006 Phase 1 PR**. PR authors MUST cite the Plan-005 Phase 1 merge commit in the Plan-006 Phase 1 PR description so the precondition is auditable. No reciprocal: Plan-005 Phase 1 has no Plan-006 import.

##### T1.8 — Normalized-kind disposition registry + exhaustiveness test

File: `packages/contracts/src/event.ts` (EXTEND — add the closed `NormalizedEventKind` union + `NORMALIZED_EVENT_KINDS` census tuple + `EVENT_DISPOSITION_BY_KIND` registry) + `packages/contracts/src/__tests__/event-disposition.test.ts` (NEW). Provides: `NormalizedEventKind` — a closed literal union of the 35 census kinds, named per the `EventCategory` / `SessionEventType` convention already in this file — plus its `NORMALIZED_EVENT_KINDS` const tuple; `EVENT_DISPOSITION_BY_KIND` keyed over `NormalizedEventKind` for **compile-time totality** (a `Record<NormalizedEventKind, EventKindDisposition>` source literal — a missing kind is a compile error, an extra key is too — from which the exported prototype-pollution-safe `ReadonlyMap<NormalizedEventKind, EventKindDisposition>` is built, matching `SESSION_EVENT_CATEGORY_BY_TYPE`'s `.get()`-safety), where `EventKindDisposition = { disposition: 'adopt' | 'rename' | 'correlate' | 'discard'; category?: EventCategory; eventType?: SessionEventType; typePending?: 'B18'; reason?: string }` — the machine-readable form of the §Event-Kind Disposition Table below over the 35 census kinds (the Plan-005 T3.5/T3.10 normalizers — the B10 bundle — consume it as the single source of disposition truth; the nine wire-level discards + the current-wire delta families in that table are the Plan-005 normalizer's wire layer, not registry keys). Plus a Vitest exhaustiveness suite asserting: registry keys set-equal `NORMALIZED_EVENT_KINDS` in **both directions** (every census kind present AND no extra key — the closed-union keying already makes a missing kind a compile error; the runtime check additionally catches a census-tuple/union drift), **never a bare `size === 35`**; every entry's `disposition` is one of the four values (`adopt` | `rename` | `correlate` | `discard`); every `adopt`/`rename` names a valid `EventCategory` (T1.1's 19-entry union) AND carries exactly one of `eventType` (a registered `SessionEventType` literal whose `SESSION_EVENT_CATEGORY_BY_TYPE` category equals the row's `category`) or `typePending: 'B18'`; the census-kind `typePending` set equals — by exact set-equality against those eight names — exactly { `init`, `turn_start`, `session_status`, `notification`, `api_retry`, `compact_boundary`, `model_rerouted`, `thread_renamed` }, the census kinds whose exact `SessionEventType` the 2026-07-22 B18 W3 Spec-006 census amendment minted (campaign Task 24 / B18's W3 leg, the designated Spec-006 census-amendment vehicle — the literals now exist in [Spec-006 §Event Type Summary](../specs/006-session-event-taxonomy-and-audit-log.md#event-type-summary); this task still builds the pinned pre-flip state, T1.10 flips it). The full B18-pending set is **nine**: these eight census kinds plus the `worker_shutting_down` delta-family orphan (the §Event-Kind Disposition Table class sweep found it adopts into `run_lifecycle` with no registered literal) — the ninth is pinned in that table's delta rows, not asserted here, because the delta families are wire-layer and not registry keys, so this machine test pins only the eight census-kind subset. This assertion is a **shrink-only ratchet**: a census amendment can only remove a kind from the set (as its literal lands the registry flips that kind from `typePending` to `eventType`), and because the test pins the set by value it fails loudly the moment the registry shrinks until registry and test are updated together — so a B18 landing that mints the literals but skips the registry update cannot close the pending set silently, and the pinned set keeps its existence loud. The closer is **T1.10** — authored into this plan 2026-07-22 by campaign Task 24 / B18 as the Phase 1 follow-on, dispatched after T1.8 and the merged B18 Spec-006 census amendment: it extends the `SessionEventType` union with the fifteen B18-minted literals closing all nine pending kinds (+ the user-message literal the row-31 `user_text` correlate targets), flips the pending registry entries from `typePending` to `eventType`, re-derives the hard-coded census totals (T1.2's 141 → 156), and shrinks this pending pin to empty. Every `correlate` and every `discard` carries a non-empty `reason` and no `category`, `eventType`, or `typePending`; no census kind is silently absent. The unknown residual — a wire kind outside the census — is not covered here; it is backstopped by the Plan-005 normalizer default-branch diagnostic (B10). Interim disposition of a `typePending` kind (a known census or delta kind whose literal has not yet landed) is that same structured default branch: until its `SessionEventType` is registered in contracts, the Plan-005 T3.5/T3.10 normalizer routes it to the B10 diagnostic rather than constructing an envelope against a missing type — no envelope, no silent drop — and it moves to `eventType` when T1.10 flips the registry (the literals themselves were minted by the 2026-07-22 B18 census amendment), with envelope construction additionally gated on the kind's `SessionEventSchema` payload variant per T1.10's flip-≠-emission rule. Depends: T1.1 (`EventCategory`), T1.2 (category registry). IdempotencyClass: N/A (type/data + test).

- **Spec coverage:** Spec-006 §Event Type Summary (the taxonomy the dispositions target, incl. the Total enumerated event types prose)
- **Verifies invariant:** none (normalized-kind → taxonomy disposition registry + its exhaustiveness test; asserts total adopt/rename/correlate/discard coverage under the no-silent-capability-loss default — no Phase-1 invariant names the normalized-kind disposition census; totality is a test-level assertion, not a named I-006 invariant)

##### T1.9 — Cross-cutting `sourceEpoch` + `sourcePosition` epoch-attribution carrier pair (CP-004-12 registration)

File: `packages/contracts/src/event.ts` (EXTEND — add the cross-cutting epoch-attribution carrier; registered 2026-07-20 as the campaign-B9 CP-004-12 follow-up) + `packages/contracts/src/__tests__/event-source-epoch.test.ts` (NEW). Provides: `SourceEpochSchema` — `z.number().int().nonnegative()` (epoch `0` is the pre-any-rollback epoch; execution-epoch semantics — advancing with each accepted `run.rolled_back` rewind — are owned by [Spec-004 §Required Behavior](../specs/004-queue-steer-pause-resume.md#required-behavior) + [Run State Machine §Invariants](../domain/run-state-machine.md#invariants), not redefined here) and its companion `SourcePositionSchema` — `z.number().int().nonnegative()`, the normalized-session-position (turn-boundary) vocabulary of Spec-004's `targetPosition`, registered because no run-scoped family's payload carries a native position key and the supersede cutoff (`turn > targetPosition`) cannot rank a late row against its epoch's surviving prefix without one (Codex round 2) — plus their inferred `SourceEpoch` / `SourcePosition` types, `SOURCE_EPOCH_PAYLOAD_KEY = 'sourceEpoch' as const` + `SOURCE_POSITION_PAYLOAD_KEY = 'sourcePosition' as const` (the registered payload-field names per Spec-006 §Event Type Enumeration), and the `withEpochStamp(payloadSchema)` composition helper that applies both optional keys and the pairing refinement (either stamp key present ⇒ both present and `runId` present — a stamp without run identity and position is unattributable). The stamp's value source is Plan-004 T3.11's per-event operation association — each provider-side operation recorded against the (epoch, turn) current when it opened (fallback: the closed delivery generation's retained pair, always-superseding by construction) per Spec-004's fence and generation-rotation semantics; T1.9 owns only the typed shape. T1.9 also pins `ORIGIN_POSITION_STUB_KEY = 'originPosition' as const` — the audit-stub projection key under which the T3.2 compactor preserves a current run-scoped row's resolved originating position and which the Plan-004 T3.12 rewind-span check reads back (Codex round 5; two plans' code share the literal, so the name is contract-pinned beside the payload-key consts). The carrier is a **payload field, not an envelope field**: it rides inside `payload`, so it sits in the RFC 8785 canonical bytes (signed, hash-chained, shred-safe like any payload field) while the envelope canonical field set stays fixed — I-006-1-03 is untouched and no version bump is taken — the registration precedes the ADR-018 point of no return ([ADR-018 §Reversibility Assessment](../decisions/018-cross-version-compatibility.md#reversibility-assessment)), which is an **emission event, not a code merge**: the project is pre-first-release with no production deployment, so no `"1.0"` envelope has been emitted in a non-test environment as of 2026-07-20 — shipped emitter code on `develop` (the Plan-003 runtime-node emitter's `RUNTIME_NODE_EVENT_VERSION`) does not cross it — making the pair part of the v1.0 baseline payload contract from first emit, the same baseline-membership path the campaign's earlier field registrations rode (`executionPosture`, the B1 terminal keys). **Conditional (Codex round 2):** if the point of no return is crossed before this task ships — any `"1.0"` emission in a non-test environment — the implementer instead takes the MINOR envelope bump (`"1.1"`) with producer and T2.3 golden-vector updates, per `ADR-018 §Decision` #8's new-optional-field rule. The registered key names are pinned by the exported consts because a later rename is forbidden-non-additive per the same `ADR-018 §Decision` #8. Stamping and consumption are Plan-004's: the T3.11 late-append leg stamps `sourceEpoch` at ingestion on pre-rollback-epoch **non-lifecycle** rows (the atomic closed-pair `driver_ask.requested` + `driver_ask.canceled` late-append included) — absence means current-epoch and the stamp is never fabricated at read time — and the T3.14 supersede projection reads it for cross-epoch keying; the audit-stub projection preserves the `sourceEpoch` + `sourcePosition` + `runId` triple at compaction, on accepted `run.rolled_back` boundary rows the `runId`/`runVersion`/`targetPosition` rewind cutoff (run-local epochs and the cutoff are unattributable without them), and on every run-scoped row the `runId` + resolved `originPosition` rewind-span detection keys (Codex round 5) — per Spec-006 §Compacted Event Format, with the compactor-side enumeration and tests in T3.2/T3.5 (Codex round 2). The carrier composes into the canonical read path, not just emitter-locals (Codex round 1): every **run-scoped** `SessionEventSchema` payload branch of the five late-append families (`assistant_output`, `tool_activity`, `usage_telemetry`, `artifact_publication`, the `interactive_request` closed pair — Spec-006 §Event Type Enumeration's scoping) present in `event.ts` at implementation time is wrapped with `withEpochStamp(...)`, and later registrants of those families through the `SessionEventSchema` union-registration seam (the CP-009-4 / CP-010-5 / CP-012-2 / CP-016-3 class) inherit the same admission requirement from the spec — strict payload schemas would otherwise reject a stamped row at subscription/replay validation. Admission is keyed on run-scopedness, not family membership alone (Codex round 2): a variant without run attribution never admits the stamp — the account-plane sentinel-bound `usage.rate_limit_update` (no `runId`) foremost — and the helper's pairing refinement rejects a stamped payload missing `sourcePosition` or `runId`; `run_lifecycle` branches never admit it (stragglers absorb, never append) — no envelope-schema edit. This Plan-004-facing provide is body-registered here (the T3.1 `withSessionAppendLock` publication precedent — the consumer-side obligation ledger is Plan-004's CP-004-12 row, and the dep-map §3 Plan-004 row names the carrier; no CP-006 row is added). The Vitest suite asserts: `SourceEpochSchema` and `SourcePositionSchema` each accept `0` and positive integers and reject negatives, non-integers, and non-numbers; `SOURCE_EPOCH_PAYLOAD_KEY` / `SOURCE_POSITION_PAYLOAD_KEY` equal the registered names `'sourceEpoch'` / `'sourcePosition'` exactly (pinning the wire names the Plan-004 legs and the Spec-006 registration share); the `withEpochStamp` pairing refinement rejects a payload carrying `sourceEpoch` without `sourcePosition`, `sourcePosition` without `sourceEpoch`, or the pair without `runId`; `ORIGIN_POSITION_STUB_KEY` equals the registered stub-projection name `'originPosition'` exactly; a fully-stamped event of each composed run-scoped family validates end-to-end through `SessionEventSchema`; the stamp keys are absent from every `run_lifecycle` branch present in the union (the absorb-never-append negative control); and the account-plane `usage.rate_limit_update` branch, present in the union, rejects them (the no-run-identity negative control). Depends: T1.3 (payload schemas the composition extends). IdempotencyClass: N/A (type).

- **Spec coverage:** Spec-006 §Event Type Enumeration (cross-cutting `sourceEpoch` payload field), Spec-006 §Compacted Event Format (audit-stub projection — `sourceEpoch` preservation)
- **Verifies invariant:** none (typed carrier registration; the stamping and consumption invariants are Plan-004's — I-004-14 late-event absorption, I-004-15 supersede marking — and the envelope canonical-set fixity stays I-006-1-03's)

#### Event-Kind Disposition Table (surveyed-runtime normalized census)

The provider drivers (Plan-005) normalize both provider wires into a normalized event vocabulary before the taxonomy maps them onto the 156 `SessionEventType` literals (141 at the T1.1–T1.2 baseline; 156 once T1.10 lands the 2026-07-22 B18 delta). This table is the **disposition contract** for that mapping: every normalized kind resolves to exactly one disposition — `adopt` (maps to an existing category/family), `rename` (maps under a different canonical name to resolve a wire collision), `correlate` (folds into an existing row via `correlation_id` — no category and no new persisted type), or `discard` (consumed transiently or dropped) — under a **no-silent-capability-loss default**: `adopt`/`rename` is the default, and every `correlate` or `discard` carries a stated reason, so a capability-bearing kind is never dropped silently. Input census: the 35 normalized kinds are enumerated in the campaign design's [§3.6 event-kind census](../superpowers/specs/2026-07-01-capability-enhancements-design.md) (self-contained by design — it reproduces all 35 in full, and this table mirrors it), and the current-wire delta families in its [§3.4 CLI-surface currency audit](../superpowers/specs/2026-07-01-capability-enhancements-design.md) (the C-4 / C-5 / C-6 currency corrections and the Class-B net-new families). The B19 provider-wire reference docs — [`docs/reference/provider-wire/README.md`](../reference/provider-wire/README.md), [`claude.md`](../reference/provider-wire/claude.md), [`codex.md`](../reference/provider-wire/codex.md) — each pinned by its own §Version pin table rather than by a number restated here, and carrying the per-wire evidence for each kind (they document the wire surfaces, not the census enumeration), pinning the primary provider sources a reviewer can follow: the [Claude Code CLI reference](https://code.claude.com/docs/en/cli-reference) and the full-history [`anthropics/claude-code` changelog](https://github.com/anthropics/claude-code) (`CHANGELOG.md`, version-anchored), both verified live 2026-07-02, and the Codex `app-server` JSON schema regenerated from the pinned binary via `codex app-server generate-json-schema`. Family-level dispositions are used for the delta rows — per-member normalization is Plan-005 T3.5/T3.10 driver detail. Totality over the **known** set is enforced by the T1.8 exhaustiveness test; the **unknown** residual is backstopped at runtime by the Plan-005 T3.5/T3.10 normalizer default-branch diagnostic (the B10 bundle), so an unrecognized wire kind surfaces a diagnostic rather than vanishing. The `rate_limits` collision resolution and the nine explicitly-discarded Claude system subtypes are the boundary inputs that travel with the census.

The T1.8 registry still builds the pinned `typePending: 'B18'` state first; T1.10 then flips each entry to its `eventType` and empties the pin — the shrink-only ratchet mechanics stay exactly as audited, with the two tasks landing as separate sequential commits.

| # | Normalized kind | Group | Disposition | Target category / reason |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| 1 | `init` | Inline timeline | adopt | `run_lifecycle` (run-start marker) — type `run.provider_initialized` (B18-minted; T1.10 flips); the daemon's `run.*` state transitions stay daemon-emitted, not provider-init-mapped — the forward marker records the provider's own init report |
| 2 | `text_delta` | Inline timeline | adopt | `assistant_output` (`assistant.message` stream) — type `assistant.message` |
| 3 | `tool_start` | Inline timeline | adopt | `tool_activity` (`tool.invoked`) — type `tool.invoked` |
| 4 | `tool_complete` | Inline timeline | adopt | `tool_activity` (tool-lifecycle completion) — type `tool.result` (failure outcome → `tool.error`) |
| 5 | `turn_start` | Inline timeline | adopt | `run_lifecycle` (turn boundary) — type `run.turn_started` (B18-minted; T1.10 flips) |
| 6 | `turn_complete` | Inline timeline | adopt | `run_lifecycle` (turn complete; `completionKind` turn-vs-task carve per the B1 taxonomy) — type `run.completed` |
| 7 | `approval_request` | Inline timeline | adopt | `interactive_request` (`driver_ask.requested`, permission ask) — type `driver_ask.requested` |

| 11 | `session_status` | Inline timeline | adopt | `session_lifecycle` (session status) — type `session.provider_status` (B18-minted; T1.10 flips): coarse provider status, with the B18-pinned no-fabricated-transition rule — provider status observations never drive the nine `session.*` state transitions (Spec-006 §Session Lifecycle) |
| 12 | `token_usage` | Inline timeline | adopt | `usage_telemetry` (`usage.token_count`) — type `usage.token_count` |

| 16 | `task_update` | Task mirror | adopt | `tool_activity` (per-task CRUD → task mirror) — type `tool.result` (task-tool result row) |

| 18 | `api_retry` | Transient retry | adopt | `usage_telemetry` (transient-retry record; Claude `system.api_retry` typed-error enum per C-5 — capability-bearing, never dropped) — type `usage.api_retry` (B18-minted; T1.10 flips) |
| 19 | `compact_boundary` | System, no timeline row | adopt | `usage_telemetry` (provider context-window compaction — distinct from the daemon `event.compacted` retention pass; feeds the advisory auto-compaction safeguard in [Spec-016 §Budget Policies](../specs/016-multi-agent-channels-and-orchestration.md#budget-policies)) — type `usage.context_compacted` (B18-minted; T1.10 flips) |

| 23 | `content_block_start` | System, no timeline row | discard | streaming-structural envelope boundary; the wrapped `text_delta` (row 2) carries the durable content — no separate timeline or persistence capability |
