| 25 | `background_task_terminal` | Background/subagent | adopt | `tool_activity` (`subagent.completed` — richer sibling completion; never replaces the tool-lifecycle completion row) — type `subagent.completed` |
| 26 | `background_task_notification` | Background/subagent | adopt | `tool_activity` (non-lifecycle task notice; may carry a durable output-file path) — type `tool.result` |
| 27 | `subagent_notification` | Background/subagent | adopt | `tool_activity` (Codex detached-child terminal injected into the parent's next turn) — type `subagent.completed` |
| 28 | `subagent_status` | Background/subagent | adopt | `tool_activity` (subagent-lifecycle row / internal child-thread status) — type `subagent.started` (fans to `subagent.completed`) |
| 29 | `codex_exec_result` | Codex process/terminal | adopt | `tool_activity` (raw exec-output signal — exited-during-wait vs yielded-with-resumable-session) — type `tool.result` |
| 30 | `terminal_interaction` | Codex process/terminal | adopt | `tool_activity` (stdin writes to a backgrounded PTY; non-empty stdin redacted from durable metadata) — type `tool.invoked` |
| 31 | `user_text` | Wire echo | correlate | correlation-only — folds into the originating app-sent user-message row via `correlation_id` (delivery confirmation of the pending send; no category and no new persisted type). **Target (B18-minted 2026-07-22):** the correlate target is `user.message` — the fourth `interactive_request` subfamily (Spec-006 §Queue and Intervention); contracts registration rides T1.10, the reason string names the literal. Interim, until that registration and its emitting row exist, the echo routes to the Plan-005 T3.5/T3.10 normalizer default-branch diagnostic (B10) — never a silent drop. Emission-side seam: the app-send path that records the user-message row is owned by the queue-submit surface owner (Plan-004/B9 — authored as its T2.9, campaign B18), not Plan-006 |
| 32 | `diff` | Heavy, persisted | adopt | `tool_activity` (payload persisted to SQLite; light meta to client) — type `tool.result` |
| 33 | `command_output` | Heavy, persisted | adopt | `tool_activity` (payload persisted; light meta to client) — type `tool.result` |
| 34 | `thinking` | Heavy, persisted | adopt | `assistant_output` (`assistant.thinking_update`; payload persisted) — type `assistant.thinking_update` |
| 35 | `proposed_plan` | Heavy, persisted | adopt | `assistant_output` (plan proposal; payload persisted) — type `assistant.message` |

Count: 14 + 3 + 1 + 6 + 4 + 2 + 1 + 4 = **35**.

**Explicitly-discarded boundary subtypes (nine, each reasoned).** These are Claude system-channel wire strings the surveyed parser discards; each is reasoned here under the no-silent-capability-loss default.

| Discarded subtype | Reason |
| --- | --- |
| `hook_started` | hook-lifecycle progress; hook execution is daemon-internal orchestration, not an audit-timeline capability |
| `hook_progress` | intra-hook progress; same daemon-internal-orchestration reason |
| `hook_response` | hook result consumed by the hook dispatcher, not a timeline capability |
| `notification` (Claude system-channel subtype) | distinct from the census `notification` kind (row 17, Codex-fed); the user-facing-notice capability is already carried there — this Claude system subtype is redundant transport noise |
| `files_persisted` | file-write summary; the adopted `diff` (32) / `command_output` (33) rows plus `artifact_publication` already carry the file-change capability |
| `tool_use_summary` | aggregate over the adopted `tool_start` (3) / `tool_complete` (4) rows; no new capability |
| `memory_recall` | provider-internal memory-retrieval signal; no audit-timeline capability |
| `local_command_output` | superseded by the adopted `command_output` (33) kind; the local variant carries no additional capability |
| `task_progress` | intra-task progress; the adopted `task_create` (15) / `task_update` (16) + `todo_update` snapshots carry the durable task state |

**Current-wire delta families (CLI-currency audit; family-level dispositions).** The `eventType` XOR `typePending` discipline (T1.8) applies to these rows too: a family adopting into a category with no registered literal for its kind is B18-pending. The mandatory class sweep of all eleven found exactly one such orphan — `worker_shutting_down` (adopts into `run_lifecycle`; its literal `run.worker_shutdown` was B18-minted 2026-07-22) — the ninth B18 target; every other family maps to a registered type, a registered-reserved family (`realtime_*`), an existing census kind's pending status (the `api_retry` enum shares row 18's), or `discard`.

| Delta family | Provider | Disposition | Target category / reason |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| result-subtype (`success` \| `error_max_turns` \| `error_max_budget_usd` \| `error_during_execution` \| `error_max_structured_output_retries`) | Claude | adopt | `run_lifecycle` terminal (`completionKind` / failure-subtype mapping per C-4; `result` only on `success`; driver reads to EOF) |
| `api_retry` typed-error enum (`authentication_failed` … `unknown` + `retry_delay_ms`) | Claude | adopt | `usage_telemetry` (enriches census row 18's `api_retry` kind — the same kind, not a distinct one; mid-run auth/billing/rate-limit now detectable per C-5; feeds the driver failure-mapping path) — type `usage.api_retry` with row 18 (B18-minted; T1.10 flips — the enum enriches the same kind, no distinct target) |
| `worker_shutting_down` | Claude | adopt | `run_lifecycle` diagnostic (mid-run worker-shutdown recovery signal — capability-bearing) — **type `run.worker_shutdown` (B18-minted 2026-07-22):** the ninth B18 target — a forward, non-state recovery diagnostic (Spec-006 §Run Lifecycle); wire-layer rather than a T1.8 registry key, so its closure is T1.10's union + category registration alone |
| plugin family | Claude | adopt | `tool_activity` (plugin invocations as tool-family rows) |
| hook family | Claude | discard | hook-lifecycle; daemon-internal orchestration, not an audit-timeline capability (consistent with the `hook_*` discards above) |
| goals (`thread/goal/*`) | Codex | adopt | `session_lifecycle` (the B1 session-goal events — `session.goal_updated` / `session.goal_cleared`) |
| guardian + `autoApprovalReview` | Codex | adopt | `approval_flow` observability rows (per C-6 — `approvalsReviewer` pinned `"user"`; guardian auto-adjudication is normalized as observability, never a Cedar-pipeline bypass) |
| `turn/diff/updated` \| `turn/plan/updated` \| `turn/moderationMetadata` | Codex | adopt | `diff` → persisted (32); `plan` → `proposed_plan` (35); `moderationMetadata` → `approval_flow` (`moderation.review_flagged`). **Wire names corrected 2026-08-28** from the truncated `turn/diff` / `turn/plan` this row carried at drafting — regenerating the protocol schema from the pinned binary (`codex app-server generate-json-schema` at codex-cli 0.150.1) emits the `/updated` suffix on both, and the bare forms appear nowhere in its 79-arm `ServerNotification` root; `turn/moderationMetadata` is genuinely bare and is unchanged. The dispositions are untouched — `diff` / `plan` here are census-kind names (rows 32 / 35), not wire names |
| `process/*` | Codex | adopt | `tool_activity` (the `codex_exec_result` (29) / `terminal_interaction` (30) family) |
| reasoning-delta | Codex | adopt | `assistant_output` (`assistant.thinking_update` reasoning stream) |
| realtime (`thread/realtime/*`) | Codex | adopt | `realtime_*` reserved family (registered reserved, no emitter until the upstream realtime flag stabilizes; gated per R8, B1) |

### Phase 2 — Crypto Protocol Core (`packages/runtime-daemon/src/events/`)

#### Tasks

##### T2.1 — RFC 8785 JCS canonicalizer

Files: `packages/runtime-daemon/src/events/canonicalizer.ts` (NEW) + `packages/contracts/src/event.ts` (EXTEND — `EVENT_ENVELOPE_SEQUENCE_MAX`). Provides: `canonicalizeEvent(envelope: EventEnvelope): CanonicalBytes` returning phantom-branded `Uint8Array` constructible only inside this module; `canonicalizeJson(value: unknown): CanonicalBytes` for Spec-024 `request_body_hash` reuse per CP-006-3; `normalizeOccurredAt(occurredAt: string): string`, the RFC 3339 UTC millisecond narrowing `canonicalizeEvent` applies to `occurredAt` before projecting — **exported** because the narrowing is many-to-one, so T3.1's `EventLogService.append` MUST persist exactly the normalized string that was signed rather than the producer's raw spelling (the `session_events.occurred_at` column is declared at that precision, and a raw spelling both violates that column contract and drops the row out of every lexical date-range scan while still verifying), and `canonicalizeEvent` hands back bytes rather than that string, so exporting the narrowing is the only way to give the append path the exact value without duplicating it; `isCanonicalOccurredAt(occurredAt: string): boolean`, the read-side companion to that narrowing — a pure, total, never-throwing predicate over the module's own `CANONICAL_OCCURRED_AT_PATTERN` testing whether a STORED `occurred_at` is the canonical spelling of the instant the signature commits to, **exported** because the narrowing being many-to-one is exactly what leaves the stored spelling outside the signature (T4.1 composes this with a green `verifyRow` to byte-bind the column and reports `occurred_at_not_canonical`; deliberately NOT wired into `canonicalizeEvent` or `verifyRow` here, since emitting a verdict from Phase 2 would be T2 code deciding a T4.1 question — I-006-2-10); in-package surface for now, `src/index.ts` not re-exporting this module; `EVENT_ENVELOPE_SEQUENCE_MAX` (`Number.MAX_SAFE_INTEGER`), the parse-boundary half of the sequence-injectivity ceiling, bound on `EventEnvelopeSchema.sequence` — its canonicalizer-side counterpart `assertRepresentableSequence` covers the direct-caller path the wire schema never sees, and the two surfaces are **deliberately un-shared** (importing the const would make them agree by construction, wrong value included; T2.3's `canonicalizer.golden.test.ts` asserts the agreement instead, so either side drifting fails a test). The module's **refusal boundary is part of what it provides** — CP-006-3 binds Plan-027 to it by identifier. The generic entry point carries **three** refusals of which exactly **two** are CP-006-3-bound, and the two counts must never be conflated: the bound pair is `assertWithinCanonicalDepth` (the `CANONICAL_JSON_MAX_DEPTH` policy ceiling) and `assertWellFormedStrings` (the RFC 8785 §3.2.2.2 MUST-terminate refusal over unpaired UTF-16 surrogates); the third is `assertNoToJsonOverride`, which refuses any value carrying a callable `toJSON` at any depth and binds Plan-027 to nothing, because `JSON.parse` output — the only thing that reaches `canonicalizeJson` on the CP-006-3 path — cannot carry a function-valued member, making its predicate unsatisfiable there. Its KIND is a third one besides POLICY and RFC-MUST: it enforces the DETERMINISM the integrity protocol assumes, since a `toJSON` re-invoked at verification time need not return what it returned at signing time. `assertRepresentableSequence` is reachable only through `canonicalizeEvent`, an asymmetry RFC 8785 conformance forces in both directions rather than a placement choice; I-006-2-09 names the closed set, that asymmetry, and the observable order on each entry point. Depends: `EventEnvelope` + `EventEnvelopeVersion` (T1.3); pinned JCS library (`canonicalize@3.0.0` — Erdtman's RFC 8785 reference implementation, Apache-2.0, zero-deps, bundled TS types; **exact-pinned**, not a caret range: the canonical bytes feed every event `row_hash` + `daemon_signature`, so the canonicalizer version is locked and the T2.3 golden-vector suite binds its output to RFC 8785 Appendix B Table 1 — a silent minor bump must never change canonical bytes). IdempotencyClass: `idempotent`.

- **Spec coverage:** Spec-006 §Canonical Serialization Rules (RFC 8785; Fields included — the canonical set; occurredAt; pii_ciphertext_digest)
- **Verifies invariant:** I-006-2-09 (the refusal set is closed over the serialized tree and the refusal order is observable — `normalizeOccurredAt`'s four guards, `assertRepresentableSequence`, the `CANONICAL_JSON_MAX_DEPTH` ceiling, `assertNoToJsonOverride`, `assertWellFormedStrings`, and the no-JSON-representation guard, split across the two entry points as RFC 8785 conformance forces; this task's `canonicalizer.ts` is the enforcement site and T2.3's `canonicalizer.golden.test.ts` the pinning suite — byte-stability is still verified by T2.3 under I-006-2-03 and single-use by signRow under I-006-2-06, which name those tasks not this one), I-006-2-10 (this task is the PROVIDER: it exports `isCanonicalOccurredAt`, the read-side check that binds a stored `occurred_at` to the spelling the signature committed to, and deliberately emits no verdict — T4.1 is the consumer that reports `occurred_at_not_canonical`)

##### T2.2 — BLAKE3 hash-chain + Ed25519 signer

File: `packages/runtime-daemon/src/events/signer.ts` (NEW). Provides: `signRow(canonical: CanonicalBytes, prevHash: Uint8Array, signingKey: Ed25519PrivateKey): SignedRow`; `verifyRow(...)` for read-side — fail-closed on the 64-zero-byte `daemon_signature` placeholder, returning `failureMode: 'signature_placeholder'` before any Ed25519 verification is attempted and never "repairing" the row by signing it with a current key (`Spec-006 §Integrity Protocol`, 2026-07-26 amendment; this check is the replacement for the deleted T2.6); `GENESIS_PREV_HASH` (32 zero bytes). Depends: T2.1; `@noble/hashes/blake3.js` + `@noble/curves/ed25519.js` (already pinned in crypto-paseto); T2.7 daemon signing-key source. IdempotencyClass: `idempotent` (RFC 8032 Ed25519 determinism).

- **Spec coverage:** Spec-006 §Integrity Protocol (chained to its predecessor; placeholder signatures are refused, never retro-signed), Spec-006 §Canonical Serialization Rules (row_hash verification)
- **Verifies invariant:** I-006-2-06 (one canonicalization per row — signRow takes CanonicalBytes once for both BLAKE3 input and Ed25519 message)

##### T2.3 — Golden-vector tests for canonicalizer + signer

Files: `packages/runtime-daemon/src/events/__tests__/canonicalizer.golden.test.ts` (NEW) + `packages/runtime-daemon/src/events/__tests__/signer.golden.test.ts` (NEW). Provides: two Vitest suites. The canonicalizer suite asserts byte-stable output across field-order drift, null-vs-undefined, clock-tick boundaries, nested-payload lex-sort, numeric edge cases per ECMA-262 ToString, `version: "1.0"` literal, the canonical eleven-member projection derived set-equal against `EventEnvelopeSchema` rather than transcribed (with `actor` as its sole null-admitting member), and all three published RFC 8785 vector sets — the §3.2.2 sample document's §3.2.4 bytes, the §3.2.3 property-sorting order carrying a code-point-sort negative control, and Appendix B Table 1's 26 number-serialization rows. It also pins T2.1's refusal boundary — every family the module raises itself, plus the precedence between them (I-006-2-09): `occurredAt` normalization's four guards, their sub-millisecond-before-calendar precedence, and the canonical form's fixed-point idempotence, the sequence safe-integer ceiling with its collapsed-pair demonstration and the drift guard holding the deliberately-un-shared `EVENT_ENVELOPE_SEQUENCE_MAX` to its canonicalizer-side counterpart, the `CANONICAL_JSON_MAX_DEPTH` ceiling with its scalar-versus-container discriminating pair at the boundary and its cycle-as-depth-exhaustion case, the callable-`toJSON` refusal across the prototype chain as well as own properties (an inherited `toJSON` is as non-deterministic as an own one, so the guard tests `typeof node.toJSON === "function"` rather than `Object.hasOwn`) with a depth-only locator that never names the carrying property, the RFC 8785 §3.2.2.2 lone-surrogate refusal across string values and property names alike (a valid-surrogate-pair positive control, a code-unit-and-index locator that never quotes the offending string — the guard runs over T2.4's PII plaintext and its message reaches logs — and a clean-parse reachability proof that `EventEnvelopeSchema` admits what the canonicalizer refuses), the no-JSON-representation guard, and the three refusals originating inside `canonicalize@3.0.0`, inventoried rather than owned (the cycle case now SHADOWED in both shapes that could reach it — an own-property cycle is reported as depth exhaustion first, and a cycle closed only through a `toJSON` result is refused by `assertNoToJsonOverride` before the serializer runs — leaving it reachable through the non-idempotent-accessor residual alone) — alongside both entry points' observable orders, depth → `toJSON` → well-formedness (the first step is a correctness constraint, not a diagnostic preference: the depth walk is what makes the two later walks terminate on cyclic input) and sequence ahead of `occurredAt` and of the inherited guards alike. The signer suite pins T2.2's commitments — `row_hash = BLAKE3(prev_hash || canonical_bytes)` in that order, genesis seeding from 32 zero bytes, single-canonicalization (the same `CanonicalBytes` feed BLAKE3 and Ed25519), ZIP-215 strict verification (`{ zip215: false }` — the forgery classes the flag exists to refuse), the observable four-stage check order with placeholder-before-chain, the `signature_placeholder` fail-closed verdict, the throw-vs-verdict error-channel split, and daemon/participant key non-cross-validation — behaviour no other Phase 2 task unit-tests (T2.5 is scoped to T2.4's codec). Inline hex fixtures (matches crypto-paseto convention). Depends: T2.1, T2.2. IdempotencyClass: N/A (test).

- **Spec coverage:** Spec-006 §Canonical Serialization Rules (same canonical form; RFC 8785; Fields included — the canonical set; present-but-null; occurredAt), Spec-006 §Integrity Protocol (chained to its predecessor; signed by the emitting daemon with Ed25519 over the same canonical byte string)
- **Verifies invariant:** I-006-1-03 (envelope field set fixed; RFC 8785 lex-sort serialization), I-006-2-03 (canonical bytes byte-stable across implementations), I-006-2-04 (genesis prev_hash = 32 zero bytes; prev_hash[n] = row_hash[n-1] — the signer suite pins the genesis seed and the BLAKE3(prev_hash || canonical_bytes) linkage that produces it), I-006-2-06 (one canonicalization per row — the signer suite asserts the same CanonicalBytes feed BLAKE3 and Ed25519, daemon and participant signatures alike), I-006-2-08 (observable four-stage check order with placeholder-before-chain, ZIP-215 strictness, daemon/participant non-cross-validation, throw-vs-verdict error channel, signature_placeholder fail-closed verdict — the five signer-suite properties), I-006-2-09 (the canonicalizer's six self-raised refusal families, scoped to the serialized tree, and the observable order on each entry point — the canonicalizer-suite counterpart to the signer-suite properties I-006-2-08 pins)

##### T2.4 — PII indirection codec (sole write path)

File: `packages/runtime-daemon/src/events/pii-indirection.ts` (NEW). Provides: `writeEventWithPii(input: RawEventInput, db, encryptor, signingKey): Promise<SignedRow>` as the one-and-only path writing non-null `pii_payload`; branded `PiiPayloadCiphertext` constructible only here; `PiiEncryptor` interface (implementation owned by Plan-022 per CP-006-1); **`isCiphertextDigestBound(storedPiiPayload: unknown, signedPayload: unknown): boolean`** — the read-side counterpart to the digest this codec mints, exported for T4.1 and sited immediately beside `embedCiphertextDigest` so the mint and the check cannot drift apart again (I-006-2-11). Pure, total, and never-throwing for the range-walk reason T2.1's `isCanonicalOccurredAt` states; both parameters are `unknown` because both arrive from SQLite, where a `BLOB`-affinity read can hand back a `string` the hash primitive would throw on. It emits no verdict — deciding `pii_ciphertext_digest_unbound` is T4.1's call, the same provider/consumer split T2.1 takes. **`PII_PARTICIPANT_ID_PAYLOAD_KEY` (`"pii_participant_id"`) + `isPiiOwnerStampBound(storedPiiParticipantId: unknown, signedPayload: unknown): boolean`** — the codec projects the PII owner's participant id into `payload` under that key on every row it writes, so the OWNER STAMP lands inside the canonical bytes and `daemon_signature` commits to whose key decrypts the ciphertext rather than to the ciphertext alone; the predicate is the read-side counterpart, comparing the `session_events.pii_participant_id` column against the signed stamp (I-006-2-12). Pure, total, never-throwing and fail-closed for the range-walk reason above, both parameters `unknown` for the same SQLite-affinity reason, and it decides FOUR states: both absent passes (a no-PII row), both present compares, and either one alone is unbound. Like its sibling it emits no verdict — `pii_owner_stamp_unbound` is T4.1's call — and is deliberately NOT wired into `canonicalizeEvent` or `verifyRow`. Depends: T2.1, T2.2; `splitPii()` shape from Plan-022 working copy (interface only; runtime injection at composition root). IdempotencyClass: `manual_reconcile_only` per AES-256-GCM random-nonce non-replay-safety (NIST SP 800-38D §8.2).

- **Spec coverage:** Spec-006 §Canonical Serialization Rules (pii_ciphertext_digest)
- **Verifies invariant:** I-006-2-01 (encrypt→digest→embed→canonicalize→sign order is load-bearing — the per-stage phantom brands this codec emits), I-006-2-02 (sole write path for pii_payload), I-006-2-07 (`RawEventInput` discriminated union types `pii_payload: never` for `audit_integrity` / `event_maintenance` — the compile-time counterpart to I-006-3-01's runtime refusal), I-006-3-01 (layer 2 — pii-indirection.ts refuses audit_integrity / event_maintenance), I-006-2-11 (the minted `pii_ciphertext_digest` is checkable — `isCiphertextDigestBound` is the provider half; T4.1 is the registered consumer), I-006-2-12 (the PII owner stamp is projected into the canonical bytes and is checkable — this codec is the PROVIDER: it mints `pii_participant_id` inside the signed payload and exports `isPiiOwnerStampBound`, emitting no verdict; T4.1 is the consumer that reports `pii_owner_stamp_unbound`)

##### T2.5 — Post-shred signature-verification property test

File: `packages/runtime-daemon/src/events/__tests__/post-shred-verify.test.ts` (NEW). Provides: Vitest property suite asserting `writeEventWithPii(...)` produces canonical bytes including `pii_ciphertext_digest`; with `pii_payload` cleared to `NULL` at rest, `daemon_signature` still verifies; tampering any envelope field post-clear → verification fails. The suite clears the column outright, which is the strictly STRONGER exclusion control and matches what `Spec-006 §Compacted Event Format` does — it is deliberately not labelled Path 1, because `Spec-022 §Shred Fan-Out` Path 1 destroys the participant's key and overwrites no column at all (CP-006-12), so a shredded row retains its ciphertext and its I-006-2-11 digest check passes unchanged. Depends: T2.4. IdempotencyClass: N/A (test).

- **Spec coverage:** Spec-006 §Canonical Serialization Rules (pii_ciphertext_digest)
- **Verifies invariant:** I-006-2-01 (encrypt→digest→embed→canonicalize→sign order; post-shred signature still verifies), I-006-2-05 (signed bytes commit to pii_ciphertext_digest, not raw ciphertext)

##### T2.7 — DaemonSigningKeySource interface + OsKeystore-sealed implementation + `daemon_signing_keys` SQLite table

Files: `packages/runtime-daemon/src/events/signing-key-source.ts` (NEW) + `packages/runtime-daemon/src/migrations/0005-daemon-signing-keys.ts` (NEW) + `packages/runtime-daemon/src/events/__tests__/signing-key-source.test.ts` (NEW — custody suite: PUBLIC-key-only `create`, signer-local `read`, seal/unseal round-trip, keystore-refusal fail-closed) + `packages/runtime-daemon/src/session/__tests__/migration-shape.test.ts` (EXTEND — the version-5 `daemon_signing_keys` shape block: column set, single-column PK, NOT NULL discipline, autoindex-only index list, and the version-5 `schema_version` row description). Provides: `DaemonSigningKeySource` interface (`create(sessionId): Promise<{publicKey: Ed25519PublicKey}>` — returns the PUBLIC key ONLY so daemon-private signing material never crosses the provisioning boundary (CP-006-7's call-sites); the freshly generated private key is sealed in-place and is reachable solely through the signer-local `read(sessionId): Promise<Ed25519PrivateKey>` path); `DaemonSigningKeySealer` (`seal(sessionId, privateKey)` / `unseal(sessionId, sealedPrivateKey)`, both async because an unseal after Spec-022's idle wipe can await a WebAuthn ceremony) as the INJECTED seal boundary; `OsKeystoreSealedDaemonSigningKeySource` implementation generating a fresh per-session Ed25519 keypair and sealing the private key THROUGH that injected boundary rather than performing the seal itself — the OS-keystore implementation and the `sealed_private_key` byte format are both Plan-022 Tier 5 deliverables per CP-006-11, which carries the `@napi-rs/keyring` v1.2.0 custody ladder and the `keytar` supersede; the binding is deliberately kept out of this module's import graph so no native dependency reaches the append path's consumers, **storing ciphertext in the new local `daemon_signing_keys` SQLite table (`session_id` TEXT PK, `public_key` BLOB NOT NULL, `sealed_private_key` BLOB NOT NULL, `created_at` TEXT NOT NULL, `rotated_at` TEXT nullable)** per ADR-004 SQLite-local-state boundary — daemon-private secrets are per-machine and MUST NOT live in shared-Postgres `sessions` (corrected post-Codex T4 review, 2026-05-28 — provenance in the Progress Log; the canonical schema doc-mirror at [local-sqlite-schema.md §Audit Log Crypto Tables](../architecture/schemas/local-sqlite-schema.md#audit-log-crypto-tables-plan-006) is the source of truth); new additive migration `0005-daemon-signing-keys.ts` creates the table at schema version 5. Depends: T2.1, T2.2 (consumer); the production provisioning call-site + post-attach roster public-key registration ride CP-006-7's two legs (leg A — composition-root call-site per the CP-006-1 assertion pattern; leg B — T4.10 post-attach registration write per CP-003-5; re-vehicled 2026-07-29, formerly attributed to a Plan-002 session-create amendment). IdempotencyClass: `manual_reconcile_only` per per-session key generation non-replay-safety.

- **Spec coverage:** Spec-022 §Daemon Master Key (the tier-1 @napi-rs/keyring v1.2.0 custody rung — declared here as the injected seal boundary, implemented by Plan-022 Tier 5 per CP-006-11), ADR-004 §Decision (SQLite-local-state boundary)
- **Verifies invariant:** none (key-custody / keystore-sealing task; daemon signing-key production + OS-keystore sealing are governed by Spec-022 §Daemon Master Key and ADR-004, outside Plan-006 §Invariants)

#### Tasks

##### T3.1 — Append-path service writing integrity columns + Plan-022 Path 1 shred callback

Files: `packages/runtime-daemon/src/events/event-log-service.ts` (NEW); `packages/runtime-daemon/src/events/ingest-halt-source.ts` (NEW Plan-006-owned — the I-006-4-03 append-gate seam declaration; see the ingest-halt clause below); `packages/runtime-daemon/src/events/session-append-lock.ts` (NEW Plan-006-owned — the dependency-leaf home of `withSessionAppendLock`; see the publication clause below); `packages/runtime-daemon/src/migrations/0006-run-lifecycle-terminal-backstop-index.ts` (NEW — the terminal-backstop index + terminal-key trigger trio this task body already specifies; file assigned 2026-08-03 by the Phase-3 migration file-assignment true-up, the F-006-HALT-06 dependency-accuracy class — no status implication; shipped as `0006` 2026-08-04, PR #287); `packages/runtime-daemon/src/migrations/0007-pii-participant-id.ts` (NEW — the durable PII owner-stamp column this task body already specifies); `packages/runtime-daemon/src/session/migration-runner.ts` (EXTEND — the runner's prescribed same-commit registration step for BOTH migrations, mirroring the T4.10 clause at §Target Areas: one further `hasMigrationApplied`-guarded block in `applyMigrations` per migration file, landed in the same commit as that file, since an orphan migration file leaves its schema absent). Also EXTENDs `packages/contracts/src/event.ts` — the exported `DAEMON_SCOPE_SENTINEL_SESSION_ID` constant (the RFC 9562 Max UUID the registry's write path refuses and the gate's carve-out reads; the shipped contracts test re-imports it instead of re-declaring the literal — F-006-HALT-08, 2026-08-02 delta) — with `packages/contracts/src/__tests__/session-event.test.ts` (EXTEND — Plan-006-owned) named in this task's file set so the replacement is an assigned edit rather than an inferred one: the daemon-scope arm's local `DAEMON_SCOPE_SENTINEL` literal is deleted in favour of the production import, which is what makes the export single-source (Codex PR #282 round 2) — and `packages/contracts/src/error.ts` — the `DAEMON_INGEST_HALTED_CODE` and `DAEMON_PII_SPLIT_BYPASS_CODE` typed-code constants (dotted `daemon.ingest_halted`, 409, and `daemon.pii_split_bypass`, 400 — both T3.1 gate refusals exported under error.ts's `*_CODE` convention so JSON-RPC producers and consumers never duplicate a bare string, the bypass constant added at Codex PR #282 round 1 — plus `DaemonIngestHaltedDetails` + `DaemonIngestHaltedDetailsSchema`, the strict `{ sessionId: SessionId }` detail carrier the refusal throws and `mapJsonRpcError` renders as `data.fields` (Codex PR #282 round 3), and its sibling `DaemonPiiSplitBypassDetails` + `DaemonPiiSplitBypassDetailsSchema`, strict `{ fieldPath: string }` — the offending PII-tagged field's payload path, minted in the same pass so the two refusals this section registers do not ship one typed and one bare under a rationale that covers both. The shape comes from THIS task's refusal contract (an `append()` whose `payload` carries a PII-tagged field with no `pii_ciphertext_digest`), never from the `daemon.pii_split_ambiguous` payload column — that column types the taxonomy event, a different thing. `fieldPath` is a KEY path and never a value, so the detail cannot carry the PII the refusal exists to protect, and the caller already holds the envelope it submitted: the field path is the one thing it lacks; mirror rows in [error-contracts.md §Daemon](../architecture/contracts/error-contracts.md#daemon) — F-006-HALT-03). Provides: `EventLogService.append(envelope, options): Promise<{id, sequence, rowHash}>` as the sole append path under per-session mutex; `registerShredCallback(handler: ShredCallback): void` invoked by Plan-022's Path 1 orchestrator after `participant_keys` DELETE commits — the `event.shredded` envelope it appends `.parse()`s through T1.11's exported `EventShreddedPayloadSchema` at the emission seam (the emitter-parses convention); refuses any `append()` whose `payload` carries a PII-tagged field without `pii_ciphertext_digest` (surfaces `daemon.pii_split_bypass` typed error). **Declares the ingest-halt admission gate — the I-006-4-03 append-path enforcement seam (landed by the 2026-08-02 follow-up amendment PR #281, Codex PR #278 round 3's routed mechanism, sequenced before Phase 3 dispatch so this task's implementer builds against the declared seam rather than retrofitting it):** `IngestHaltSource` in `ingest-halt-source.ts` — `isHalted(sessionId: SessionId): boolean`, a synchronous read over implementation-held in-memory state (no I/O and no lock acquisition on the read path, so consulting it under `withSessionAppendLock` adds no edge to the lock order — the `halt`/`clear` write surface acquires only that same per-session append lock, a second user of an existing lock rather than a new one, so no new lock-order edge arises there either; T4.2's implementation derives its halted set from sweeps over the durable key stores and never reads those stores on the append path) — constructor-injected into `EventLogService` with the vacuous default class `NeverHaltedIngestHaltSource` (every session admissible) standing until T4.2's production wiring. The file also declares the production halt-state carrier `IngestHaltRegistry`: implements `IngestHaltSource` over its own in-memory session-keyed set, exposes the `halt(sessionId)` / `clear(sessionId)` write surface, takes zero constructor dependencies, and refuses the node-scope sentinel session on the write path (fail-loud throw) — the sentinel carve-out as a mechanical property of the sole production implementation. The redundant paths are pinned (F-006-HALT-07, 2026-08-02 delta): `halt()` on an already-halted session and `clear()` on a never-halted session are silent no-ops (distinct from the sentinel case, which throws), and `halt()` decides its no-op on a membership check BEFORE lock acquisition — T4.2's coalesced rerun re-issues `halt()` on every sweep while a collision persists, and a no-op that still paid lock acquisition would serialize the sweep behind an append parked in T2.7's human-gated unseal for no state change — while `clear()` decides AFTER acquisition, because an un-halt must order against in-flight appends. **Halt publication serializes with in-flight appends (PR #281 Codex round 2):** `halt(sessionId)` and `clear(sessionId)` publish their set mutation inside `withSessionAppendLock(sessionId, …)` — the same helper `append()` runs under, imported from the dependency-leaf `session-append-lock.ts` rather than constructor-injected, so the zero-constructor-dependency property and the cycle-freedom both survive (home pinned at PR #281 Codex round 3: `event-log-service.ts` value-imports this file's `NeverHaltedIngestHaltSource` as its constructor default, so a lock helper published from `event-log-service.ts` would make this import close an eager ESM value cycle — the class the T1.12 `event-core.ts` dependency-leaf hoist ruled on; the leaf imports neither sibling). The lock hold is what the lock-free read alone cannot give: an append that already passed the gate consult can be parked mid-critical-section in T2.7's asynchronous key unseal when the observer detects a collision, and an unserialized `halt()` would return while that admitted row still commits behind it; under the lock, publication waits for the in-flight append to release, so every append strictly precedes or strictly follows a halt — after `halt(sessionId)` returns, no admitted-but-uncommitted append remains for that session. The sentinel-session refusal throws before any lock acquisition. The registry is the observer↔service construction-cycle breaker (PR #281 Codex round 1, P1): T4.2's `KeyReuseObserver` appends `key_reuse_detected` through `EventLogService` (the sole append path) while also supplying its halt state, so neither can constructor-inject the other — the composition root instead constructs registry → `EventLogService` (reading the registry as its `IngestHaltSource`) → observer (populating the registry from its sweeps, appending through the service), and the runtime assertion checks identity (the service's halt source IS the registry instance the started observer writes), per the T3.2 `RollbackAttributionSource` declare-vacuous-then-implement shape and the CP-006-1 assertion pattern. `append()` consults the source FIRST under the lock, before canonicalization or any other admission work: a halted `sessionId` is refused with the typed `daemon.ingest_halted` error — a hard write-path rejection like `daemon.pii_split_bypass`, not a taxonomy event — fail-closed while the collision persists in the stores per I-006-4-03's halt rule. **The refusal's wire detail shape is pinned (Codex PR #282 round 3):** the thrown error carries typed details `{ sessionId }` validated by `DaemonIngestHaltedDetailsSchema` (strict, `SessionIdSchema`-typed — authored in `error.ts` on the `ResourceLimitExceededDetailsSchema` precedent), which `mapJsonRpcError` surfaces as `data.fields.sessionId` beside `data.type = "daemon.ingest_halted"`; T3.5 asserts the MAPPED envelope, not merely that the typed code was thrown, so a `detail`-less throw fails the arm. This is a deliberate divergence from the code+message-only convention `error.ts` records for the `runtimenode.*` 409s — that convention exists because a CONFLICTING session's id would leak a third party's identity, whereas the halted id is the caller's own append target, already in its hand. The sentinel carve-out lives in the source's contract rather than in gate special-casing: `key_reuse_detected` binds to the node-scope sentinel session per `Spec-006 §Daemon-Scope Event Binding And Node-Scope Anchoring`, and no `IngestHaltSource` implementation ever admits the sentinel session into its halted set, so the alarm append can never self-deadlock. **Publishes `withSessionAppendLock<T>(sessionId: SessionId, critical: () => Promise<T>): Promise<T>` from the dependency-leaf `session-append-lock.ts`** (module-singleton lock state, so every importer — `append()`, the registry's `halt`/`clear`, Plan-004's emitter — serializes on the same per-session lock; the leaf imports neither sibling, PR #281 Codex round 3) — the per-session append lock as a wrappable, owner-scoped-reentrant primitive: `append()` runs its own write under it, and Plan-004's B9 T3.7 terminal emitter wraps its guard-swap-append in it so the `(runId, runVersion)` terminal check-and-append is atomic against concurrent appends. Reentrancy is owner-scoped via an `AsyncLocalStorage` (`node:async_hooks`) context carrying the held-lock token: an `append()` issued inside a `withSessionAppendLock` critical section runs in that same async context, reads the token, and reuses the hold rather than re-acquiring (so the emitter's wrap never self-deadlocks); ANY caller outside that context — including same-session concurrent work on a different async chain — sees no token and blocks until release. **Release-on-settle is unconditional (F-006-HALT-01, the 2026-08-02 T3.1-seam targeted readiness-audit delta):** the hold releases in a `finally` owned by the frame that ACQUIRED it: an acquiring `critical()` that REJECTS releases the hold and propagates the rejection unchanged, with the `AsyncLocalStorage` held-lock token torn down on the same settle path so a rejected acquisition can never leave a stale token visible to a later same-context call; a REENTRANT frame that rejects propagates the rejection unchanged WITHOUT releasing the hold or touching the token (Codex PR #282 round 1) — only the acquiring frame settles the physical hold, so an outer owner that CATCHES an inner rejection and continues still holds the lock, its next nested call is still recognized as reentrant, and the hold releases exactly once when the owner frame settles — load-bearing because the gate refusal below throws `daemon.ingest_halted` INSIDE the critical section while `clear(sessionId)` acquires this same hold, so a leak-on-rejection would wedge the session unrecoverably (the module-singleton state makes the wedge process-lifetime, and the un-halt path itself deadlocks). The module also publishes a test-only reset export (`__resetSessionAppendLocksForTest()`, test-only by name and JSDoc — F-006-HALT-09): the lock state is process-global by design, so T3.5's parked-append fixtures reset module state in `beforeEach` rather than leaking held locks across arms. The four lock properties are T3.5-tested: nested-call progress (an inner `append()` completes under the outer hold), concurrent same-session blocking (two parallel `withSessionAppendLock` calls on one session serialize — the second waits for the first to release), cross-session non-blocking (locks on distinct sessions run concurrently), and owner-scoped rejection-release (a rejecting acquiring `critical()` releases the hold — a subsequent same-session acquisition proceeds — while an inner REENTRANT rejection caught by the outer owner releases nothing: the arm asserts the outer's next nested call still runs reentrantly and the hold releases exactly once on the owner's settle; Codex PR #282 round 1). New additive migration `0006-run-lifecycle-terminal-backstop-index.ts` adds a partial UNIQUE index `idx_session_events_run_terminal_once` on `session_events (json_extract(payload, '$.runId'), json_extract(payload, '$.runVersion')) WHERE category = 'run_lifecycle' AND type IN ('run.completed', 'run.failed', 'run.interrupted')` — the schema-level backstop for the terminal exactly-once property: a second terminal row for the same `(runId, runVersion)` epoch raises a SQLite UNIQUE-constraint error (fail-loud, never a silent append-if-absent API). The index is scoped to the three terminal `run_lifecycle` types — the `completed` / `interrupted` / `failed` terminal run states — so non-terminal `run_lifecycle` rows and every other category insert freely, and NULL `runId` / `runVersion` rows bypass the index (SQLite treats NULLs as distinct); the non-null-key precondition is the Plan-004 B9 T3.7 emitter's leg, and this index is the defense-in-depth backstop behind that guard, not a replacement for it. The same migration installs the projection-level terminal-key CHECK that Spec-006's at-most-once-terminal-emission rule (B1) assigns to this B11 schema work — since SQLite cannot `ALTER TABLE … ADD CHECK` on the Plan-001-created `session_events` table ([sqlite.org/`lang_altertable`.html](https://sqlite.org/`lang_altertable`.html) omitted features), the idiomatic equivalent is a trigger trio, `trg_run_terminal_key_insert` (`BEFORE INSERT`), `trg_run_terminal_key_update` (`BEFORE UPDATE OF payload, category, type`), and `trg_run_terminal_key_promote` (`BEFORE UPDATE OF category, type` — rejects re-typing a non-terminal row INTO the terminal set outright; terminal rows are INSERT-only, so a null-keyed promotion cannot slip the OLD-keyed update leg and the NULL-distinct index), with full DDL in [local-sqlite-schema.md](../architecture/schemas/local-sqlite-schema.md). The INSERT leg, scoped `WHEN NEW.category = 'run_lifecycle' AND NEW.type IN ('run.completed', 'run.failed', 'run.interrupted')`, aborts (`SELECT RAISE(ABORT, …)`) when the terminal key is NULL **or the wrong SQLite storage class** — `json_type` must be `'text'` for `runId` (a `RunId` string) and `'integer'` for `runVersion` (the any-run-progression counter per `Spec-006 §Run Lifecycle (run_lifecycle)`) — so a type-drifted `"7"`-vs-`7` key that would slip past the storage-class-keyed UNIQUE index is caught; this closes the NULL-key bypass the UNIQUE index cannot catch (NULLs are distinct) and fails loud on exactly the malformed terminal rows the backstop exists to guard. The **`UPDATE` leg is load-bearing** and is keyed off `OLD` (a row that WAS terminal cannot escape by mutation): beyond the same NULL/type checks it also aborts a value-changing key rewrite (`json_extract(NEW…)` `IS NOT` `json_extract(OLD…)`, null-safe — a compactor bug rewriting `(R,7)` to another non-null pair would otherwise free the index slot for a duplicate terminal) and a `category`/`type` de-scope (`NEW.category IS NOT OLD.category OR NEW.type IS NOT OLD.type` — flipping a terminal row out of the guarded set is the same escape), so it enforces the T3.2 stub-preservation rule (`runId` / `runVersion` / `category` / `type` kept for the row's whole retention life) against the compactor itself. A promotion regression test accompanies the trio: an `UPDATE` re-typing a non-terminal row to `('run_lifecycle', 'run.completed')` with NULL keys must abort on the promote leg, never silently satisfy the NULL-distinct index. **Two normalization obligations land on this append path, discharged before canonicalization and both inherited from T2.1:** (1) `append()` MUST persist the **normalized** `occurredAt` — the string T2.1's exported `normalizeOccurredAt` returns — never the producer's raw input, because the wire schema admits `+05:00` offsets and omitted seconds while the `session_events.occurred_at` column is declared RFC 3339 UTC at millisecond precision, and because normalization is many-to-one, so `daemon_signature` commits to the instant rather than to the stored bytes (a raw spelling both violates the column contract and drops the row out of every lexical date-range scan while still verifying); (2) `append()` MUST perform the `actor` three-state → two-state narrowing (absent / `null` / string → `null` / string) before the envelope reaches `canonicalizeEvent`, because `session_events.actor` collapses absent and `null` onto one row while canonical bytes distinguish them — signing an ABSENT `actor` for a row that will persist SQL NULL emits bytes a verifier rehydrating that row cannot reproduce, i.e. an untampered row failing verification. Neither obligation has another home: T2.1 refuses outright to guess the `actor` shape, and it can normalize `occurredAt` but never persist it. **A third obligation lands here from T2.4: the durable PII owner-stamp column.** New additive migration `0007-pii-participant-id.ts` adds `session_events.pii_participant_id` (TEXT, nullable — NULL on every row with no `pii_payload`), and the append path MUST populate it with the SAME participant id T2.4's codec stamped into the signed `payload` under `PII_PARTICIPANT_ID_PAYLOAD_KEY` on that same write. The column is what I-006-2-12 gives T4.1 something to compare the signed claim AGAINST — until it lands, the claim inside the canonical bytes is the only copy and the binding is unenforceable, which is why `Spec-006 §Canonical Serialization Rules` assigns the column to this task by name. Divergence between the two is not an error state to handle here but exactly the tamper the read-side predicate reports. **A fourth obligation lands by the 2026-08-11 amendment (Codex PR #323 round 2): the canonical-size ceiling.** `append()` MUST refuse any envelope whose `canonical_bytes(row)` exceeds the exported `EVENT_CANONICAL_BYTES_MAX = 32768` constant (authored beside `EVENT_ENVELOPE_SEQUENCE_MAX` in `packages/contracts/src/event.ts`) with the typed `daemon.event_canonical_bytes_exceeded` error (dotted code + `*_CODE` constant in `packages/contracts/src/error.ts`, registered at [error-contracts.md §Daemon](../architecture/contracts/error-contracts.md#daemon), 400 — the `daemon.pii_split_bypass` hard-write-path-rejection class), before any row is written and never by truncation, per `Spec-006 §Canonical Serialization Rules`: the bound is the serviceability guarantee that every appended row can later travel the `Spec-008 §Peer History Backfill On Join (V1)` relay seam inside one 64 KB frame (base64 4/3 expansion plus entry, chunk, AEAD, and framing overhead ≈45 KiB worst case). The shipped append path predates the check and takes it — with the constant, the error registration, and a refusal test whose fixture exceeds the bound while a boundary-size fixture appends — in the same immediate follow-up code PR as T3.3's anchor-claim preimage update — landed 2026-08-12, PR #324 (no shipped test or fixture appends within an order of magnitude of the bound, so nothing existing is invalidated). Depends: T2.1, T2.2, T2.4, T2.7; Plan-001 forward-declared columns; Plan-001 `I-001-2` (sequence as canonical replay key). IdempotencyClass: `manual_reconcile_only` per chain-append non-retry-safety.

- **Spec coverage:** Spec-006 §Integrity Protocol (chained to its predecessor), Spec-006 §Resolved Questions and V1 Scope Decisions (sequence numbers), Spec-006 §Canonical Serialization Rules (pii_ciphertext_digest), Spec-006 §Event Maintenance (event_maintenance) (event.shredded), Spec-006 §Security Events (security_events) (daemon.pii_split_bypass typed error — characterized at the daemon.pii_split_ambiguous row as an append-without-pii_ciphertext_digest hard rejection, distinct from that taxonomy event), Spec-006 §Event Type Summary (run_lifecycle category census row — the 13-type enumeration whose 3-type terminal subset scopes the backstop index), Spec-006 §Run Lifecycle (run_lifecycle) (projection-level terminal-key CHECK assignment + NULL-bypass — this task's trigger trio), Spec-006 §Audit Integrity (audit_integrity) (the key_reuse_detected observation event + node-scope sentinel binding the gate's halt state derives from; the append-admission halt rule itself is plan-declared at I-006-4-03, not spec-text — honest-coverage scoping per PR #281 Codex round 2), Spec-006 §Daemon-Scope Event Binding And Node-Scope Anchoring (the sentinel carve-out in the IngestHaltSource contract)
- **Verifies invariant:** none (append-path service + `withSessionAppendLock` publication + terminal-backstop index + terminal-key CHECK trigger trio; the terminal exactly-once property is enforced primarily by Plan-004's B9 T3.7 emitter, and this index is its schema-level backstop rather than a Plan-006-named invariant; the write-path and PII-split invariants name Phase 2 pii-indirection.ts, not this task; the ingest-halt gate declared here is I-006-4-03's enforcement point, but that invariant stays T4.2-named — the observer owns detection and the halted set, this task owns only the consult-and-refuse)
- **Precondition (added 2026-07-27, PR #256 Codex round 3):** before this task's `EventLogService.append` is wired as a durable writer, Plan-001's `SessionService.append` MUST be retired or guarded, and Plan-003's shipped `RuntimeNodeEventEmitter` re-pointed off it. `SessionService.append` writes `prev_hash` / `row_hash` / `daemon_signature` as unconditional zero-fill placeholders with no flag and no guard, it is exported from the package root, and Plan-003 routes its five `runtime_node.*` events through it — so "T3.1 is the sole durable writer" is a sequencing obligation this row owns, not a property the tree already has. It holds vacuously today for a reason that will expire: `runtime-daemon` has no dependents, no `bin`, and every `openDatabase` caller is under `__tests__/`, so no composition root wires a real database yet. The first one that does decides which append path runs first, and if that is the legacy path the rows it writes are exactly the never-signed rows `signature_placeholder` refuses fail-closed — detected, but only after the fact. Discovered while adjudicating a review finding against the [Plan-001 §Data And Storage Changes](./001-shared-session-core.md#data-and-storage-changes) placeholder-convention bullet, which is corrected in the same PR to state the verified state rather than forecast this one. **Discharged (guard + decouple legs) 2026-07-28, PR #272:** `SessionService.append` throws unless constructed with the test-only `allowUnsignedPlaceholderAppend` opt-in (pinned by the exported-title negative control `packages/runtime-daemon/src/session/__tests__/session-service.test.ts#DEFAULT_CONSTRUCTED_APPEND_REFUSAL_TEST`), and the emitter's dep is the structural `SessionEventLog` seam in `node-event-emitter.ts`; the remaining leg — wiring `EventLogService` as the production append path — is this task's own work, and it is a re-point, not an injection (PR #272 Codex round 1): the seam is synchronous-transactional by contract — Plan-003's L2 producers emit inside better-sqlite3 connection-level transactions whose rollback semantics need `append`'s failure to throw synchronously, and the emitter refuses a thenable `append` result fail-closed — so wiring the Promise-returning `append(envelope, options)` means re-pointing the emitter and restructuring those producers' dual-write atomicity around `withSessionAppendLock`.

##### T3.2 — Compactor with three triggers + anchor-before-compaction protocol + audit-stub format + `retention_class` discriminator (Design B — typed column)

Files: `packages/runtime-daemon/src/events/compactor.ts` (NEW); `packages/runtime-daemon/src/migrations/0009-retention-class-and-stub-signature.ts` (NEW — the additive `retention_class` + `stub_signature` migration this task body already specifies); `packages/runtime-daemon/src/session/migration-runner.ts` (EXTEND — that migration's prescribed same-commit `hasMigrationApplied`-guarded registration block; both files assigned 2026-08-03 by the Phase-3 migration file-assignment true-up, shipped as `0009` 2026-08-04, PR #287). Provides: `Compactor.tick(): Promise<CompactionPassResult>` invoked by daemon idle scheduler; SQL selector excluding `category IN ('audit_integrity', 'event_maintenance')` (layer 1 of three-layer enforcement); **anchor-before-compaction enforcement per [Spec-006 §Post-Compaction Integrity](../specs/006-session-event-taxonomy-and-audit-log.md#post-compaction-integrity) — before mutating any row in the to-be-compacted range, verify a covering Merkle anchor exists in `pending_anchor_uploads` or `event_log_anchors`; if absent, force-fire via T3.3 `MerkleAnchorService.anchorRange({sessionId, fromSeq, toSeq})` and wait for durable queue insertion before proceeding (refuses to compact if force-fire fails)**; **per-row stub commitment — for each row, build the audit-stub projection (`Spec-006 §Compacted Event Format`) — for terminal `run_lifecycle` rows (`run.completed` / `run.failed` / `run.interrupted`) the projection preserves `payload.runId` + `payload.runVersion` verbatim, so the T3.1 `idx_session_events_run_terminal_once` backstop's indexed expressions stay non-NULL and the exactly-once guarantee holds across compaction (`Spec-006 §Compacted Event Format` requires both on terminal `run_lifecycle` rows); for accepted `run.rolled_back` rows the projection preserves `payload.runId` + `payload.runVersion` + `payload.targetPosition` verbatim (the epoch-boundary rewind cutoff the Plan-004 T3.14 supersede projection rebuilds from — campaign B9 CP-004-12); and for any row whose payload carries the cross-cutting `sourceEpoch` stamp it preserves the `sourceEpoch` + `sourcePosition` + `runId` triple verbatim (run-local epoch attribution — same registration); for **every run-scoped row** (payload carries `runId`) it preserves that `runId` — read directly from the payload, vacuous-default era included — plus, when the injected source resolves one, the row's originating position under the T1.9-pinned `ORIGIN_POSITION_STUB_KEY` (`originPosition`), the `Spec-004 §Required Behavior` rewind-span check's detection keys (Codex round 5); additionally, for a run-scoped row that the injected **`RollbackAttributionSource`** attributes to a superseded epoch/turn at compaction time (a rolled-back run's ordinary rewound-tail row — the live row never carried the stamp), the projection **stamps** that same triple into the stub per `Spec-006 §Compacted Event Format` compaction-time stamping, while a current-attributed row takes no stamp; the task also provides that interface — `RollbackAttributionSource.attributeAtCompaction({sessionId, eventId, sequence}): Promise<{disposition: 'current', position?: number} | {disposition: 'superseded', sourceEpoch: number, sourcePosition: number, runId: RunId} | {disposition: 'defer'}>` — `'defer'` (Codex round 4) meaning a dispatched-but-unconcluded rollback intervention's rewind span covers the row, so the compactor **skips it this pass** (the row stays live, `retention_class` NULL, picked up by a later tick after the intervention concludes — the `Spec-004 §Required Behavior` admission-serialization reciprocal: without deferral, idle compaction in the admission-to-boundary window would stub the rewound tail unstamped, no accepted boundary existing yet, stranding it unattributable for the log's whole retention life), with the per-row **attribute-and-stub running under T3.1's `withSessionAppendLock`** (one hold per row, never across I/O) so the admission-side span-check-plus-intervention-write and the compactor-side attribute-and-stub serialize, never interleave; constructor-injected with a vacuous default (`'current'` for every row, never `'defer'`) that stands until Plan-004 T3.14's supersede projection implements it **and its composition-root wiring replaces the default** — the CP-006-1 `PiiEncryptor` injection precedent, composition root (Plan-001 / Plan-003 bootstrap) included: Plan-004 T3.14 ships the wiring edit plus the runtime assertion that production construction no longer carries the default, per the CP-006-1 assertion pattern (body-registered provide-forward; until Plan-004 lands, no rollback intervention exists and the default is correct, so tier order is preserved) — serialize it once to its canonical byte string `B = canonical_bytes(stub)`, compute `stub_signature = Ed25519(B)` with the daemon signing key, then store that **same** `B` verbatim in `payload` (sign-exact-bytes invariant — no re-serialization between signing and storing, so the verifier checks `stub_signature` directly over the stored `payload` bytes; canonical bytes bind `id`+`sequence`, so the signature is non-replayable across rows)**; audit-stub UPDATE that **REPLACES** `payload` with that canonical byte string `B` (the JCS-canonicalized audit-stub projection) (the column is `NOT NULL` — it is rewritten, NOT nulled — so replay/renderer can still surface the visible stub per `Spec-006 §Replay Interaction with Compacted Regions`), NULLs `correlation_id`/`causation_id`/`pii_payload`, sets `retention_class = 'audit_stub'`, and writes `stub_signature`, but NEVER mutates `prev_hash`/`row_hash`/`daemon_signature`/`participant_signature`/`monotonic_ns`/`version` (I-006-3-03); `event.compacted` envelope per `Spec-006 §Event Maintenance (event_maintenance)` (`.parse()`d through T1.11's exported `EventCompactedPayloadSchema` at the emission seam); **new additive migration `0009-retention-class-and-stub-signature.ts` adds `session_events.retention_class TEXT CHECK (retention_class IS NULL OR retention_class = 'audit_stub')` (typed discriminator over JSON-field probe; the column-level CHECK closes the discriminator domain and is ALTER-ADD-COLUMN-addable — it references only the new column and permits NULL, so pre-migration rows pass) + `session_events.stub_signature BLOB` (per-row post-compaction commitment, NULL for live rows) columns. The co-presence invariant (`retention_class = 'audit_stub'` ⟺ non-NULL `stub_signature`) is a two-column constraint that ALTER cannot add as a table-level CHECK without a 12-step rebuild of the append-only audit log; it is enforced at the verification layer instead (T4.1 — NULL `stub_signature` on an `audit_stub` row → `stub_signature_invalid`).** Depends: T3.1 (event.compacted emission); T3.3 (`MerkleAnchorService.anchorRange()` API extension for force-fire); T2.7 daemon signing key (consumer — `stub_signature` minting); Phase 1 contracts. IdempotencyClass: `compensable` per mid-pass replay-safety (re-entry skips already-stubbed rows via `retention_class IS NULL`; anchor force-fire is idempotent per T3.3 `UNIQUE(session_id, node_id, start_sequence, end_sequence)`).

##### T3.5 — Phase 3 contract-test suite + end-to-end shred-safety regression (`Plan-006 §Test And Verification Plan` acceptance gate)

Files: Five `__tests__/` files spanning T3.1-T3.4 + shred-safety E2E. Provides: Genesis-and-multi-row chain integrity test; trigger tests for each of the three compaction thresholds; cadence test for the earlier-of rule with the `AnchorPayload` no-`payload`-field TypeScript structural assertion; batch-boundary granularity (1 batch → 1 event) + rollback path + reconcile-on-startup; **E2E: 60+ events with non-null `pii_payload` → compaction pass → Plan-022 Path 1 crypto-shred via `participant_keys` DELETE → `event.shredded` callback → integrity verifier reruns over full chain → all signatures verify, all chain hashes verify, and the shredded participant's PII partition reads back as a single `<pii-shredded>` marker at one reserved payload key — partition-level, never per field (the field names live inside the ciphertext) — with the arms additionally asserting the negative property that no byte of the plaintext survives anywhere in the projection.** **Terminal-backstop index fail-loud test (T3.1's `idx_session_events_run_terminal_once`): a second terminal `run_lifecycle` row (`run.completed` / `run.failed` / `run.interrupted`) carrying the same non-null `(runId, runVersion)` raises a SQLite UNIQUE-constraint error rather than silently absorbing (fail-loud); the same duplicate-terminal insertion re-run **after** the first terminal row has been compacted to an audit stub still raises the UNIQUE violation — because T3.2's audit-stub projection preserves `runId` + `runVersion`, the backstop holds across the live→compacted transition (a compactor omission or payload-path drift that NULLed either indexed expression would silently re-admit the duplicate, and this case is the guard against exactly that); and the index is correctly scoped — a non-terminal `run_lifecycle` duplicate and a terminal row for a different `(runId, runVersion)` both insert freely. Terminal-key CHECK trigger tests (T3.1's `trg_run_terminal_key_insert` / `trg_run_terminal_key_update`): a terminal `run_lifecycle` INSERT with NULL `runId` or `runVersion` — which the UNIQUE index passes because NULLs are distinct — is aborted by `trg_run_terminal_key_insert` (fail-loud at the schema layer, backstopping the Plan-004 B9 T3.7 emitter's non-null precondition, not replacing it); and a stub rewrite (`UPDATE OF payload`) that drops `runId` or `runVersion` from a terminal row is aborted by `trg_run_terminal_key_update` — the negative control for T3.2's stub-preservation rule, so a compactor that violated preservation fails loud here rather than silently reopening the NULL-bypass (the emitter-behavior triplet itself rides B9 T3.7, not this suite).** Terminal-key **type/value negative tests** (T3.1's json_type + value-equality guards): a terminal INSERT whose `runId` is a JSON number (not text) and one whose `runVersion` is a JSON string (not integer) each abort via the `json_type` checks (the `"7"`-vs-`7` storage-class drift the UNIQUE index would miss); a stub-rewriting `UPDATE` that ALTERS `runId` / `runVersion` to a different non-null value (not merely drops it) aborts via the value-equality `IS NOT` check, and one that flips `category` or `type` off the terminal set aborts via the de-scope check — the compactor-bug guards behind T3.2's stub-preservation. Rollback-attribution preservation tests (T3.2's projection, campaign B9 CP-004-12 — Codex round 2): compacting an accepted `run.rolled_back` row preserves `runId` + `runVersion` + `targetPosition` verbatim in the stored stub projection; compacting a `sourceEpoch`-stamped row preserves the `sourceEpoch` + `sourcePosition` + `runId` triple verbatim; and — against a fixture `RollbackAttributionSource` (the vacuous default in every other suite) — compacting a rolled-back run's ordinary rewound-tail row **stamps** the triple into its stub while a current-attributed sibling's stub stays unstamped (compaction-time stamping + its negative control); a fixture source answering `'defer'` leaves the row live (`retention_class` NULL, no stub write) and a later pass with the source answering `'superseded'` stamps it — the deferral round-trip (Codex round 4) — and every run-scoped row's stub preserves its `runId` with a position-resolving fixture additionally writing `originPosition` while the vacuous default writes none (the legacy-stub control: `runId` present, position absent — Codex round 5) — while a concurrent same-session `withSessionAppendLock` call issued mid-stub blocks until the row's attribute-and-stub completes (the serialization the `Spec-004 §Required Behavior` admission check relies on) — each read back from the stored `payload` bytes, pinning the projection itself at the compactor layer (the Plan-004 T3.14 rebuild-after-compaction integration test consumes these fields downstream; this suite is the compactor-side guarantee it relies on). Ingest-halt gate tests (T3.1's `IngestHaltSource` seam): the vacuous `NeverHaltedIngestHaltSource` default admits every session (the dormancy control); `IngestHaltRegistry` round-trips — `halt(sessionId)` flips `isHalted` true, `clear(sessionId)` flips it back, driving live refusal then re-admission through a registry-wired `append()` — the refuse → `clear()` → re-admit round trip asserted deadlock-free, a property that exists only because rejection releases the hold (F-006-HALT-01) — a repeat `halt()` on an already-halted session returns without blocking behind a parked append (the pre-lock membership short-circuit, F-006-HALT-07), and the write path throws on an attempt to halt the node-scope sentinel session; the halt-vs-in-flight-append interleaving arm (PR #281 Codex round 2): with an `append()` parked mid-critical-section in a fixture async sealer, a concurrent `halt(sessionId)` does not return until the parked append commits and releases the session lock, and the next `append()` for that session then refuses — halt publication serializes on `withSessionAppendLock`, never landing mid-append — while a concurrent `halt` on a DIFFERENT session returns without waiting (the lock is per-session) — the lock suite resets the module-singleton state in `beforeEach` through the test-only reset export, so a parked fixture that fails or times out cannot poison later same-session arms (F-006-HALT-09); against a fixture halt source, `append()` for a halted session refuses with the typed `daemon.ingest_halted` error before canonicalization and before any write (no partial row, no chain-head advance), a non-halted sibling session appends normally in the same pass (the halt is session-keyed, never daemon-global), and the sentinel carve-out holds — a contract-honoring fixture never halts the node-scope sentinel session, so the alarm append path stays admissible under an active halt. Append-lock tests (T3.1's `withSessionAppendLock`): nested-call progress (an inner `append()` completes under the outer hold), concurrent same-session blocking (two parallel `withSessionAppendLock` calls serialize — the second waits), and cross-session non-blocking (distinct sessions run concurrently). Depends: T3.1-T3.4; Phase 2; Plan-022's `splitPii()` (mock via fixture if Plan-022 implementation hasn't landed). IdempotencyClass: N/A (test).

### Phase 3B — Machine-authored content column (explicit-label supplement)

**Goal:** Ship `session_events.content_payload` and its wrapped key home `session_content_keys` — the durable encrypted home for assistant- and tool-generated prose, its write path, its read-side verification, and the five payload variants without which nothing can write to it — as an additive supplement to the already-merged Phase 3. A supplement rather than an edit to Phase 3, because Phase 3 shipped 2026-08-04 (PR #287) and a merged phase is not amendable in place; the Plan-016 Phase 3B and Plan-004 Phase 3B supplements are the precedent. Task numbering continues the Phase-3 series.

**Gate:** Phase 3 merged. Nothing else — the column is Plan-006-internal and consumes no other plan's contract. Its **consumers** are gated the other way: [Plan-005](./005-provider-driver-contract-and-capabilities.md) T3.20 holds on this phase merging (CP-006-15 ⇄ CP-005-13).

#### Tasks

##### T3.6 — `content_payload` column, session-key sealer, and the extended write path

Files: `packages/runtime-daemon/src/migrations/0NNN-content-payload.ts` (NEW — ordinal deliberately unpinned in this plan and resolved at the task's own PR, the `received_from_node_id` placeholder idiom, because ordinals are claimed in merge order and `0012` is already claimed by an in-flight branch; **landed at this task's PR as `0013-content-payload.ts`, and the five variants take the union 25 → 30 arms** — a landed-fact annotation, nothing normative; the migration does two additive things and nothing else: `ALTER TABLE session_events ADD COLUMN content_payload BLOB` — the `0007-pii-participant-id.ts` shape, nullable with no `CHECK`, no `NOT NULL`, and no `FK`, so no 12-step table rebuild is required — and `CREATE TABLE session_content_keys` per [Local SQLite Schema §Session Content Keys](../architecture/schemas/local-sqlite-schema.md#session-content-keys-plan-006), the wrapped home of the per-session DEK); `packages/runtime-daemon/src/session/migration-runner.ts` (EXTEND — the prescribed same-commit `hasMigrationApplied`-guarded registration block, since an orphan migration file leaves its schema absent); `packages/runtime-daemon/src/events/pii-indirection.ts` (EXTEND — the second codec, housed in the sole write path rather than beside it); `packages/runtime-daemon/src/events/event-log-service.ts` (EXTEND — persist the column from the same codec result that produced the digest, exactly as T3.1 persists `pii_participant_id`); `packages/runtime-daemon/src/events/session-content-key-store.ts` (NEW — the sole reader/minter of `session_content_keys`: lazily mints a session's 32-byte DEK on first content-bearing append, persists it only as an XChaCha20-Poly1305 envelope under the daemon master key — 24-byte random nonce, wire `nonce || ciphertext || tag`, `AAD = session_id || "ais.session-content-wrap.v1" || key_version`, the Plan-022 participant-wrap form domain-separated by its own info string — with **two required negative tests**: a blob moved to another session's row MUST fail to unwrap, and a blob replayed under another `key_version` MUST fail to unwrap (without the AAD both authenticate on the master key alone and degrade into a silent `'turn_content_unavailable'`), and exposes the re-wrap entry point [Spec-022](../specs/022-data-retention-and-gdpr.md)' rotate-on-shred calls **inside its existing `BEGIN EXCLUSIVE`**, never as a pass of its own); `packages/contracts/src/event.ts` (EXTEND — the `CONTENT_PAYLOAD_PLAINTEXT_MAX` and `CONTENT_CIPHERTEXT_DIGEST_PAYLOAD_KEY` constants, exported so producers and the verifier never duplicate a literal, the `PII_PARTICIPANT_ID_PAYLOAD_KEY` convention; **and the five body-bearing `SessionEventSchema` payload variants** — `assistant.message`, `assistant.thinking_update`, `tool.invoked`, `tool.result`, `tool.error` — registered through the additive union-registration seam this plan owns, each strict and each declaring the descriptive members only (`contentType?` / `contentLength?` / `contentTruncated?` / `contentCiphertextDigest?`) and **no** body member. Without these five the column can be created, sealed, and cleared but never written to: the union had 25 arms at this task's authoring, none assistant or tool, so both driver normalizers resolved every one of these types `payload-variant-pending` and refused envelope construction — the compile-time fact this whole amendment exists to answer, which a column alone does not change; `packages/contracts/src/event.ts` additionally declares the read-side **`HydratedSessionEvent`** type — the verified event paired with a closed two-arm `content` union (available: body + signed `contentLength` / `contentTruncated`; unavailable: the reason) — so the decrypted body is never spliced into `payload`, which would either add an undeclared key that fails strict validation or mutate the apparent shape of already-verified bytes). **Provides:** the session content-key store — a random 32-byte AES-256 DEK minted lazily on a session's first content-bearing append and persisted only as an XChaCha20-Poly1305 envelope under the daemon master key in `session_content_keys`, the `participant_keys.encrypted_key_blob` custody shape reused rather than a new one invented, so [Spec-022](../specs/022-data-retention-and-gdpr.md)' rotate-on-shred re-wraps it in the same `BEGIN EXCLUSIVE` that re-wraps `participant_keys` and no body is lost to an unrelated participant's erasure; AES-256-GCM sealing with a fresh 96-bit IV and AAD = `session_id || event_id`, stored `iv || ciphertext || tag`; the codepoint-boundary truncation at `CONTENT_PAYLOAD_PLAINTEXT_MAX` with the `contentTruncated` / pre-truncation `contentLength` payload members; and the branded output type that makes the column unconstructible outside this module. **Does NOT** widen the schema doc: [Local SQLite Schema §Session Events](../architecture/schemas/local-sqlite-schema.md#session-events-plan-001-extended-by-plans-006-008-015) already carries the column and is the authority this migration mirrors.
