##### T3.7 — Compactor clears `content_payload`; Phase 3B contract tests

Files: `packages/runtime-daemon/src/events/compactor.ts` (EXTEND — three coordinated changes. (a) Add `content_payload` to the columns the stub transaction sets NULL, alongside `pii_payload` and `pii_participant_id`. (b) **Preserve `contentLength` and `contentTruncated` in the stub projection** whenever the source payload carries them, the conditional payload-derived shape `channelId` already uses — the digest deliberately does not survive, being a commitment to bytes this act destroys, while the two descriptive scalars are exactly what an audit stub is for. (c) **Add `COALESCE(LENGTH(content_payload), 0)` to both byte-accounting statements** — `#sessionSummaryStmt`'s per-session `live_bytes` sum and `#storageCutoffStmt`'s running cumulative total — which today sum `payload` + `pii_payload` only. Naming the two rather than saying _every accounting query_ keeps the obligation checkable by count. Without (c) a content-bearing session compacts on the 50,000-event count trigger alone and can reach roughly 12.5 GiB at 256 KiB per row, twenty-five times past the 500 MB threshold [Spec-006 §Event Compaction Policy](../specs/006-session-event-taxonomy-and-audit-log.md#event-compaction-policy) claims); `packages/runtime-daemon/src/events/__tests__/` (EXTEND). **Provides:** the compaction clause as shipped behavior, plus the tests that make the two new invariants falsifiable rather than asserted — a round-trip seal/open over a session-keyed body; a negative test that no plaintext byte of an over-bound body survives past the truncation point and that `contentLength` still reports the pre-truncation length; a type-level assertion that an `audit_integrity` or `event_maintenance` `RawEventInput` cannot carry the column; a canonical-bytes test proving the ciphertext is excluded and the digest included, byte-identical to a row that carries no content at all once the digest is fixed; a post-compaction test asserting the column is NULL, that the stub projection carries no digest member, and that it **does** carry `contentLength` / `contentTruncated` when the source payload had them; and a storage-threshold test built from content-heavy rows — rows whose `payload` and `pii_payload` are small and whose `content_payload` is large — asserting the 500 MB storage trigger fires on them, which is the only test shape that fails against the pre-fix accounting. _(landed fact, 2026-08-30, PR #386 round-3 fold: the retirement leg joins this task's Provides — no Phase-3B Provides had named a deletion path on `session_content_keys`, leaving obsolete wrapped DEKs immortal and every historical session on the rotate-on-shred re-wrap walk. Shipped as the third narrow seam `SessionContentKeyDisposer` — `deleteIfUnreferenced`, check-and-delete in one `.immediate` transaction inside `withSessionAppendLock`, so a concurrent mint-then-insert cannot interleave — consumed by the compactor after its clearing pass, verified the sole `content_payload = NULL` writer in `src`, and required with no default at construction; future clearing paths, session purge and the widened Spec-022 sweep among them, consume the same seam. No census moves: no table, column, event type, wire method, Cedar action, or error code.)_

- **Spec coverage:** Spec-006 §Compacted Event Format; Spec-006 §Canonical Serialization Rules
- **Verifies invariant:** I-006-3-05, I-006-3-06

##### T3.8 — Verify-on-read binding, the received-row arm, and the hydrated read path

Files: `packages/runtime-daemon/src/events/pii-indirection.ts` (EXTEND — generalize the digest-binding predicate. The shipped `isCiphertextDigestBound` is hard-wired to the `pii_ciphertext_digest` payload key and the `pii_payload` column; T3.8 factors the four-state compare into a column-and-key-parameterized core and re-expresses the existing PII predicate over it, so the two modes share one implementation rather than the content one being a copy that can drift); `packages/runtime-daemon/src/events/` verifier (EXTEND — register the sixteenth verification mode `content_ciphertext_digest_unbound` with its `received_from_node_id` provenance dispatch) _(landed fact, 2026-08-30, PR #386 review fold: no such verifier module exists at Phase 3B — `integrity-verifier.ts` is T4.1's to CREATE, and `signer.ts`'s three-mode `verifyRow` structurally cannot see the column, so this clause named an EXTEND of a file a later task creates. What Phase 3B ships is the mode's contracts registration plus the provenance dispatch itself — `isContentCiphertextDigestBoundUnderProvenance` in `pii-indirection.ts`, compare-on-origin / require-absent-on-received per I-006-3-07 — consumed by the real read entry point; the verdict-emitting verifier consumes that predicate at T4.1, exactly as I-006-2-10 already orders the PII modes. Erratum-class annotation; no census or invariant content moves.)_; `packages/runtime-daemon/src/events/content-read.ts` (NEW — the hydrated read path: open `content_payload` under the session content key and return `HydratedSessionEvent`, never mutating `payload`); `packages/runtime-daemon/src/events/__tests__/` (EXTEND). **Provides:** the read-side half the append-side tasks leave unguarded. Without it a `content_payload` replaced or removed after signing leaves the row's signature green while decryption merely reports the body unavailable — silently reclassifying at-rest tampering as ordinary transcript loss, which is exactly the confusion `'turn_content_unavailable'` must not absorb. The mode compares on origin rows (`received_from_node_id IS NULL`) and takes the **require-absent** arm on received rows, since the column is never relayed; it is a verifier `failureMode`, so no [error-contracts.md](../architecture/contracts/error-contracts.md) row is added, exactly as the fourteenth and fifteenth add none. The hydrated path resolves the `unavailable` arm's reason — column NULL, key unavailable, digest unbound, or row compacted — which is where the fold's `'turn_content_unavailable'` gets its truth value instead of inferring loss from an absent key. Tests: a replaced-ciphertext row fails; a digest-with-column-missing row fails; a clean received row with a NULL column **passes** rather than being reported tampered; a fail-closed assertion that a non-`Uint8Array`, non-NULL column value is reported unbound rather than skipped; and a round-trip proving the hydrated projection carries the body while the verified event's `payload` is byte-identical to what was signed.

- **Spec coverage:** Spec-006 §Canonical Serialization Rules; Spec-006 §Audit Integrity (`audit_integrity`)
- **Verifies invariant:** I-006-3-07

### Phase 4 — Read-Side + SDK + Desktop Stub

#### Tasks

##### T4.1 — `integrity-verifier.ts` three-check verifier + full 16-value `failureMode` enum + anchor + stub-signature + scalar-binding compacted-row verification + stored-`occurred_at` canonical-form check + PII digest and owner-stamp binding checks

Files: `packages/runtime-daemon/src/events/integrity-verifier.ts` (NEW) + test. Provides: `verifyRange(params): Promise<VerifyResult>` returning `{kind: 'verified' | 'failed', ...}` with the 16-value `failureMode` enum (`'hash_mismatch' | 'signature_mismatch' | 'anchor_mismatch' | 'inclusion_proof_failed' | 'consistency_proof_failed' | 'log_file_missing' | 'log_file_moved' | 'anchor_missing_for_compacted_range' | 'anchor_signature_invalid' | 'stub_signature_invalid' | 'stub_scalar_mismatch' | 'signature_placeholder' | 'occurred_at_not_canonical' | 'pii_ciphertext_digest_unbound' | 'pii_owner_stamp_unbound' | 'signing_key_slot_conflict'`) verbatim from `Spec-006 §Audit Integrity (audit_integrity)` (post-amendment — the four `anchor_missing_for_compacted_range` / `anchor_signature_invalid` / `stub_signature_invalid` / `stub_scalar_mismatch` modes are additive-MINOR extensions per `ADR-018 §Decision` #8 sanctioning the post-compaction integrity protocol; `signature_placeholder` is the 2026-07-26 additive-MINOR fail-closed mode for a row whose `daemon_signature` is still the 64-zero-byte placeholder — surfaced by T2.2's `verifyRow` ahead of any Ed25519 verification, never reported as `signature_mismatch`, per `Spec-006 §Integrity Protocol`; `occurred_at_not_canonical` is the 2026-07-27 additive-MINOR stored-spelling mode, paired `failurePath: 'signature'` because that field names the guarantee that failed rather than the column the defect occupies; `pii_owner_stamp_unbound` is the 2026-07-27 additive-MINOR owner-stamp mode taking the same sanction and the same `failurePath` for the same reason; `signing_key_slot_conflict` is the 2026-08-01 additive-MINOR sixteenth mode (Codex PR #274 round 5) taking the same `ADR-018 §Decision` #8 sanction and the same `failurePath: 'signature'` — in the `VerifierFailureModeSchema` this task ships but NOT in `verifyRange`'s emission set, the first mode whose emitter is not a read-side verifier: T4.10's daemon-side registrar conflict handler appends it when a genuine daemon's re-registration is refused 409 for its own occupied `(session_id, node_id)` slot, so this verifier emits fifteen of the sixteen; the `audit_integrity_failed` payload is `failureMode`-DISCRIMINATED per `Spec-006 §Audit Integrity (audit_integrity)` (2026-08-01 follow-up, PR #276): the fifteen verifier modes REQUIRE the Merkle triple `treeSize`/`expectedRootHash`/`observedRootHash` while the registrar arm carries `failureMode`/`failurePath`/`detail` only — an emitter that verified no range never fabricates roots — and the payload schema ships both arms as one discriminated union — `AuditIntegrityFailedPayloadSchema`, authored at T1.11 in `packages/contracts/src/event.ts` (authoring site resolved by the 2026-08-01 delta's docket-7 pass; this task is a registered consumer, `.parse()`-ing it at its emission seam), the symbol name pinned per the `<EventType>PayloadSchema` naming idiom (`packages/contracts/src/runtime-node.ts#RuntimeNodeRegisteredPayloadSchema`; the plan-pinned `ApprovalFlowEventPayloadSchema` authored at `Plan-012 §Phase 1 — Approval contracts + persistence` T1.1) so the implementer never coins a sibling — the verifier arm keyed on `VerifierFailureModeSchema.exclude(['signing_key_slot_conflict'])` — the Zod enum `.exclude()` derivation per [Zod §Enums](https://zod.dev/api#enums) (accessed 2026-08-01: _"To create a new enum schema, excluding certain values"_ — the documented method, distinct from the spec-cited discriminated-union page), keeping the sixteen-value schema the single source of truth rather than hand-maintaining a fifteen-value sibling, explicitly type-annotated per the repo-wide `isolatedDeclarations` requirement); **stored-`occurred_at` canonical-form check, run per row on BOTH retention classes: call T2.1's exported `isCanonicalOccurredAt` on the `occurred_at` column and report `failureMode: 'occurred_at_not_canonical'` when it returns `false` (I-006-2-10). This is a SECOND check rather than a stronger verification — `canonicalizeEvent` normalizes `occurredAt` and passes every other member through byte-for-byte, so hash and signature both still verify over a column respelled at rest into another spelling of the same instant, while the respelled row drops out of the lexical date-range scans this walk itself uses. The predicate never throws, which is load-bearing here specifically: one throw inside the range walk would abort it and silence audit of the whole remaining tail, so a non-string column value must yield a verdict, never an exception. It is NOT redundant with check (c) below — (c) catches a respelling applied after compaction, whereas one applied while the row was live is copied verbatim into the projection by `Spec-006 §Compacted Event Format`, signed into the stub bytes, and byte-equal to its column forever.** **PII-ciphertext digest binding, run per row and provenance-dispatched on the marker (the 2026-08-11 received-row provenance amendment — no row is skipped by provenance) as a POSTCONDITION of the signature check rather than before it: on an origin-authored row (`received_from_node_id IS NULL`) call T2.4's exported `isCiphertextDigestBound(piiPayloadColumn, verifiedPayload)` and report `failureMode: 'pii_ciphertext_digest_unbound'` with `failurePath: 'signature'` when it returns `false`; on a received row take the require-absent arm instead — `pii_payload` MUST be NULL, a planted value reported under the same mode and never compared, because the origin's signed digest riding the re-signed bytes is exactly what a planted ciphertext would be compared against, a copy vouching for its own source (Security Architecture rule 2's received-row segment) (I-006-2-11). Ordering is not stylistic — the digest it compares against lives INSIDE the payload the Ed25519 check authenticates, so recomputing it over unverified bytes proves nothing; the two column-shape preconditions above are pure form tests and therefore run first. The predicate decides four states, so no caller-side NULL guard is needed and none should be added: a compacted row lands in the both-absent state (`Spec-006 §Compacted Event Format` NULLs `pii_payload` and re-projects without a digest) and a crypto-shredded row lands in the compare state and PASSES, because `Spec-022 §Shred Fan-Out` Path 1 destroys the key and overwrites no column. Like the form check it never throws, and it additionally shape-guards the column value, since a `BLOB`-affinity read can return a `string` on which the hash primitive would throw — a non-`Uint8Array` non-NULL value is reported unbound, fail-closed, rather than skipped.** **PII owner-stamp binding, run per row under the same provenance dispatch as a POSTCONDITION of the same signature check: on an origin-authored row call T2.4's exported `isPiiOwnerStampBound(piiParticipantIdColumn, verifiedPayload)` and report `failureMode: 'pii_owner_stamp_unbound'` with `failurePath: 'signature'` when it returns `false`; on a received row the require-absent arm — a planted `pii_participant_id` reported under the same mode, never compared, the durable stamp being NULL by its own no-`pii_payload`-no-stamp rule and a mirror of the signed claim carrying zero integrity content (I-006-2-12). It is NOT discharged by the digest check beside it — the digest binds WHICH bytes the row holds, the stamp binds WHOSE data they are, and after `Spec-022 §Shred Fan-Out` Path 1 destroys the key no decrypt can ever re-attribute the retained ciphertext, so the stamp is the sole surviving answer to the second question and a tampered one corrupts the `affectedSessionIds[]` / `piiPayloadsCleared` scope selector an `event.shredded` row retains indefinitely as compliance evidence. Like its two siblings the predicate never throws, decides four states (both absent passes — an ordinary no-PII row; both present compares; either alone is unbound), and needs no caller-side NULL guard, so none should be added. **One caller-side scoping was conditional and is now RESOLVED (T3.1 + T3.2 landed 2026-08-04):** the predicate assumes compaction clears the stamp column alongside `pii_payload`, which is the SET disposition rather than an even split — `Spec-006 §Compacted Event Format`'s 2026-07-27 amendment sets the durable column to cleared, matching the stub projection that already carries no `pii_participant_id`, and T3.1's additive migration `0007-pii-participant-id.ts` created the column while T3.2's compactor clears it in the same stub-write statement that NULLs `pii_payload` — confirming that resolution rather than picking between two live branches. No caller-side `retention_class IS NULL` scoping is therefore needed: a compacted row lands in the predicate's both-absent state and passes, and the RETAIN branch — under which this check would have reported every compacted row unbound — did not happen. Softening the predicate's either-alone-is-unbound state remains the wrong fix and is ruled out here — that state is the evidence-destruction case the check exists to catch.** `VerifierFailureModeSchema` + `VerifierFailurePathSchema` (`'inclusion' | 'consistency' | 'signature'`) Zod schemas (authored at T1.11 beside the payload schemas per the 2026-08-01 delta; consumed here); appends `audit_integrity_verified` OR `audit_integrity_failed` per pass (I-006-4-01 exactly-one; both payloads `.parse()`d through the T1.11 exports at this emission seam). **Compacted-row handling per `Spec-006 §Post-Compaction Integrity`: for any row with `retention_class = 'audit_stub'`, run THREE checks (all must pass): (a) skip per-row chain recomputation against the original (canonical bytes unrecoverable) and instead verify a covering Merkle anchor exists in `pending_anchor_uploads` or `event_log_anchors` AND its daemon Ed25519 `root_signature` verifies over the anchor claim — coordinates and root together per `Spec-006 §Anchoring Cadence`'s 2026-08-11 amendment, so a relabeled span fails the signature rather than passing the coverage test — anchor-missing → `failureMode: 'anchor_missing_for_compacted_range'`, anchor-signature-invalid → `failureMode: 'anchor_signature_invalid'`; (b) verify `stub_signature` directly over the canonical byte string stored in `payload` (the exact bytes the compactor signed — not re-canonicalized, not reconstructed from scalar columns) using the `NodeId`-resolved daemon Ed25519 public key — tampered/replayed/missing `stub_signature` → `failureMode: 'stub_signature_invalid'` (the signature is REQUIRED on every `audit_stub` row; absence is a failure, never a skip); (c) decode the audit-stub projection from the just-verified `payload` bytes and assert each surviving scalar column (`id`, `session_id`, `sequence`, `occurred_at`, `category`, `type`, `actor`) byte-equals its projection counterpart (`occurred_at` ↔ `occurredAt`, `session_id` ↔ `sessionId`, …; `compactedAt`/`summary` have no scalar column) — any divergence → `failureMode: 'stub_scalar_mismatch'` (closes the forge-a-scalar-column gap where `stub_signature` over `payload` and the anchor over the frozen `row_hash` both still verify but a filter/reconstruction reads the tampered scalar). Mixed ranges (some uncompacted, some `audit_stub`) are split-verified: chain-recompute the uncompacted prefix + run all three compacted-row checks on the compacted suffix; all must pass.** **Tests arm the provenance dispatch on both classes (2026-08-11):** a received-row fixture (marker column non-NULL) planted with a non-NULL `pii_payload` is reported `pii_ciphertext_digest_unbound`, and one planted with a non-NULL `pii_participant_id` is reported `pii_owner_stamp_unbound` — each driven through `verifyRange`, never by calling the predicates directly — while a clean received row passes both arms and origin-authored fixtures exercise the four-state compares unchanged; the write-side half of the same amendment (a backfill append writes neither PII column) is Plan-008's `T-008r-4-T46`, and marker forgery in either direction is already covered as `signature_mismatch` (Plan-008's `T-008r-4-T42`). Depends: Phase 1 contracts; Phase 2 canonicalizer + signer; Plan-001 `session_events` table; Plan-006 P3 `event_log_anchors` + `pending_anchor_uploads`. IdempotencyClass: `compensable` per per-pass new-row append (consumers dedupe on `(verifierNodeId, fromSeq, toSeq)`).

- **Spec coverage:** Spec-006 §Audit Integrity (audit_integrity) (the audit_integrity_verified + audit_integrity_failed rows), Spec-006 §Post-Compaction Integrity (anchor_missing_for_compacted_range; stub_signature; stub_scalar_mismatch; mixed ranges), Spec-006 §Integrity Protocol (placeholder signatures are refused, never retro-signed — signature_placeholder)
- **Verifies invariant:** I-006-3-03 (compaction anchor-before-stub + chain-commitment-frozen; three-check verifier), I-006-4-01 (verifyRange emits exactly one verified / failed per range), I-006-4-02 (verifier rows are themselves chain-and-signature-protected), I-006-2-10 (this task is the CONSUMER: it calls T2.1's exported isCanonicalOccurredAt per row on both retention classes and reports occurred_at_not_canonical, the verdict T2.1 deliberately does not emit), I-006-2-11 (this task is likewise the CONSUMER: it calls T2.4's exported isCiphertextDigestBound per row as a postcondition of the signature check and reports pii_ciphertext_digest_unbound, the verdict T2.4 deliberately does not emit), I-006-2-12 (CONSUMER of the third provider/consumer pair: it calls T2.4's exported isPiiOwnerStampBound against the T3.1 stamp column as a postcondition of the same signature check and reports pii_owner_stamp_unbound; the compacted-row scoping the predicate defers to its caller is settled by T3.2's clearing stub-write, so this task adds no retention_class filter — but it does add the provenance dispatch: both PII binding checks dispatch on `received_from_node_id` — the four-state compare on origin-authored rows (NULL marker), the require-absent arm on received rows (a planted `pii_payload` or stamp reported under the existing `_unbound` modes, never compared) — per the 2026-08-11 received-row provenance amendment and Security Architecture rule 2's received-row segment)

##### T4.2 — `key-reuse-observer.ts` enforcing `refuse_on_rotation`

Files: `packages/runtime-daemon/src/events/key-reuse-observer.ts` (NEW) + test; `packages/runtime-daemon/src/bootstrap/index.ts` (EXTEND — the sanctioned wiring-call edit into the Plan-007-owned daemon bootstrap orchestrator, the corpus-documented wiring point per the cross-plan §2 bootstrap row; the Plan-026 `registerOnboarding*` / Plan-010 T5.3 retention-sweeper precedent — a wiring call, not ownership; F-006-HALT-04, 2026-08-02 delta); `packages/runtime-daemon/src/events/signing-key-source.ts` (EXTEND — Plan-006-owned: the `listKeyedSessionIds()` keyed-session enumeration read over the `daemon_signing_keys` table the source already owns, plus the injected first-mint hook the composition root binds to `observer.start` — Codex PR #282 round 1). Provides: `KeyReuseObserver` class with `start(sessionId)` / `stop(sessionId)` / `getDetectedReuse()`, populating T3.1's `IngestHaltRegistry` — the `IngestHaltSource` the append gate reads — via `halt`/`clear` from its sweeps rather than implementing the interface itself (the registry split that breaks the observer↔service construction cycle, PR #281 Codex round 1); **sweep publication is per-session single-flight (PR #281 Codex round 3):** at most one sweep per session is in flight — a trigger arriving mid-sweep coalesces into a dirty-flag rerun over a fresh store snapshot after the current completes — so `halt`/`clear` publication always reflects the latest-completed snapshot and a stale sweep can never `clear(sessionId)` over a newer sweep's `halt` (the append lock orders publication against appends; single-flight supplies the sweep-vs-sweep freshness ordering the lock alone cannot), **and sweep-internal ordering is halt-then-alarm (F-006-HALT-05, the 2026-08-02 delta's design adjudication):** on a detected collision the sweep publishes `halt(sessionId)` FIRST and AWAITS it — the publication serializes behind at most the in-flight append, which can be parked in T2.7's human-gated unseal; awaiting is acceptable because single-flight isolation is per-session (other sessions' sweeps proceed) and the coalesced rerun preserves freshness — and only then appends the `key_reuse_detected` alarm, which binds to the node-scope sentinel session and therefore acquires a DIFFERENT per-session lock: the sentinel carve-out restated as the ordering guarantee (the alarm append can neither self-deadlock on the halt it just published nor race it, and the gate closes before the durable record lands — fail-closed priority), with a T4.2 arm that parks an append in the fixture unseal and asserts the halt lands immediately after the parked append settles and the alarm appends after the halt; emits `key_reuse_detected` with `rotationInvariantViolated: 'refuse_on_rotation'` verbatim (`Spec-006 §Audit Integrity (audit_integrity)`; payload `.parse()`d through T1.11's exported `KeyReuseDetectedPayloadSchema` at the emission seam); HALTS ingest from EVERY LOCALLY-HOSTED `(session_id, node_id)` identity the colliding key appears under — never first-wins, first-seen included, because when the threat is "attacker registered first" the first-seen identity is exactly the attacker's (I-006-4-03's fail-closed-on-both-identities rule; Codex PR #278 round 1 trued this task to the sharpened invariant — the prior text halted only the singular colliding signer — and round 3 scoped the halt to the authority a daemon actually holds: the enforcement point is this daemon's OWN append path — V1 has no daemon-to-daemon event ingress and the canonical field set carries no `NodeId`, so a remote identity presents nothing local to halt; remote identities are enumerated in the alarm's `observedIdentities`, halted by their own hosting daemons' observers converging on the same shared roster, and refused new slots by the control plane's register-once conflict arm). The append-path enforcement SEAM — T3.1's injected `IngestHaltSource`, consulted by `EventLogService.append` before admission, the `RollbackAttributionSource` declare-vacuous-then-implement shape — is DECLARED at T3.1 as of the 2026-08-02 follow-up amendment PR #281 (Codex PR #278 round 3's routed mechanism, sequenced before Phase 3 dispatch so T3.1's implementer builds against the declared seam rather than retrofitting it): this task supplies the production wiring — the observer maintains in T3.1's `IngestHaltRegistry` the session-keyed halted set its sweeps derive, constructed registry → `EventLogService` → observer so the observer (which appends through the service) never has to exist before the service does — and the composition root supersedes T3.1's vacuous `NeverHaltedIngestHaltSource` default with the identity runtime assertion (the service's halt source IS the started observer's registry — the CP-006-1 pattern), owning the observer's lifecycle end-to-end (PR #281 Codex round 2; lifecycle source concretized at Codex PR #282 round 1: the observable population is the KEYED sessions — identities holding rows in T2.7's `daemon_signing_keys` table, the only identities a key-reuse collision can implicate — so the wiring consumes Plan-006-owned surfaces only: at daemon boot an enumeration pass starts the observer for every keyed session via the source's new `listKeyedSessionIds()` read, and a session keyed AFTER boot is started by the source's injected first-mint hook — `create(sessionId)`'s first key mint is the observable birth, the hook a vacuous default until the composition root binds `observer.start`, the declare-vacuous-then-implement shape — so no keyed session is ever silently unobserved; `stop(sessionId)` keeps its F-006-HALT-10-pinned semantics below with NO V1 teardown caller — the shipped daemon exposes no session-teardown signal (`SessionService` carries no lifecycle surface; Plan-007's gateway owns any future one), so an inactive keyed session simply stays swept, bounded by keyed-session count, the bound the standing-entry residue clause already accepts). **The post-commit mint hook does not carry correctness alone — the observer runs a self-quiescing RECONCILIATION TICK (Codex PR #282 round 3, closing both P1s of the same class; since the 2026-08-11 decoupling deleted the delivery hook, the tick is also the deferred alarm's SOLE re-trigger):** a single periodic tick that (i) re-runs `listKeyedSessionIds()` and starts the observer for any keyed session it is not already observing (`start(sessionId)` is idempotent ON ITS OWN TERMS: a start for a session already in the observed set returns without re-registering it or issuing a second sweep trigger — deliberately NOT the `halt()` no-op shape, whose pre-lock membership short-circuit is a lock-ordering property with no analogue here, since `start` takes no lock), and (ii) re-triggers the sweep for every session still carrying a DEFERRED alarm. Both legs feed the same per-session single-flight trigger, so a tick landing mid-sweep coalesces into the dirty-flag rerun rather than racing it, and the tick disarms itself when the enumeration finds nothing unobserved and no alarm is deferred (re-arming when a sweep defers or a mint hook rejects), so a quiet daemon does no periodic work. **The arming ORDER is pinned, because a disarm rule evaluated in the wrong window would silently defeat the tick:** the composition root arms the tick AFTER the boot enumeration pass returns, and disarm is decided only on the RESULT of a COMPLETED evaluation — an evaluation whose enumeration read succeeded and whose deferred-alarm set was read after the boot-started observers' first sweeps settled. Both conditions are trivially true in the boot window before any sweep has run, so an evaluation racing boot would disarm the tick on emptiness that only means not-yet; an enumeration read that REJECTS likewise leaves the tick armed rather than treating the failed read as nothing-to-do (fail-armed, the fail-closed shape). Deferred-alarm state is in-memory like the halted set and re-derived the same way — a restart drops it, the boot enumeration starts the observers, their sweeps re-detect and re-defer, and the tick (armed before those sweeps ran) is still armed to carry them. This is what makes the post-commit mint hook safe to keep non-blocking — and the deferred alarm safe with no delivery hook at all (2026-08-11): the T2.7 first-mint hook may reject AFTER the signing-key row commits (a retried `create()` is no longer a first mint, and boot-only enumeration would leave that session unobserved until restart — R3 P1), and a deferred alarm's node-identity wait resolves at daemon startup with no re-sendable external event left to hook (the pre-decoupling delivery trigger's R3 P1 — an acknowledged, never-re-sent delivery stranding the alarm — died with the machinery). Under the tick each case self-heals within one interval with no state held hostage: the mint still commits, and neither alarm nor observation depends on a hook that cannot be retried. T4.2 arms both recovery paths — a rejecting first-mint hook followed by a tick that starts the session, and a deferred alarm followed by a tick that emits it once the node identity resolves — plus the quiescence arm (no deferred alarm, clean enumeration → the tick disarms). Carrying restart re-derivation (halt state is DERIVED from the durable key stores — a fresh observer's sweep-at-start re-detects and re-halts, so no separate halt table exists to desync) and the sentinel carve-out (`key_reuse_detected` binds to the node-scope sentinel session per `Spec-006 §Daemon-Scope Event Binding And Node-Scope Anchoring`, never a member of the halted set, so the alarm append can never self-deadlock). **Stop semantics pinned (F-006-HALT-10, 2026-08-02 delta):** `stop(sessionId)` leaves a published halt entry STANDING — un-halt remains the-collision-leaving-the-observable-set only, never a lifecycle side effect, and a torn-down session's standing entry is harmless residue bounded by session count — and a `stop` arriving mid-sweep cancels the pending coalesced rerun and suppresses the completing sweep's publication for that session (the stop-vs-in-flight-sweep race). **The suppression is mechanized as a per-session SWEEP GENERATION checked inside the lock callback (Codex PR #282 round 2)** — the registry API takes no cancellation token, and a `halt(sessionId)` already queued behind an append parked in T2.7's asynchronous unseal would otherwise still execute its queued lock callback and publish AFTER the observer stopped: the observer keeps a per-session generation counter, `stop(sessionId)` bumps it synchronously, and the observer publishes by wrapping its own `withSessionAppendLock(sessionId, …)` critical section that re-reads the counter as its FIRST statement under the hold and returns without publishing when the generation it captured at sweep start is stale; the `registry.halt(sessionId)` call it then makes on the live path reuses that same hold through the owner-scoped reentrancy above, so no registry-API cancellation surface is minted and the check is unmissable — it is the only path to publication. T4.2 arms each half, the cancellation arm parking the queued publication behind an unseal, calling `stop`, releasing the park, and asserting the session is NOT halted. The standing-entry rule is process-scoped by design: within one process a torn-down-then-reopened session keeps refusing on the standing entry, while a daemon restart boots the registry empty and admits until the boot enumeration sweep re-derives the halt from the durable key stores — same logical state, opposite gate, both converging on the first sweep; the divergence is exactly the boot-to-first-sweep admission window the delivered-binding scoping (F-006-HALT-11) names as the acknowledged residual. Detection-to-halt lag is inherent to the observer pattern — the Sigstore precedent is detection, not prevention: rows appended before a sweep detects are the verifier's and the alarm's forensic domain; the gate, once wired, guarantees no FURTHER admission from implicated local identities while the collision persists. Un-halting is explicit operator work over the durable record — in V1 that means resolving the collision in the STORES (tearing down the implicated sessions; a successor session mints a fresh key), never suppressing the alarm: no rotation ceremony exists that could make a still-colliding identity trustworthy again. Detection SUBSTRATE pinned at Codex PR #274 round 5 (2026-08-01): the key STORES, not the event stream — the canonical field set carries no `NodeId` and no key material (`Spec-006 §Canonical Serialization Rules`), so the observer compares the daemon-local `daemon_signing_keys` rows and the Plan-001 participant-roster / `daemon_signing_public_keys` roster snapshot for one Ed25519 public key appearing under two distinct `(session_id, node_id)` identities — the control-plane snapshot resolved through a thin caller scoped to `runtimenode.signingkeyroster` consuming the SAME T3.3-declared `DaemonCredentialProvider` (CP-006-13's third consumer, added at Codex PR #278 round 1; Tier-5-dormant behind the same runtime stub assertion, an honest vacuity rather than a coverage gap: the roster gains rows only once the same Plan-018 wiring activates the T4.10 registrar, so until then the daemon-local comparison IS the whole observable set); the `LocalSubscriptionConsumer<EventEnvelope>` subscription TRIGGERS a sweep and never evidences one. LOCAL-IDENTITY SOURCE (Codex PR #278 round 4; re-homed 2026-08-11 by the admission-time decoupling): `daemon_signing_keys` is `session_id`-keyed and carries no `node_id`, so the locally-hosted classification and the `detectorNodeId` stamp both resolve through the ONE durable local node identity the daemon holds — the shipped Plan-003 Phase-2 node registry's `node_trust_state` row (`node_id`-keyed, no session dimension, populated at daemon startup), consumed via a constructor-injected `node-registry.ts` read (the credential-provider injection mirror; the pre-decoupling source — T4.10's delivered attachment binding and its store — was deleted with the delivery machinery): under the one-node-per-daemon V1 topology a roster identity `(S, N)` is locally hosted iff `S` is locally materialized (holds a `daemon_signing_keys` row) and `N` equals the daemon's own registry node id, and the alarm's `detectorNodeId` is that same registry node id. The HALT never awaits that identity: it keys on the daemon's own `daemon_signing_keys` row — a local key whose fingerprint joins a collision implicates this daemon's own append path for that session whatever its node identity — so fail-closed holds in every window WITH RESPECT TO THE NODE-IDENTITY READ (the claim this sentence scopes — F-006-HALT-11's delivered-binding scoping, re-scoped 2026-08-11 onto the registry read): the halt never awaits the registry row. The acknowledged residual is the boot-to-first-sweep admission window — the registry boots empty and is populated only by sweeps, so a collision already durably present in `daemon_signing_keys` admits appends until the composition root's boot enumeration starts the session's observer and its first sweep completes; bounded by boot-enumeration-plus-sweep latency, and inherent to the observer pattern the 2026-08-01 delta adjudicated, not introduced by the ingest-halt seam. EMISSION in the pre-identity window (a sweep firing before the daemon's startup node registration lands its `node_trust_state` row) is contract-blocked twice over — the `Spec-006 §Audit Integrity (audit_integrity)` payload requires `detectorNodeId`, and the alarm's node-scope sentinel binding needs the same identity — so the sweep DEFERS the append (never the halt) and logs the deferral (the registrar backoff-logging mirror); with the delivery hook deleted (2026-08-11 — its F-006-HALT-02 hook home died with the `event.deliverAttachmentId` handler and the machinery around it), the RECONCILIATION TICK below is the deferred alarm's re-trigger, and the window it covers is daemon-startup-ordering-narrow rather than renderer-delivery-wide: the registry row lands at daemon boot with no renderer, shell, or control-plane party in the path, so the deferral resolves within one tick interval of startup, and the round-2 strictly-larger-set re-emission rule already tolerates identity knowledge arriving late. Tests verify the both-identities halt (a synthetic collision under two locally-hosted `(session_id, node_id)` identities halts BOTH, first-seen included, and the emitted payload's `observedIdentities` carries every pair — a remote identity is enumerated in the payload yet contributes no local halt entry, the round-3 scoping), halt persistence while the collision persists in the stores (a restarted observer's sweep-at-start re-detects and re-halts; un-halt = the collision leaving the observable set via teardown, never alarm suppression), the roster-leg dormancy (stubbed provider → daemon-local-only sweep, no attempt against the roster procedure), and the third-identity-after-first-alarm re-emission (a collision alarmed under identities {A, B} later observed under a third identity C emits a SECOND `key_reuse_detected` whose `observedIdentities` carries the complete {A, B, C} set and halts C too, while a re-sweep observing an unchanged set stays deduped — Codex PR #278 round 2), the production-seam classification case (round 4; re-homed 2026-08-11: the observer over a REAL `node-registry.ts` read against a real `node_trust_state` row, never a mocked source — a collision spanning the registry-identified local identity and a roster-only remote identity halts the local one, enumerates both in `observedIdentities`, and stamps `detectorNodeId` from the registry read), and the pre-identity deferral case (round 4, re-derived 2026-08-11: no `node_trust_state` row yet — the sweep halts the implicated local session's append path, defers the alarm append with the deferral logged, and the reconciliation tick's next evaluation after the startup registration lands the row dispatches the sweep that emits the complete-set alarm — asserted with NO intervening session event between the registration and the emission, the halted-single-session-daemon regression control (round 3)). Halt observability rides the T3.1 seam: the halt assertions read the registry's `isHalted(sessionId)`, and an end-to-end arm drives `append()` through a registry-wired `EventLogService` whose `IngestHaltRegistry` the observer populates, asserting the typed `daemon.ingest_halted` refusal (the production-wiring counterpart of T3.5's fixture-level gate tests), and a post-start-session arm: a session opened AFTER the composition root has run is picked up by the lifecycle binding — its observer starts, sweeps, and on a planted collision halts it — never started by hand in the test (the silently-unobserved regression control), and an overlapping-sweeps arm (round 3): two concurrent triggers on one session serialize into sweep-then-coalesced-rerun — with the collision planted between the first sweep's snapshot and the second trigger, the final published state is halted and `append()` still refuses after both settle (the stale-`clear`-reopens-ingest regression control) — while triggers on distinct sessions sweep concurrently (single-flight is per-session). Depends: Phase 1 `EventEnvelope` + `NodeId`; Phase 2 Ed25519 fingerprint; T3.1 (`IngestHaltRegistry` — the halt-state carrier this observer populates; `IngestHaltSource` stays the registry's read interface, which the observer never implements); T3.3 (`DaemonCredentialProvider`); the shipped Plan-003 Phase-2 node registry — `node-registry.ts` over `node_trust_state` — as the observer's durable local-identity source (round 4's delivered-binding source re-homed 2026-08-11 by the admission-time decoupling; read-only consumption of a Tier-2-shipped surface, dissolving the phase's former T4.10 → T4.2 intra-phase edge — no Phase 4 task now depends on T4.10); Plan-007 `LocalSubscriptionConsumer<EventEnvelope>`; Plan-001 participant-roster snapshot. IdempotencyClass: `compensable` per dedupe-by-`(offendingKeyFingerprint, detectorNodeId, normalized observedIdentities set)` — the identity set joined the key at Codex PR #278 round 2, because the round-1 both-identities halt made the alarm's duty grow with the set: under register-once semantics the observed set only ever grows, a sweep observing a STRICTLY LARGER set re-emits carrying the complete current set — so the LATEST durable record always enumerates every halted identity and the operator un-halt workflow reads one event, never a reconstruction — and a sweep re-observing the same set is deduped.

- **Spec coverage:** Spec-006 §Audit Integrity (audit_integrity) (key_reuse_detected)
- **Verifies invariant:** I-006-4-03 (refuse_on_rotation — halts ingest from every locally-hosted `(session_id, node_id)` identity the colliding key appears under, never first-wins)

##### T4.3 — `replay-service.ts` for `EventReadAfterCursor` + `EventReadWindow`

Files: `packages/runtime-daemon/src/events/replay-service.ts` (NEW) + test; `packages/contracts/src/session.ts` (EXTEND — Plan-001-owned, Plan-006 extends per the [§2 shared-file ownership rule](../architecture/cross-plan-dependencies.md#ownership-rule)): the `encodeEventCursor` / `decodeEventCursor` pair beside `EventCursorSchema`, and the `SessionReadResponseSchema.timelineCursors.earliest` optional-field widening, each with contracts-test coverage; `packages/contracts/src/error.ts` (EXTEND — registers the typed `CURSOR_UNRESOLVABLE` error / dotted `event.cursor_unresolvable`, mirrored in [error-contracts.md §Event](../architecture/contracts/error-contracts.md#event)) + its contracts test; `packages/runtime-daemon/src/ipc/handlers/session-read.ts` (EXTEND — the `session.read` materializer populates `timelineCursors.earliest`) + its handler test. Provides: `readAfterCursor(params): Promise<EventReadAfterCursorResponse>` returning `{events, nextCursor, hasMore}`; `readWindow(params): Promise<EventReadWindowResponse>` bounded by `[fromSequence, toSequence]`; events from compacted regions returned as audit-stub envelopes with `payload.retentionClass === 'audit_stub'` (I-006-4-04 — never silent omission). **Cursor encoding (LOCKED — predecessor-position model): an `EventCursor` is a log POSITION**, an integer ≥ -1 rendered as a decimal string under the existing opaque `EventCursor` brand — clients keep treating it as opaque. Position `-1` is start-of-log (before the first row); a position ≥ 0 is an event's `sequence` (`EventEnvelope.sequence` stays nonnegative). The daemon-side `encodeEventCursor(position: number): EventCursor` / `decodeEventCursor(cursor: EventCursor): number` pair (exported from `packages/contracts/src/session.ts` beside `EventCursorSchema`) has domain **integer ≥ -1**: `decodeEventCursor` raises the typed `CURSOR_UNRESOLVABLE` error (registered in `packages/contracts/src/error.ts`, the contracts error-taxonomy pattern, e.g. `VERSION_FLOOR_EXCEEDED_CODE`) for a non-integer or a value `< -1` (a legacy SDK-synthesized UUID, a corrupted value), never a silent parse and never a dual-format fallback decoder. Cursor semantics: a cursor denotes a log position, and `readAfterCursor` scans strict `sequence > decodeEventCursor(afterCursor)` in ascending order; the `afterCursor` parameter is **optional** — present ⇒ scan after that position; **absent ≡ the start-of-log position `-1`**, so a cursorless read returns the full surviving range, matching SSE first-connect semantics exactly (no `Last-Event-ID` header ⇒ replay from the start). `hasMore` is `true` exactly when the page cap leaves rows still beyond `nextCursor`, and `nextCursor` is `encodeEventCursor` of the last returned event's sequence (an empty page returns the input cursor unchanged with `hasMore: false`; a cursorless call's effective input is the start-of-log position `-1`, so an empty cursorless page returns `encodeEventCursor(-1)` — `nextCursor` is wire-required and is never absent or invented). Compacted ranges are gap-inverted — a compacted span is never a hole in the sequence scan; each compacted position surfaces its audit-stub envelope at that exact sequence, so the cursor advances monotonically across compacted ranges and a reader can never step past a compacted region without observing it (the I-006-4-04 no-silent-omission guarantee at the cursor layer). SSE resumption: the `Last-Event-ID` header value is `encodeEventCursor(envelope.sequence)`, derived from each delivered envelope's required `sequence` field (`EventEnvelope.sequence`, `packages/contracts/src/event.ts` — always present, so `LocalSubscriptionProducer<EventEnvelope>` already carries it and no notification-schema change is needed; T4.7 retires the old UUID synthesis) — a reconnecting subscriber resumes exactly after its last-delivered sequence, replaying any compacted-as-stub positions it had not yet seen. Also populates `timelineCursors.earliest?: EventCursor` — **the position immediately BEFORE the oldest surviving row**, hence a directly resumable cursor (the `SessionReadResponseSchema` widening + `session.read` materializer are in Files; architecture mirror in [api-payload-contracts.md](../architecture/contracts/api-payload-contracts.md)): a V1-constant `encodeEventCursor(-1)` because compaction rewrites rows to audit stubs in place but never deletes them — a future retention pass that deletes through `sequence` N-1 moves it to `encodeEventCursor(N-1)`, the position before the new floor row N. Because `earliest` is a real position, `readAfterCursor(earliest)` scans `sequence > -1` and returns the full surviving range with no off-by-one — no separate cursorless-floor primitive is needed. Consumer rule: **resume from `acknowledged ?? earliest`** — with no acknowledged cursor, resume from `earliest`; the gap predicate `decodeEventCursor(acknowledged) < decodeEventCursor(earliest)` ⇒ the acknowledged position fell below the surviving floor ⇒ events were lost ⇒ reset the projection and resume from `earliest` (a clean floor `earliest = encodeEventCursor(N-1)` against `acknowledged = encodeEventCursor(N-1)` gives `N-1 < N-1` = false, so no false resync). Three failure modes converge on that one reset-and-resume-from-`earliest` path: a `CURSOR_UNRESOLVABLE` decode (legacy or corrupted cursor), the gap predicate above, and the live-stream sequence-gap detection (T4.7). Rationale: pre-1.0, no shipped external client holds a durable cursor (the UUID synthesis is in-repo only, retired here), so the format is fixed pre-release. `earliest` is **optional** for version skew: a new daemon ALWAYS populates it, but an **absent** `earliest` ⇒ the responder predates the field, so the client cannot gap-detect and conservatively resumes from start (the `afterCursor`-omitted read, the same safe full-replay); it becomes required at the next MAJOR envelope version per the `ADR-018` additive-MINOR-now versioning ladder. **[Non-normative cross-reference, campaign B12 / W2.5 (2026-07-21): the normative home of the absent-`earliest` consumer disposition is [Plan-008 I-008-12(d)](./008-control-plane-relay-and-session-join.md#i-008-12--client-event-stream-resume-is-floor-checked-gap-safe-and-at-most-once-applied), merged with the B12 bundle — the sole consumer discovers the absence from the `session.read` response itself and refuses the resume cycle SDK-locally before any projection reset or subsequent replay/subscribe wire call, because a responder absent `earliest` predates this very read surface (T4.3/T4.5/T4.7 land together) and the cursorless read named here would method-not-found. This annotation records that supersession without re-opening any Plan-006 behavior: the producer surface and the version-ladder record above stand unchanged (the B13 one-clause doc-only reciprocal precedent).]** Depends: T4.6 Zod schemas; Phase 1 contracts; Phase 3 T3.2 compactor format; Plan-001 daemon-migration substrate; `ADR-018 §Decision` #6 + #9 (upcaster chain on read; unknown-MAJOR accept-and-stub). IdempotencyClass: `idempotent` (pure read).

- **Spec coverage:** Spec-006 §Interfaces And Contracts (EventReadAfterCursor; EventReadWindow), Spec-006 §Fallback Behavior (audit-visible stub), Spec-006 §Replay Interaction with Compacted Regions (replay cursor tracks compacted ranges)
- **Verifies invariant:** I-006-4-04 (replay across compacted regions returns audit stubs, never silent omission — reinforced at the cursor layer by gap-inverted monotonic advance)

resolve-then-attempt converges — the delivery-precondition cases died with the machinery), the restart case (a fresh registrar over a database carrying a provisioned key re-registers via `readPublicKey` with same-key idempotence — the durable path exercised, not mocked), the suspended-member refusal-then-converge after `reactivate` (round 4), `NOT_FOUND`-vs-typed-code discrimination on the `data.aisError` envelope, conflict surfacing as the durable `signing_key_slot_conflict` `audit_integrity_failed` append (round 5), the registrar-built event round-tripping the discriminated payload schema — the registrar arm carrying `failureMode`/`failurePath`/`detail` and none of the Merkle triple (2026-08-01 PR #276 follow-up), and the below-floor park cases re-derived for the daemon-local release path: the floor refusal parks the loop with no backoff retry, a registrar restarted with a higher self-declared `clientVersion` (the upgrade-restart fixture) converges to registered, and a same-version restart re-parks — asserted as its own negative case). Provides: the NodeId-keyed public-key resolution surface [security-architecture.md §Per-Event Daemon Signature](../architecture/security-architecture.md#per-event-daemon-signature) describes ("registered in the session participant roster at join time, keyed by `NodeId`" — a contract this task now meets literally: registration keys on membership admission, the 2026-08-11 decoupling) — the surface that makes uploaded session-scope anchors independently verifiable (CP-006-7 leg B), erasure-durable by construction (no participant FK, so outside the Spec-022 §Shred Fan-Out Path-2 `REFERENCES participants(id)` closure by that spec's own scope rule), and the roster prerequisite [Spec-008 §Peer History Backfill On Join (V1)](../specs/008-control-plane-relay-and-session-join.md#peer-history-backfill-on-join-v1) verify-before-append consumes — every admitted member's daemon is rostered from admission, so a backfill origin resolves a key wherever its member was ever admitted, and the unrostered-origin honesty arm narrows to genuinely never-admitted or pre-upgrade origins. Depends: T2.7 (the registered key is `create(sessionId)`'s returned public key; this task extends T2.7's own `DaemonSigningKeyProvisioner` with the public-only read); T3.1 (round 5, 2026-08-01 — the durable conflict alarm appends through `EventLogService.append`, Phase 3's sole `session_events` write path, injected into the registrar by the same composition root that starts it; Phase 3 precedes Phase 4 in the phase sequence, so the seam exists before any T4.10 dispatch); Plan-003 shipped Phase 3/5 surfaces (`runtime-node-router.factory.ts` shared builder, `runtimeNodeClient`) — consumed via sanctioned seam edits, no re-authoring — plus the shipped Phase-2 node registry (`node-registry.ts` / `node_trust_state`, the registrar's injected node-identity read — read-only consumption of a Tier-2-shipped surface, no build-order edge). Deliberately NOT T4.2: the `refuse_on_rotation` mirroring is posture-level only — the two refusals implement the same spec posture on disjoint surfaces (T4.2's daemon-side ingest observer vs this task's control-plane registration service) and share no code, no types, and no wire contract (the control-plane package cannot import the daemon observer), so the posture-mirroring creates no build-order edge — and the round-4 SUBSTRATE edge is DISSOLVED by the 2026-08-11 decoupling: T4.2's durable local-identity source is now the same shipped Plan-003 node registry this task's registrar reads (its delivered-binding source died with the delivery machinery), so no Phase 4 task depends on T4.10. T4.9's terminal integration suite is likewise unaffected — it drives `eventClient`, not `runtimeNodeClient`. IdempotencyClass: `retry_safe` (register-once semantics; a same-key replay is a no-op, a different-key replay is refused).

- **Spec coverage:** Spec-006 §Canonical Serialization Rules (the daemon public key is the NodeId-resolved public key from the session participant roster — this task ships the roster's registration store and resolution query), Spec-006 §Daemon-Scope Event Binding And Node-Scope Anchoring (the session-scope/roster-resolution side of the verification-inverse rule), Spec-008 §Peer History Backfill On Join (V1) (the admission-time signing-key registration decoupling — registration keyed on membership admission with a producer binding that requires no attachment row, landed 2026-08-11 so a never-attached member's authored history resolves a roster key), Spec-022 §Shred Fan-Out (the Path-2 `REFERENCES participants(id)` closure the erasure-durable key store deliberately sits outside), ADR-018 §Decision (#7 bidirectional MINOR compatibility — additive procedures leave every shipped `.strict()` wire shape byte-identical in both skew directions; #8 additive-only MINOR)
- **Verifies invariant:** I-006-4-08 (register-once + different-key typed refusal at the control-plane roster — the mirror of I-006-4-03's daemon-side `refuse_on_rotation`, minted at PR #274 round 3: T4.2 declares I-006-4-03 for the same posture, so the earlier bold none-arm left one of the posture's two enforcement surfaces invariant-less — an asymmetry the readiness-audit delta would have had to adjudicate anyway), I-006-4-09 (the collapsed authorization refusal + the two attempt preconditions stay conjunctive — minted by the 2026-08-01 delta at the surfaces where a dropped predicate is silent; predicate set re-derived by the 2026-08-11 admission-time decoupling)

## Open Authoring Decisions (Category 2 — Audit-Surfaced)

These are decisions the spec or prior plan iterations did not settle, surfaced by the 2026-05-28 Plan-006 audit. Load-bearing decisions are ratified inline; all others are resolved in-PR with hardened-mode defaults.

- **Event contracts directory placement.** Resolved: Extend `packages/contracts/src/event.ts` top-level per Plan-001's contracts package layout (extend the canonical file; do not fork a sibling). The `events/` subdirectory referenced by prior plan iterations does not exist; no other plan adopts a subdirectory pattern.
- **RFC 8785 lex-sort vs `Spec-006 §Canonical Serialization Rules` listed order.** Resolved: RFC 8785 §3.2.3 UTF-16 code-unit lex-sort is non-negotiable (the canonical-bytes invariant depends on it). `Spec-006 §Canonical Serialization Rules` lists field MEMBERSHIP, not serialized order — amended in this audit PR ("Fields included (the canonical set; serialized order is mandated by RFC 8785 §3.2.3 UTF-16 code-unit lex-sort of member names, not the order listed here)"). T2.3 golden vectors assert the lex-sorted output as ground truth.
- **Daemon Ed25519 signing-key custody.** Resolved (user-ratified 2026-05-28): Plan-006 T2.7 self-contains. Phase 2 ships `DaemonSigningKeySource` interface + `OsKeystoreSealedDaemonSigningKeySource` implementation; per-session Ed25519 sealed via OS-keystore master key (`@napi-rs/keyring` v1.2.0 per [Spec-022 §Daemon Master Key](../specs/022-data-retention-and-gdpr.md#daemon-master-key) — Keychain/libsecret/DPAPI; supersedes prior `keytar` reference); stored as ciphertext in new local `daemon_signing_keys` SQLite table per ADR-004 SQLite-local-state boundary (post-Codex-T4-review redesign 2026-05-28; supersedes the pre-review draft that mis-located the column on shared-Postgres `sessions`). Plan-002 amendment-via-extension adds session-create call-site + roster public-key registration per CP-006-7. No Plan-022 (Tier 5) dependency → no tier inversion. **Superseded in part 2026-07-27 (PR #256), on this ratification's own terms rather than against them.** T2.7 as shipped splits the seal out as an injected `DaemonSigningKeySealer` and carries no keystore code, because no document specifies the `daemon_signing_keys.sealed_private_key` byte format — `Spec-022 §Daemon Master Key` covers the MASTER key's custody and Plan-022 covers the `participant_keys.encrypted_key_blob` wrap, neither this column — and inventing a third format here would pre-commit every later reader of it, including a master-key-rotation re-wrap, on a guess. So the design DOES now depend on Plan-022 (Tier 5) for both the implementation and the format, registered as CP-006-11. **The no-tier-inversion conclusion is unchanged; only its premise moves** — it rests on the injected interface (this module imports nothing from Plan-022 and pulls no native keyring binding into the append path's consumers) rather than on the absence of a dependency. The 2026-05-28 ratification stands as the record of what was decided then. **A second clause is superseded 2026-07-29: the "Plan-002 amendment-via-extension" sentence recorded the wrong vehicle and the wrong lifecycle point** — Plan-002 owns no session-create surface, and roster registration happens at attach (join time) per [security-architecture.md §Per-Event Daemon Signature](../architecture/security-architecture.md#per-event-daemon-signature); see CP-006-7's re-vehicled two-leg form (composition-root call-site, leg A; T4.10 post-attach registration write, leg B). The custody design itself — what was actually ratified — is unchanged.
- **JCS library: vendor vs in-house.** Resolved: Vendor — `canonicalize` package (Erdtman's RFC 8785 reference implementation; ships with the RFC). T2.3 lifts the RFC 8785 Appendix B Table 1 number-serialization vectors verbatim as a sub-suite to catch version-drift bugs. In-house implementation rejected per ECMA-262 ToString edge-case risk.
- **Golden-vector storage: inline hex vs JSON fixture.** Resolved: Inline hex strings in the test file. Matches crypto-paseto convention (the `packages/crypto-paseto/src/internal/v4-local-deterministic.ts#encryptV4LocalDeterministic` golden-token tests in `packages/crypto-paseto/src/__tests__/v4-local.test.ts`); PR-diff is the audit trail for byte-stable artifacts.
- **`pii_ciphertext_digest` position + wire shape.** Resolved: Top-level field on `payload` (matches `Spec-006 §Canonical Serialization Rules` + `Spec-022 §Argument` literal reading); wire shape is **lowercase hex 64-char string** (matches BLAKE3 reference output convention; T2.3 golden vectors are reviewer-friendly in hex).
- **`participantSignature` minting boundary.** Resolved: Phase 2 signer exports both `mintParticipantSignature(...)` (write-side helper accepting participant private key) and `verifyParticipantSignature(...)` (read-side); the WHICH-events-are-sensitive enum lives in `packages/contracts/src/event.ts` and is owned by **T4.6** (reassigned 2026-07-26 — the original "Phase 1 extension" wording named a phase that shipped closed without the enum; T4.6's row carries the taxonomy-derivation obligation the enum requires); the WHEN-to-mint decision is Plan-002 / Plan-022 territory.
- **Partition-anchor queue durability.** Resolved: Design A — new SQLite table `pending_anchor_uploads` with columns mirroring `AnchorPayload` plus `attempt_count`, `last_attempt_at`, `last_error`; `UNIQUE(session_id, node_id, start_sequence, end_sequence)` constraint. Survives daemon restart; queryable for operator triage; storage footprint bounded at ≤ 1 anchor per 300s per session. Design B (reconstruct from Postgres deltas) fails the no-re-signing constraint; Design C (in-memory) fails durability.
- **Audit-stub representation: column add vs payload discriminator.** Resolved: Design B — new `session_events.retention_class TEXT` column. Partial index `WHERE retention_class IS NULL` keeps hot-path replay queries fast; explicit column makes stub state visible at row inspection; three-layer enforcement easier with typed column than JSON-field probe.
- **Migration-runner integration: callback vs polling.** Resolved: Hybrid — callback seam on the runner (primary path; immediate emission via `onBatchFinish(handler)`) + startup reconciliation fallback (`MAX(sequence) WHERE type = 'schema.migrated'` gap fill, guarding against crash between commit and emit). Callback amendment to Plan-001 migration-runner per cross-plan-deps.md §2 housekeeping exception (Plan-006 may fix-in-place since the change adds a callback seam without altering semantics).
- **`session_snapshots` extension scope.** Resolved: Reading (a) — existing `as_of_sequence` already carries cursor-state semantics per `docs/architecture/schemas/local-sqlite-schema.md §Session Snapshots (Plan-001, extended by Plans 006, 015)`; T4.4 adds ONLY the compaction-incidence flag columns `has_compacted_ranges` + `compacted_range_count`. Plan-015 owns the separate `replay_cursors` table for projector resumption; the two surfaces answer different questions.
- **`EventSubscription` backpressure model.** Resolved: Phase 4 implementer reads Plan-007 CP-007-4 `LocalSubscriptionProducer<T>` contract first; if Plan-007 ratifies a backpressure model there, T4.5 follows. If Plan-007 CP-007-4 is silent at T4.5 dispatch time, surface as a Plan-007 ratification before T4.5 lands. **Phase 4 owes pre-dispatch verification.**
- **`summary` field max-length and generation rule.** Resolved: Deferred to Phase 3 T3.2 (`compactor.ts` is the emission site). T4.8's `<CompactedStubSegment>` MUST handle arbitrary lengths gracefully (CSS truncation + tooltip-on-hover); renderer-side resilience does not gate Phase 5's emission rule.
- **Live-stream gap detection mechanism.** Resolved: Sequence-number monotonicity check in the SDK's `EventClient.subscribe` (Option (b)) as canonical gap-detection; SSE reconnect via `Last-Event-ID` header (Option (a)) as reconnect path. Heartbeat-with-cursor (Option (c)) deferred unless Plan-008-bootstrap heartbeat substrate adds it natively.

## Cross-Plan Obligations

Each obligation gets a CP-006-N ID. The Cross-Plan Dependency Graph entry for Plan-006 ([cross-plan-dependencies.md §3 Inter-Plan Dependency Graph](../architecture/cross-plan-dependencies.md#3-inter-plan-dependency-graph), the Plan-006 row) mirrors this list. Two Plan-004-facing seams are body-registered rather than CP-rowed, per the T3.1 `withSessionAppendLock` publication precedent: the T1.9 `sourceEpoch` + `sourcePosition` carrier pair (the CP-004-12 registration — the obligation ledger is Plan-004's row), and the T3.2 `RollbackAttributionSource` injection seam (interface Plan-006-owned; Plan-004 T3.14's supersede projection implements it and wires it at the composition root, vacuous-default until then — the CP-006-1 injection pattern).

- **CP-006-1 — Plan-022 PII codec interface boundary.** Phase 2 T2.4's `pii-indirection.ts` consumes `PiiEncryptor` as an interface; Plan-022 (Tier 5) ships the AES-256-GCM + HKDF-SHA256 + XChaCha20-Poly1305 implementation. Phase 2 ships interface + test-only stub; composition root (Plan-001 / Plan-003 bootstrap) MUST inject the real codec — never the stub — and ships a runtime assertion against the stub outside tests.
- **CP-006-2 — Anchor-router mount on Plan-008-bootstrap.** Phase 3 T3.3 ships `packages/control-plane/src/event-anchors/anchor-router.ts` as a tRPC procedure; mount point is Plan-008-bootstrap `host.ts` per cross-plan-deps.md §2. Plan-006 extends; Plan-008 substrate owns.
- **CP-006-3 — RFC 8785 JCS shared with Spec-024 dispatch.** Spec-024 §request_body_hash computes a BLAKE3 digest over RFC-8785-canonicalized JSON. Phase 2 T2.1's `canonicalizeEvent` + `canonicalizeJson` are the workspace's single source of truth for RFC 8785; Plan-027 (Spec-024 implementer) MUST consume — never re-implement. **The shared entry point is BOUNDED, not unlimited. It carries THREE refusals, of which exactly TWO bind Plan-027 under this obligation — the two counts are different numbers and must never be conflated — and those two differ in KIND:** (i) the **policy ceiling** — `canonicalizeJson` refuses any value nesting containers past a fixed ceiling (`CANONICAL_JSON_MAX_DEPTH`, 64 as T2.1 ships it), a stack-overflow guard, since the pinned `canonicalize@3.0.0` recurses once per nesting level with no guard of its own and this entry point is where untrusted dispatch bodies arrive; and (ii) the **RFC-mandated refusal** — `canonicalizeJson` refuses any string value or property name carrying an unpaired UTF-16 surrogate, because RFC 8785 §3.2.2.2 makes terminating on such data a normative MUST while the pinned `canonicalize@3.0.0` escapes it through `JSON.stringify` and keeps going, emitting bytes no conforming verifier will ever reproduce. (i) is checked first, and that order is a correctness constraint rather than a diagnostic preference — the depth walk is what makes the well-formedness walk terminate on cyclic input — so a body defective both ways reports the over-deep refusal and never the other. Three consequences for Plan-027, one deliverable each: (1) a **registered rejection reason** for an over-deep `action_payload`; (2) a **distinct** registered rejection reason for an ill-formed one — (1) and (2) MUST NOT collapse into a single reason, an over-deep payload and an ill-formed one being different client errors whose merger destroys the diagnostic (both refusals are plain throws at T2.1, so both are caught and mapped on the intake path, never surfaced raw to a remote caller); and (3) renegotiation of the **depth ceiling ONLY** — a ceiling that proves too tight for a real capability payload is **renegotiated with T2.1**, raising the constant being a two-way door, and forking the canonicalizer to escape it is the CP-006-3 violation this obligation exists to prevent. **(3) does not extend to (ii).** The well-formedness refusal is a normative MUST-terminate — neither renegotiable nor raisable, and not T2.1's policy to relax in either direction, since asking for it is asking Plan-006 to violate RFC 8785 — so catch-and-map per (2) is Plan-027's only available response there. **The entry point's THIRD refusal adds no fourth obligation, and the reason is load-bearing rather than a concession.** `assertNoToJsonOverride` (T2.1, 2026-07-27) refuses any value carrying a callable `toJSON` at any depth, and its KIND is a third one besides POLICY and RFC-MUST: it enforces the DETERMINISM the integrity protocol assumes, a `toJSON` being arbitrary caller code that need not return at verification time what it returned at signing time. It binds nothing HERE because it cannot fire here: Spec-024 bodies reach `canonicalizeJson` through `JSON.parse`, whose output can carry no function-valued member — a wire `{"toJSON":"x"}` parses to a STRING, and `"__proto__"` parses as an ordinary own data property rather than a prototype mutation — so the predicate is unsatisfiable on parsed input and no dispatch body can trip it. **That argument is scoped to the PARSED-INTAKE path and is not a claim about every Spec-024 call site.** A sender-side `request_body_hash` computed over a body Plan-027 CONSTRUCTS in process rather than parses (an object literal carrying a `Date`, a `Buffer`, or any class instance with a `toJSON`) is outside the argument's reach, and would meet a live refusal. Nothing in this obligation covers that case today; whether it needs a fourth reciprocal is a question for the Spec-024 intake-surface census, to be re-derived against T2.1 rather than assumed either way.
- **CP-006-4 — `event.*` JSON-RPC namespace registration with Plan-007.** Phase 4 T4.7 registers `event.readAfterCursor`, `event.readWindow`, `event.subscribe` under Plan-007's namespace registry per Plan-007 I-007-9. Plan-007 Phase 4 (Tier 4) MUST accept these three method-name registrations; without them, the SDK seam fails to register at daemon startup. The FOURTH registration the 2026-08-01 delta added under the same namespace — T4.10's `event.deliverAttachmentId` delivery method with its `event.ts` contracts pair and `ipc/handlers/` binder — was RETRACTED 2026-08-11 by the admission-time registration decoupling (the Spec-008-pinned leg, landed with the Plan-008/Spec-008 restoring targeted readiness-audit delta, cross-plan §6 node NS-58): the registrar's node identity resolves from the daemon's own shipped Plan-003 node registry, no renderer delivery exists, and the `event.*` registration set returns to the three T4.7 names. Reciprocal entry on Plan-007's side per CP-007-7, its fourth-name mention retracted in the same swap.
- **CP-006-5 — `CapabilityDetails` + `providerFailureDetail` carry-forward with Plan-005 (CLOSES CP-005-5).** Phase 1 T1.4 binds typed `CapabilityDetails` interface for `runtime_node.capability_*` event payloads; Phase 3 audit-mirror adds `providerFailureDetail?: string` to `run.failed` payload (`RunStateChangeEvent` in `docs/architecture/contracts/api-payload-contracts.md §Plan-004 — Queue Steer Pause Resume`). Both are ADR-018-compliant additive-only MINOR additions. **Discharge artifacts applied in this audit PR.** **Carry-forward (Plan-003 cross-plan amendment 2026-06-02, PR #137):** the `runtime_node.capability_declared` / `capability_updated` payload-shape Zod schemas are **authored by Plan-003 Phase 2** in `packages/contracts/src/runtime-node.ts` (CREATE), shipping `capabilityDetails` / `previousState` / `newState` as interim-opaque `z.record(z.string(), z.unknown())`. Phase 1 T1.4 therefore **EXTENDs** those existing schemas — it lands CP-003-1's leg (c), binding the canonical `CapabilityDetails` wrapper over the interim-opaque field as the canonical-first arm of a tolerant union, while legs (a) registration into the discriminated `SessionEventSchema` union (`event.ts`) and (b) `EventEnvelope` wrapping ride the later Tier-4 legs; it does **not** re-author the payload shapes from scratch. See Plan-003 §CP-003-1 (Payload-shape ownership) and [cross-plan-dependencies.md §3 Plan-003 row](../architecture/cross-plan-dependencies.md#3-inter-plan-dependency-graph).
- **CP-006-6 — Plan-001 forward-declared schema is immutable.** Plan-006 Phase 3 reads columns shipped by Plan-001 in `0001-initial.ts` but never re-shapes them. Plan-001 `I-001-3` enforces this. New tables + columns Plan-006 needs (`daemon_signing_keys` table, `session_events.retention_class`, `session_snapshots.{has_compacted_ranges, compacted_range_count}`, `pending_anchor_uploads` table) ship as Plan-006-owned additive migrations at Tier 4, never as modifications to `0001-initial.ts`.
- **CP-006-7 — daemon signing-key provisioning + admission-time roster public-key registration (re-vehicled 2026-07-29; decoupled from attach 2026-08-11 by the Spec-008-pinned admission-time leg; formerly "Plan-002 amendment for daemon_signing_key generation at session-create").** The original framing was a double misattribution, corrected here on primary sources rather than renumbered: Plan-002 (Tier 2, invites/membership/presence) owns no session-create surface on any reading of its file, and session-create is the wrong sole lifecycle point — in a multi-node session (Plan-003 T4.3 attaches two nodes to one live session), a subsequent daemon's local session-materialization happens at ATTACH on a machine where session-create never ran. [security-architecture.md §Per-Event Daemon Signature](../architecture/security-architecture.md#per-event-daemon-signature) has named the correct point all along: the public key is registered in the session participant roster **at join time**, keyed by `NodeId`. The obligation therefore decomposes into two legs, each with a real owner. **Leg A — local provisioning (call-site).** `DaemonSigningKeyProvisioner.create(sessionId)` (T2.7's PUBLIC-key-only half) fires at the daemon's local session-establishment: session-create on the originating daemon, attach-side materialization on a subsequent one. No production call-site exists today because no production composition root wires the durable append path (Plan-007 ships handler shells; the daemon `bootstrap/` tree is Spec-027 secure-defaults only) — the same vacuous-safety argument T3.1's discharged precondition records. The obligation binds at the FIRST production composition root that wires T3.1's append path, in the CP-006-1 assertion-pattern shape (body-registered provide-forward: the wiring PR ships the provisioning call-site plus a runtime assertion that production construction provisions before append), with T3.1's null-key fail-closed throw as the backstop that makes an omission loud rather than silent. **Leg B — roster registration (code leg, T4.10 — registered by this amendment; carrier re-designed in the same PR's review round).** The control plane is the join-time carrier: the daemon registers its session-scoped PUBLIC key (T2.7 `create`'s return, or the `readPublicKey` re-read T4.10 adds for the restart/upgrade path) through a new idempotent procedure — the registrar starts at the daemon-local session-establishment moment and the registration LANDS at that same admission-time moment — the 2026-08-11 decoupling deleted the attach-artifact gate, so a never-attached member's daemon registers too (the round-3 trigger re-anchor recorded at T4.10, completed by the decoupling) — `runtimenode.signingkeyregister` — persisting into the new Plan-006-owned `daemon_signing_public_keys` table (`PRIMARY KEY (session_id, node_id)`), with `runtimenode.signingkeyroster` as the `NodeId`-keyed resolution query; reciprocal CP-003-5 sanctions the seam edits this takes on Plan-003-owned surfaces (the router-factory procedure-key registrations and the `runtimeNodeClient` pass-throughs — the §2 Plan-024 `leaseupdate`/`lease-projection-service.ts` precedent; the service file and the migration are Plan-006-owned siblings). Two prior carrier designs were rejected in this amendment's own review round, each on primary sources: an additive optional field on `RuntimeNodeAttachRequest` (every shipped request/response schema in `runtime-node.ts` is `.strict()`, so the field breaks the new-daemon→old-control-plane skew direction at parse — violating ADR-018 §Decision #7 bidirectional-MINOR with an opaque strict-parse rejection instead of the §Decision #10 typed version errors — and no pre-attach discovery surface exists to gate it), and a nullable column on `runtime_node_attachments` (Spec-022 §Shred Fan-Out hard-DELETEs that table on participant erasure as operational state, which would destroy the only external key copy exactly where it is most needed — the crypto-shredded `runtime_node.*` stream and the `event_log_anchors` rows are RETAINED post-erasure and still need verification; the dedicated table carries no participant FK and sits outside the Path-2 `REFERENCES participants(id)` closure by that spec's own scope rule — machine-generated key material, no personal data). Registration semantics are pinned: register-once per `(session_id, node_id)`; a same-key replay is an acknowledged no-op (retry-safe re-attach); a registration presenting a DIFFERENT key is refused with a typed error before any row mutation, never silently overwritten — the control-plane mirror of T4.2's `refuse_on_rotation` posture, pinned as I-006-4-08 at round 3, and the concrete backing for security-architecture.md's rotation posture, whose rotation ceremony no document specifies today (ADR-010 covers CLI-identity custody only; until a rotation ceremony is specified, refusal IS the rotation policy). Version-skew posture (ADR-018 §Decision #7): no shipped wire shape changes in either direction — an old daemon simply never calls the new procedures, and a new daemon calling an old control plane receives tRPC `NOT_FOUND` and degrades honestly (emitter-only verifiability until the control plane upgrades), the procedure's absence itself serving as the discovery signal the attach flow lacks. **Resolution semantics.** `Spec-006 §Canonical Serialization Rules` (the daemon public key is the `NodeId`-resolved public key from the session participant roster — served by `runtimenode.signingkeyroster` over `daemon_signing_public_keys`) governs the EXTERNAL verifier; the daemon-local verifier (T4.1) checking rows it authored resolves its own `session_id`-keyed `daemon_signing_keys` store — the degenerate `NodeId` = self arm — since no daemon-local roster mirror exists (`node_capabilities`/`node_trust_state` carry no key column). What leg B buys in V1: session-scope anchors uploaded to `event_log_anchors` become independently verifiable by parties other than the emitting daemon — without the roster row, `root_signature` is verifiable by no one but its author, a gap Spec-006 makes explicit for node-scope anchors and was silent on for session-scope. The §5 V1.1-row forgeable-key objection transfers to leg B only in PART (re-scoped at Codex PR #274 round 5, 2026-08-01): its core — sessionless node-scope chains with no roster to resolve against — does not apply, since a session-scope key is bound to `(session_id, node_id)` through the authenticated admission-time registration (the caller's verified PASETO `sub` must hold active session membership, per the round-4 authorization collapse as re-derived by the 2026-08-11 decoupling — the attachment predicates died with the delivery machinery). But that predicate and the daemon-bound CP-006-13 credential both resolve at PARTICIPANT granularity — `sub` names the OWNER, never the calling daemon, and V1 defines no node-identity credential — so they close cross-participant planting only. Within one participant's own holdings the 2026-08-11 decoupling accepts a named widening: the withdrawn server-minted-`attachmentId` equality had narrowed the cross-node plant to a loud two-call usurpation that first rewrote the victim's attach row, and without it a sibling daemon's slot capture is one honest-looking first call — a residual either way, never a closure, with the [cross-plan-dependencies.md §5](../architecture/cross-plan-dependencies.md#5-canonical-build-order) V1.1 Plan-018/Plan-003 node-identity leg as the closure — and a genuine daemon refused the different-key 409 for its own slot appends the durable `audit_integrity_failed` `failureMode: 'signing_key_slot_conflict'`, so a plant that beats the genuine daemon to the slot still leaves a durable alarm the moment that daemon registers. No master-key crypto leaks across the boundary in either leg: `create` returns the public key only (the T2.7 custody split).
- **CP-006-8 — Plan-013 audit-stub renderer surface (forward-declared).** Phase 4 T4.8 ships `<CompactedStubSegment>` as the audit-stub render contract per `Spec-006 §Compacted Event Format`.
