The future Plan-013 (Timeline Rendering And Replay UI) MUST consume — not re-implement. Bidirectional since 2026-07-20: Plan-013 §Implementation Steps' renderer step consumes `<CompactedStubSegment>`, composing it with the CP-004-13 superseded treatment for compacted superseded rows (this row's earlier does-not-exist-yet gloss predated Plan-013's authoring).
- **CP-006-9 — Plan-015 recovery-dispatcher consumes T4.3 read-after-cursor.** Plan-015 (Tier 7) recovery path calls `eventClient.readAfterCursor` (or the daemon-internal `readAfterCursor` on `packages/runtime-daemon/src/events/replay-service.ts` — full-pathed to disambiguate from Plan-015's own `packages/runtime-daemon/src/replay/replay-service.ts`) to rebuild projections from the last `replay_cursors.last_sequence` checkpoint. T4.6 Zod schemas are the binding interface.
- **CP-006-10 — Plan-020 metrics + dashboard consume audit-integrity events (forward-declared).** Plan-020 (Metrics & Dashboards; Non-Goal of Plan-006 per §Non-Goals) SHOULD consume `audit_integrity_verified`, `audit_integrity_failed`, `key_reuse_detected` for an audit-health dashboard. Binding when Plan-020 is authored.
- **CP-006-11 — Plan-022 daemon master-key custody for TWO seams: the daemon signing-key seal boundary (with the `sealed_private_key` byte format) and, since 2026-08-30, the session-content-key wrap.** Phase 2 T2.7 declares `DaemonSigningKeySealer` (`seal(sessionId, privateKey)` / `unseal(sessionId, sealedPrivateKey)`) as an INJECTED interface and ships no keystore code at all; `@napi-rs/keyring` is deliberately absent from `signing-key-source.ts`'s import graph. Plan-022 (Tier 5) ships the implementation, sealing under the OS-keystore-managed daemon master key whose custody ladder is [Spec-022 §Daemon Master Key](../specs/022-data-retention-and-gdpr.md#daemon-master-key) (`@napi-rs/keyring` v1.2.0 — Keychain `kSecAttrAccessibleWhenUnlockedThisDeviceOnly` on macOS / `CRED_TYPE_GENERIC` `CRED_PERSIST_LOCAL_MACHINE` on Windows / Secret Service via libsecret + kwallet6 + keyutils fallback on Linux; supersedes the prior `keytar` reference — keytar is unmaintained), building on the master-key custody ladder Plan-022 already owns at `packages/runtime-daemon/src/bootstrap/daemon-key-store.ts`. **Plan-022 also SPECIFIES the byte format of `daemon_signing_keys.sealed_private_key`, which no document specifies today:** `Spec-022 §Daemon Master Key` fixes the MASTER key's own custody (the tier ladder, the KEK derivation, the 98-byte envelope) and Plan-022 fixes the `participant_keys.encrypted_key_blob` wrap (XChaCha20-Poly1305, AAD `participant_id || "ais.master-wrap.v1" || key_version`) — neither covers this column, so T2.7 declares the OPERATION and leaves the FORMAT with the implementor rather than pre-committing every later reader of the column, including whatever re-wrap a master-key rotation needs, on a guess. **The `sessionId` AAD binding is requested, not assumed:** the interface passes `sessionId` on both sides so an implementation CAN bind it as associated data — the shape Plan-022's participant wrap already uses — but having fixed no format it cannot claim the binding happens, and an implementation that ignores the argument satisfies the types. `DaemonSigningKeySource.read` derives the public half of whatever `unseal` returns and refuses it unless it matches that row's `daemon_signing_keys.public_key`, so a blob copied between rows surfaces as a refusal naming the row rather than as rows signed under another session's key; that is a backstop, not a substitute, because an AEAD that will not open never produces the key material in the first place. `unseal` is asynchronous because it can block on a human — `Spec-022 §Daemon Master Key` wipes the in-memory master on an idle timer and re-unwraps "via keystore + PRF assertion (desktop) or passphrase prompt (CLI) on next access", so the first `unseal` after an idle wipe can await a WebAuthn ceremony. Composition root MUST inject the real sealer — NEVER a test fake — and ships a runtime assertion against the fake outside tests; both land at Plan-022 Tier 5 with the implementation they guard, Phase 2 shipping only the interface plus test-only fakes. Interface + stub + composition-root-injection shape matches CP-006-1 and reciprocates the same Plan-022 keystore boundary Plan-007 registers as CP-007-8. **No tier inversion:** the dependency runs through an injected interface, so this module imports nothing from Plan-022 and pulls no native binding into the append path's consumers — the property the `daemon_signing_keys` row in [cross-plan-dependencies.md §1 Table Ownership Map](../architecture/cross-plan-dependencies.md#1-table-ownership-map) requires, and the reason the keyring import is kept out: on CI legs where a headless Linux box has no Secret Service, `Spec-023 §Native Keystore` requires the keystore layer to detect the no-keystore case and refuse rather than silently fall back. **The second seam (2026-08-30, the machine-authored prose amendment) is a widening of this obligation rather than a second one.** T3.6's `session-content-key-store.ts` declares `DaemonMasterKeySource` as an INJECTED interface with no production implementation in this package, for the identical reason T2.7 declares `DaemonSigningKeySealer` — the custody ladder is Plan-022's at Tier 5, and importing it here would pull a native keyring binding into the append path's consumers and invert the tier order. The asymmetry with the first seam is deliberate and is the whole reason the second one is cheap: this store FIXES its own envelope format in-module (XChaCha20-Poly1305, 24-byte random nonce, wire `nonce || ciphertext || tag`, `AAD = session_id || "ais.session-content-wrap.v1" || key_version`) rather than leaving it to the implementor, because unlike `sealed_private_key` the format IS specified — [Local SQLite Schema §Session Content Keys](../architecture/schemas/local-sqlite-schema.md#session-content-keys-plan-006) fixes it, mirroring the `participant_keys.encrypted_key_blob` wrap Plan-022 already owns. So this seam owes Plan-022 key MATERIAL only, where the first owes it material AND a format. The reciprocal call runs the other way as well: rotate-on-shred invokes `SessionContentKeyStore.rewrapAll` — synchronous by contract — inside the `BEGIN EXCLUSIVE` that already re-wraps `participant_keys`, since a better-sqlite3 transaction cannot span an `await` and a promise-returning re-wrap would commit around nothing, leaving the crash window in which participant keys sit under the new master while content keys are still under the destroyed one.
- **CP-006-12 — Plan-022 Path 1 must preserve the `pii_payload` ciphertext bytes AND the `pii_participant_id` owner stamp, or amend the binding verification rules in the same change.** I-006-2-11 makes `BLAKE3(pii_payload)` = the signed `pii_ciphertext_digest` an unconditional check over every non-NULL `pii_payload`, in BOTH retention states, with no crypto-shred carve-out. That is correct against the shred mechanism as Plan-022 and Spec-022 specify it today: [Spec-022 §Shred Fan-Out](../specs/022-data-retention-and-gdpr.md#shred-fan-out) Path 1 destroys the participant's `participant_keys` row and overwrites no column, and the [GDPR Manual Erasure Runbook](../operations/gdpr-manual-erasure-runbook.md) issues no `UPDATE` against `session_events` at all — its own Path-1 verification step ("spot-check that a previously-readable `pii_payload` no longer decrypts") is unexecutable unless the ciphertext is still there. **If Plan-022's Tier-5 implementation ever overwrites, zeroes, or NULLs the column** — as defence in depth, or to make `piiPayloadsCleared` a literal count of cleared bytes — then every shredded row's digest check flips to `pii_ciphertext_digest_unbound` and the GDPR path pages on every erasure. Plan-022 therefore owns one of two obligations: preserve the bytes, or land the skip-condition amendment to [Security Architecture §Verification Rules](../architecture/security-architecture.md#verification-rules) rule 2 and to I-006-2-11 in the same change that introduces the overwrite. **The obligation covers a SECOND column as of 2026-07-27.** I-006-2-12 makes the same unconditional, carve-out-free claim about `session_events.pii_participant_id` — the durable PII owner stamp T3.1's additive migration adds — and makes it for a sharper reason than the digest: Path 1 destroys the key, so no decrypt can re-attribute the retained ciphertext afterwards and the stamp becomes the SOLE surviving evidence of whose data the row held. That is precisely what `Spec-022 §Shred Fan-Out`'s own scope selector reads and what the `affectedSessionIds[]` / `piiPayloadsCleared` counters on a retained `event.shredded` row assert, so clearing the stamp during an erasure would destroy the attribution the erasure's compliance evidence cites, and would flip every shredded row to `pii_owner_stamp_unbound`. The obligation is unchanged in KIND — Path 1 already overwrites nothing, so preserving the stamp costs Plan-022 nothing — and this half is registered BEFORE the column ships rather than after, which is the point of writing it now: the digest half had to be written against a corpus that already contradicted itself. **The collision is the point** — an unwritten rule would let the overwrite land silently and leave the ciphertext unbound forever, which is the state this PR found and closed. Registered because the divergence is already latent in the corpus rather than hypothetical: this plan's own Spec-006 census row and §Canonical Serialization Rules bullet BOTH described the bytes as "zeroed" until 2026-07-27, overstating a mechanism Spec-022 owns, and both are corrected in this PR.
- **CP-006-13 — Plan-018 daemon credential provider for the T4.10 registrar (registered at PR #274 round 4, 2026-07-31).** The T4.10 registrar authenticates as the node-owner participant — the daemon's standing posture per [component-architecture-local-daemon.md §Responsibilities](../architecture/component-architecture-local-daemon.md#responsibilities) ("authenticate to the Collaboration Control Plane using PASETO v4 tokens per Spec-008"), so no custody contract is violated: Spec-023's auth-material exclusivity clause binds shell-vs-renderer, is desktop-only, and expressly cedes daemon internals to that component doc — presenting a PASETO v4.public access token with a per-request DPoP proof signed by a daemon-held key the token's `cnf.jkt` binds (api-payload-contracts.md §Authenticated Principal; forwarding the shell's token is DPoP-unsound, since the shell's token binds the shell's key). **What no V1 document specifies is the token's issuance-into-the-daemon path and its refresh across the registrar's session-lifetime retry loop.** The protocol layer is owned by ADR-010/Spec-008; the issuance implementation lands with Plan-018's Tier-5 PASETO wiring — the same gate the production `resolveCurrentParticipantId` `tier5DeferralError` stub already imposes on every shipped `runtimenode.*` procedure, so no Tier-4 code is blocked. Plan-006 therefore declares a constructor-injected credential-provider INTERFACE — `DaemonCredentialProvider` at T3.3's `daemon-credential-provider.ts`, the declaration hoisted from T4.10 to the Phase-3 first consumer at Codex PR #278 round 1 so no earlier-phase task imports a later-phase symbol (invoked per attempt, each retry carrying fresh material; an auth failure is a retryable transport failure on the existing backoff), with the implementation + composition-root wiring owed by Plan-018 — the CP-006-1/CP-006-11 injected-interface shape, runtime assertion against the stub included, so tier order is preserved. The seam is deliberately credential-class-agnostic: if a future ADR-010 amendment mints a distinct node-scoped credential (the Spec-008 relay `connectionToken` purpose-scoped model), the interface absorbs it unchanged — that amendment is ADR-010's + Plan-018's to author, not this plan's. Two audited sibling surfaces shared this seam's silence at registration; the 2026-08-01 T4.10 targeted readiness-audit delta adjudicated both. Plan-006's own T3.3 anchor uploader IS this plan's to author — the earlier neither-is-this-plan's-to-author clause was wrong for that surface — and T3.3 now owns the declaration its uploader consumes; with T4.2's roster caller (Codex PR #278 round 1) the CP-018-12 reciprocal covers the three Plan-006 callers — the T3.3 uploader, the T4.2 roster caller, and the T4.10 registrar — plus, foreign-owned, Plan-024 T-024-3B-3's lease publisher: the four-caller set as of 2026-08-01. Plan-024 T-024-3B-3's lease publisher (Phase 3B, W2.5 targeted re-audit delivered 2026-07-21) stays foreign-owned and was routed to a follow-up Plan-024 amendment PR per the runbook's cross-tier contingency — recorded in the delta's audit record, never auto-amended from here; that amendment landed 2026-08-02 as [Plan-024 CP-024-5](./024-rust-pty-sidecar.md#cross-plan-obligations) (PR #279), binding the lease publisher onto the seam as its fourth caller and gating Plan-024 Phase 3B on Plan-006 Phase 3 merged. A fifth caller followed 2026-08-12: Plan-014's daemon-resident relay transfer service ([Plan-014 CP-014-5](./014-artifacts-files-and-attachments.md#cross-plan-obligations-provided), Codex PR #326 round 2) — its Task-7 upload calls and Task-8 fetch-token mint present the same token + proof via `mintForAttempt`, and its fetch legs scope the seam's one growth, a proof-for-a-presented-token affordance (DPoP proofs whose `ath` hashes the presented fetch token, under the same daemon-held key). **Discharged 2026-08-15 (Plan-018 NS-62 promotion pass, PR #334):** the owed decomposition is authored as Plan-018 Phase 5 — T5.4/T5.5 the implementation + issuance/refresh path, T5.7 the composition-root wiring with the runtime stub assertion at the five live sites, and T5.6 the presented-token affordance, whose interface growth (`mintProofForPresentedToken`, same-proof-key custody) is REGISTERED on T3.3's declaration in that same swap with the `.ts` edit landing at T5.6's dispatch as a sanctioned seam edit — flipping this plan under the runbook interface-growth rule and restoring it `approved` in the same swap under NS-62's targeted coverage (the PR #278 self-audit shape). The caller enumeration is henceforth live-plus-forward-declared, never a bare count: five live (T3.3 uploader, T4.2 roster caller, T4.10 registrar, Plan-024 lease publisher, Plan-014 relay transfer), plus Plan-019 T3.1's `attention.notificationEmit` publisher (D-019-3, Tier 8) and Plan-027 T2.1's participant-roster reader (Tier 9), each injected by its own plan. Mirrored on the [cross-plan-dependencies.md §3 Inter-Plan Dependency Graph](../architecture/cross-plan-dependencies.md#3-inter-plan-dependency-graph) Plan-006 row, with the reciprocal registered as [Plan-018 CP-018-12](./018-identity-and-participant-state.md#cross-plan-obligations) — which also carries the matching implementation-derived edge on the §3 Plan-018 row and the Plan-018 header-Dependencies clause.
- **CP-006-15 — Plan-005 consumes `content_payload` at the canonical-transcript fold (⇄ CP-005-13; registered 2026-08-30, §6 node NS-94).** Phase 3B ships the column, its session-key sealer, and the read path that opens it. [Plan-005](./005-provider-driver-contract-and-capabilities.md)'s canonical-transcript fold is its first consumer and its reason for existing: [ADR-029](../decisions/029-canonical-transcript-is-authoritative.md) makes the transcript authoritative for the content of a provider session, and a projection over `session_events` can only be authoritative for content the rows hold. The hold is **task-scoped and one-directional** — it binds Plan-005 T3.20 alone (the BL-153 `held task-scoped on T4.8` form), not Plan-005 Phase 3, and Plan-006 owes nothing back beyond shipping this phase. Deliberately **not** a phase-level gate: T3.19's fold and export are correct over a body-less log — that is the state they were authored against — so gating the phase would block work this column does not block. What T3.20 cannot do without the column is the one thing it exists to do: its post-replay assertion is the only admissible evidence a replay worked, and over content-free turns that assertion cannot separate a provider that accepted every frame from one that silently dropped them all, both answering with turns that have nothing to compare. Plan-006 does **not** own the fold's loss reporting: the two step-1 (Fold) `DeclaredLossKind` members this column's states produce, `'turn_content_unavailable'` (undecryptable) and `'turn_content_truncated'` (stored as a prefix), are declared by Plan-005's fold from the row states this plan writes.
- **CP-006-14 — RETRACTED 2026-08-11 (registered 2026-08-01 by the T4.10 targeted readiness-audit delta as the Plan-003 renderer call-site for the T4.10 `attachmentId` delivery).** The Spec-008-pinned admission-time registration decoupling (landed with the Plan-008/Spec-008 restoring targeted readiness-audit delta — PR #323, cross-plan §6 node NS-58) removed the register request's `attachmentId` member and with it the entire delivery machinery this obligation existed to carry: the `event.deliverAttachmentId` method and its contracts pair, the `daemon_attachment_deliveries` table and its store, the `MethodRegistry` binder, and the AttachFlow attach-success forward + reconnect replay. No Plan-003 surface is edited — the sanctioned CP-003-6 seam edit never dispatched, the shipped attach view keeps its single-procedure header claim untouched, and the renderer-state-loss residual with its documented fresh-attach remediation dies with the delivery. The registrar's node identity resolves instead from the daemon's own shipped Plan-003 Phase-2 node registry (`node-registry.ts` over `node_trust_state` — a read-only constructor-injected consumption, no seam edit). Obligation numbers are never reused; this entry stands as the retraction record. Reciprocal: [Plan-003 §Cross-Plan Obligations](./003-runtime-node-attach.md#cross-plan-obligations) CP-003-6, retracted in the same swap; the §3 Plan-006 and Plan-003 rows trued with it.

## Invariants

Each invariant gets an I-006-N ID with the enforcement strategy and the test that confirms it.

- **I-006-1-01 — Category/type bijection.** Every event type belongs to exactly one category; `SESSION_EVENT_CATEGORY_BY_TYPE.size === 156` (140 per the 2026-07-02 Spec-006 B1 amendment — the pre-B1 target was 130 — +1 `pty.control_changed` per the 2026-07-06 B4 amendment, +15 per the 2026-07-22 B18 amendment, +2 (`agent.provider_switched`, `agent.provider_switch_failed`) per the 2026-08-26 same-agent provider-switch amendment, +1 (`session.peer_invocation_set`) per the 2026-08-26 Spec-030 peer-invocation amendment; 141/19 at the T1.1–T1.2 baseline until T1.10 lands the B18 delta, 156/20 until Plan-016 T1.13 lands the provider-switch delta under CP-016-3, and 158/20 until Plan-030 T4.2 lands the peer-invocation delta under CP-030-5, reaching 159/20 — the shipped assertion tracks the union, and the union is widened by those tasks, not by any amendment) and `new Set(SESSION_EVENT_CATEGORY_BY_TYPE.values()).size === 20`. Enforced via Phase 1 type-level exhaustiveness check + runtime assertion. Cite: `Spec-006 §Event Type Summary`.
- **I-006-1-02 — Event-type-string immutability.** Type strings are immutable wire identifiers; renames are forbidden once a type is registered. Enforced via build-time const-assertion + `ADR-018 §Decision` #8 (MINOR additive-only). Cite: `Spec-006 §Canonical Serialization Rules`; `ADR-018 §Decision` #8.
- **I-006-1-03 — Envelope field set is fixed; serialized order is RFC 8785 lex-sort.** The Zod schema declares the canonical field set; RFC 8785 §3.2.3 mandates UTF-16 code-unit lex-sort serialization. JSDoc on `EventEnvelopeSchema` references the `Spec-006 §Canonical Serialization Rules` amendment. Enforced via Phase 2 T2.3 golden-vector tests + `Spec-006 §Canonical Serialization Rules` prose amendment. Cite: `Spec-006 §Canonical Serialization Rules` + amendment; RFC 8785 §3.2.3.
- **I-006-1-04 — `EventEnvelope.version` immutability.** Producer-set at emit; never rewritten on read (upcaster chain mutates in-memory representation only). Enforced via `EventEnvelopeVersion` brand + JSDoc cite to `ADR-018 §Decision` #2/#6. Cite: `ADR-018 §Decision` #2, #6; `Spec-006 §EventEnvelope Version Semantics`.
- **I-006-2-01 — Encrypt → digest → embed → canonicalize → sign order is load-bearing.** Phantom-branded types per stage make reversing the order a TS compile error: `RawEventInput → EventWithPiiDigest → CanonicalBytes → SignedRow`. T2.5 post-shred property test verifies signature still verifies after `pii_payload` shred. Cite: `Spec-006 §Canonical Serialization Rules` (the `pii_payload` exclusion from the canonical form, which is what makes the digest-before-canonicalize position of this order load-bearing); `Spec-022 §Signature Safety Under Shred` (the post-shred verification guarantee the order exists to preserve).
- **I-006-2-02 — `pii-indirection.ts` is the sole write path for `pii_payload`.** Branded `PiiPayloadCiphertext` constructible only inside this module; Plan-001's persistence-layer INSERT helper types the parameter as `PiiPayloadCiphertext | null`; cross-module construction is a TS compile error. Cite: `Plan-006 §Encrypt-Then-Digest-Then-Sign Order`.
- **I-006-2-03 — Canonical bytes are byte-stable across implementations.** T2.3 golden-vector tests + the RFC 8785 Appendix B Table 1 number-serialization conformance vectors guarantee byte-identical output for semantically-equal envelopes. Cite: RFC 8785 Appendix B (Table 1 — ECMAScript-Compatible JSON Number Serialization Samples); `Spec-006 §Canonical Serialization Rules`.
- **I-006-2-04 — Genesis `prev_hash` = 32 zero bytes; subsequent `prev_hash[n] = row_hash[n-1]`.** Enforced when rows are written, not retroactively: `GENESIS_PREV_HASH` in `signer.ts` is the sole genesis seed (T2.2, shipped), and T3.1's append path holds the linkage BY CONSTRUCTION rather than by an assertion — stronger, because there is no predicate that could be dropped: under the session append lock it reads `sequence` and `row_hash` from the chain head in ONE query (two queries could straddle a concurrent commit and chain a row to a predecessor its `sequence` does not follow), seeds `GENESIS_PREV_HASH` only when that head is absent, and persists the very `prevHash` the signer committed to rather than a re-read of the column. T2.3's `signer.golden.test.ts` golden vectors pin both the seed and the `BLAKE3(prev_hash || canonical_bytes)` ordering (shipped), and T3.1's `event-log-service.test.ts` hash-chain suite pins the derivation end to end: genesis at `sequence` 0, every row chained to its predecessor and verifying, and per-session partitioning. The T2.6 backfill migration that previously carried half this clause was deleted 2026-07-26 — write-time enforcement is strictly stronger, and no unchained or unsigned row can pre-exist it because T3.1 is the first code that durably writes a row and it signs (`Plan-006 §Progress Log`). Cite: `Spec-006 §Integrity Protocol`; `Plan-006 §Hash Chain`; `Plan-001 §Phase 3 — Daemon Migration And Projection` (integrity-column placeholder convention).
- **I-006-2-05 — Signed bytes commit to `pii_ciphertext_digest`, not raw ciphertext.** `daemon_signature` covers `canonical_bytes(row)` which includes the digest but excludes `pii_payload`; post-shred verification still succeeds. T2.5 verifies. **Scope clarification (2026-07-27) — this invariant is UNCHANGED, keeps its id, and is neither amended nor superseded; what follows records the scope it always had, so the question is not reopened.** What the canonical form holds out is the PII PARTITION'S CONTENT — the plaintext and the ciphertext — which is why the invariant is worded "commit to `pii_ciphertext_digest`, **not raw ciphertext**" rather than as a rule about anything that references that partition. It was never a rule against identifiers and could not have been: `actor` is one of the canonical eleven members and `0001-initial.ts` documents that column as "participant_id or agent_id or NULL for system", so the canonical form has signed participant ids since the day it shipped. I-006-2-12's `pii_participant_id` owner stamp therefore lands inside the signed bytes as a CLARIFICATION of this invariant's scope rather than an exception to it, and it is not redundant with `actor`: the two carry the same KIND of datum but answer different questions — `actor` names who EMITTED the row, the stamp names whose content key SEALED the ciphertext — and they diverge exactly where the divergence is expensive, since an agent-emitted row (`actor` = an `agent_id`) or a system-emitted one (`actor` NULL) can still carry a participant's PII, and there `actor` cannot answer the question `Spec-022 §Shred Fan-Out`'s scope selector asks. The partition itself stays out, and T2.5 leg 1's sentinel assertions are what keep it out. Cite: `Spec-022 §Signature Safety Under Shred`; `Spec-006 §Canonical Serialization Rules`.
- **I-006-2-06 — One canonicalization per row.** `signRow` takes `CanonicalBytes` once and uses it for both BLAKE3 input and Ed25519 message — no opportunity for drift. Cite: [Security Architecture §Per-Event Daemon Signature](../architecture/security-architecture.md#per-event-daemon-signature) (`daemon_signature` covers the SAME `canonical_bytes(row)` that feeds `row_hash` — signing and hashing share one byte string, so a verifier never re-canonicalizes).
- **I-006-2-07 — `audit_integrity` and `event_maintenance` categories carry no `pii_payload`.** Discriminated-union type on `RawEventInput`: events in these categories have `pii_payload: never`. Cite: `Spec-006 §Audit Integrity (audit_integrity)` (audit_integrity never-shred invariant + base payload shape); `Spec-006 §Event Maintenance (event_maintenance)` (event_maintenance never-shred invariant + base payload shape).
- **I-006-2-08 — `verifyRow`'s check order is observable, and the placeholder stage precedes the chain stage.** First failure wins, so the order IS the verdict for a row failing more than one check: (1) structural chain-column shape → `hash_mismatch`; (2) all three integrity columns zero-filled → `signature_placeholder`; (3) `BLAKE3(prev_hash || canonical_bytes)` recompute → `hash_mismatch`; (4) Ed25519 over the same canonical bytes → `signature_mismatch`. Stage 2 anywhere later is dead code — a placeholder row zero-fills the chain columns too, so it would fail stage 3 first and `signature_placeholder` could never be returned. Three properties are load-bearing on the same function: Ed25519 verification is ZIP-215-strict (`{ zip215: false }` — not the library default for this curve), daemon and participant signatures are never cross-validated, and key-resolution / plumbing bugs (wrong-width key, non-`Uint8Array` input) throw rather than returning a tampering verdict that would raise `audit_integrity_failed` on every row they touch. Enforced in `signer.ts` (T2.2); all five pinned by T2.3's `signer.golden.test.ts`. Cite: `Spec-006 §Integrity Protocol` (placeholder signatures are refused, never retro-signed); [Security Architecture §Verification Rules](../architecture/security-architecture.md#verification-rules) (rule 1's all-placeholder exception is what MANDATES the stage-2 position rather than merely permitting it, keeping rule 2's precondition reachable).
- **I-006-2-09 — The canonicalizer's refusal set is closed over the serialized tree, and its refusal order is observable.** `canonicalizer.ts` raises six refusal families itself and no seventh: `normalizeOccurredAt`'s four guards (a lexical shape outside RFC 3339 with an uppercase `T` and a `Z` or `±HH:MM` offset; sub-millisecond precision the canonical form cannot hold, refused rather than truncated because truncation would leave `daemon_signature` not committing to the recorded timestamp; a lexically-valid but non-existent calendar date, caught by reading the fields back after the setters roll them over; and an offset fold landing outside the four-digit-year range `toISOString()` renders, in both directions), `assertRepresentableSequence` (a `sequence` outside the IEEE-754 safe-integer range, where distinct sequences collapse onto one double and two different events could share a `row_hash` in a chain built to be injective), the `CANONICAL_JSON_MAX_DEPTH` ceiling (a stack-exhaustion guard — the exact-pinned `canonicalize@3.0.0` recurses once per nesting level with no bound of its own, and CP-006-3 routes untrusted Spec-024 bodies straight into it), `assertNoToJsonOverride` (any value carrying a callable `toJSON` at any depth — arbitrary caller code that would otherwise divert the serializer onto a tree no guard inspected, and re-invoking it at verification time need not reproduce the bytes that were signed), `assertWellFormedStrings` (RFC 8785 §3.2.2.2's MUST-terminate obligation over any unpaired UTF-16 surrogate in a string value or a member name — the library instead escapes it through `JSON.stringify` and emits valid-but-non-conforming text), and the no-JSON-representation guard (a top-level `undefined`, function, or symbol, for which the serializer returns no output and the encoder would otherwise hand the signer zero canonical bytes). Three further refusals originate inside `canonicalize@3.0.0` and surface with its bare wording (`NaN is not allowed`, `Infinity is not allowed`, `Circular reference detected`); they are inventoried here, not owned — and the cycle refusal is now SHADOWED in both shapes that could reach it: the depth walk drives an own-property cycle past the ceiling and reports it as depth exhaustion first, and a cycle closed only through a `toJSON` result is refused by `assertNoToJsonOverride` one guard later, before any serialization runs. What survives of it is the non-idempotent-accessor residual below, not a live path. **Which refusal sits on which entry point is forced by RFC 8785 conformance in both directions, not a placement choice:** well-formedness is generic because §3.2.2.2 obliges it for every string in every input, member names included, while the sequence refusal is event-only because Appendix B Table 1 lists unsafe integers as inputs with MANDATED outputs, so a generic unsafe-number refusal would fail the very specification this module implements; the `occurredAt` guards are reachable on two surfaces — inside `canonicalizeEvent`'s projection and through the exported `normalizeOccurredAt` T3.1's append path calls directly — and the no-JSON-representation guard is generic in effect as well as in placement, unreachable through `canonicalizeEvent`, whose top-level value is always the projection object and therefore always serializes (the guard fires on a top-level `typeof canonicalText !== "string"`, which the library returns only where it delegates to `JSON.stringify` for a non-object). **The first refusal to fire is the only one a caller sees, so the order IS the diagnostic:** `canonicalizeJson` runs the depth ceiling → the `toJSON` refusal → well-formedness → whatever the serializer itself refuses (the three library throws fire inside that call, ahead of the last guard) → no-JSON-representation, and `canonicalizeEvent` runs sequence → `occurredAt` → the three it inherits, in that same relative order. Depth-FIRST is a CORRECTNESS constraint rather than a diagnostic preference: neither of the two walks behind it carries cycle detection or a bound of its own, and it is the depth walk — which reports a cycle as depth exhaustion — that makes both terminate on cyclic input at all, so demoting it turns a refusal into a hang. The `toJSON` refusal's own position, between the ceiling and well-formedness, is a correctness constraint too and in the other direction: placed any later it would be walking a tree the well-formedness guard had already been diverted away from. **The closure claim now reaches the serialized tree, and exactly ONE residual keeps it from being unconditional.** `assertNoToJsonOverride` closed the wider of the two bypasses this invariant previously scoped itself around: a shallow object whose `toJSON()` returned a deeper or ill-formed tree used to clear every guard and be serialized anyway. The guard tests `typeof node.toJSON === "function"` down the whole prototype chain rather than `Object.hasOwn`, deliberately — an inherited `toJSON` diverts the serializer exactly as an own one does. **It REFUSES rather than inspecting the diverted output, and non-determinism is why:** a `toJSON` is arbitrary caller code re-invoked on every canonicalization, so the same value can serialize to different bytes at signing time and at verification time, and this module's entire protocol turns on re-canonicalizing a row and comparing bytes — a guard that walked the `toJSON` RESULT would still have to assume the serializer later saw that same result. Depth-ceiling evasion and §3.2.2.2 evasion are the second and third motivations, both subsumed by refusing the carrier outright. **The surviving residual is the non-idempotent ACCESSOR.** The depth walk's `Object.values` invokes each own enumerable getter once, `assertNoToJsonOverride`'s a second time, `assertWellFormedStrings`' `Object.entries` a third, and the serializer three more times per object member — **six** invocations through `canonicalizeJson`, all measured — so a getter returning different values across them puts the four walks and the serializer on different trees, and is the only remaining way to hand the serializer a tree no guard inspected. It is unreachable on the untrusted CP-006-3 path — `JSON.parse` output carries neither accessors nor a callable `toJSON`, the latter because a parsed `{"toJSON":"x"}` yields a STRING — which is what makes the scoped claim load-bearing rather than weak. The over-refusal direction stays harmless by construction: a dropped `undefined` or symbol member's NAME is still checked, and an array's non-index own enumerable properties are walked. Beyond that accessor residual, three more are characterized rather than closed: nothing upstream refuses ahead of this module on the well-formedness axis (`EventEnvelopeSchema` admits lone surrogates in `actor`, `correlationId`, `causationId`, and in `payload` keys and values alike, so the canonicalizer is the first and only refusal on that path); `assertRepresentableSequence` re-checks no schema domain rule, so a negative-but-safe `sequence` passes here and is refused at the parse boundary alone; and T1.9's `sourceEpoch` / `sourcePosition` pair rides inside `payload` as untyped JSON, carrying the identical collapse hazard with the parse boundary as its only enforcement. Enforced in `canonicalizer.ts` (T2.1); every family is pinned by T2.3's `canonicalizer.golden.test.ts`, whose four dedicated precedence tests fix the order. Cite: `Spec-006 §Canonical Serialization Rules` (RFC 8785 JCS; `occurredAt` normalized to millisecond precision so ordering is byte-stable); RFC 8785 §3.2.2.2 (the lone-surrogate MUST-terminate obligation) + Appendix B Table 1 (the mandated unsafe-number outputs that forbid a generic sequence refusal); `Plan-006 §Cross-Plan Obligations` (CP-006-3 — the TWO of the generic entry point's three refusals that BIND Plan-027, caught and mapped to distinct rejection reasons with the renegotiation affordance scoped to the policy ceiling alone; the third, the `toJSON` refusal, binds nothing there, its predicate being unsatisfiable on `JSON.parse` output).
- **I-006-2-10 — The stored `occurred_at` spelling is bound to the signature, and the binding holds in both retention classes.** `canonicalizeEvent` normalizes exactly ONE canonical member — `occurredAt`, through `normalizeOccurredAt` — and passes every other member through byte-for-byte, so for that one column `daemon_signature` commits to the INSTANT rather than to the bytes `session_events.occurred_at` actually holds. The many-to-one narrowing that makes hash reproduction robust is therefore the same property that leaves the stored spelling uncommitted: an at-rest rewrite of the column into a different spelling of the same instant (a `+00:00` or `-05:00` offset in place of `Z`, appended trailing-zero digits) re-canonicalizes to identical bytes, so the chain hash AND the Ed25519 signature both still verify, and a verifier reproducing the hash from the respelled column agrees with the attacker rather than catching them. The damage is not cosmetic: `occurred_at` is a TEXT column ordered and windowed lexically, so a respelled row sorts out of position and drops out of the very date-range scans an audit walk uses — pinned green today by two `post-shred-verify.test.ts` controls whose UPDATEs this predicate is what makes catchable. **The binding is a SECOND check, not a stronger verification, and no amount of re-verification substitutes for it.** `isCanonicalOccurredAt` (T2.1, exported over the module's own `CANONICAL_OCCURRED_AT_PATTERN`) tests the stored string against the canonical form, which admits exactly one spelling per instant and is a fixed point of `normalizeOccurredAt`; composed with a green `verifyRow`, canonical means the stored string IS the signed string and non-canonical means it is a respelling — a conjunction neither half states alone. **It applies to compacted rows too, which is the non-obvious half.** I-006-3-03 check (c) requires `occurred_at` to byte-equal the signed projection's `occurredAt`, so it catches a respelling applied AFTER compaction (`stub_scalar_mismatch`); it cannot catch one applied BEFORE, because `Spec-006 §Compacted Event Format` preserves the original timestamp verbatim — a row respelled while live has the bad spelling copied into the projection, signed into the stub bytes, and byte-equal to its column forever. Compaction LAUNDERS the live-path defect into signed bytes, which is why this predicate is the only binding that catches the respelling in either state. **Never throwing is load-bearing rather than defensive style:** T4.1 consumes it inside a range walk, where a single throw would abort the walk and silence audit of the entire tail, so it returns a verdict on every input — including the non-string a BLOB-affinity tamper can put in a TEXT column. The verdict it feeds is `occurred_at_not_canonical` paired with `failurePath: 'signature'`, that field naming the GUARANTEE that failed (the signature binds the stored bytes) rather than the column the defect occupies. Residual, characterized rather than closed: the check is LEXICAL, so `2026-02-30` and `T25:00:00` satisfy it while naming no instant; nothing is lost, because every such string is refused by `normalizeOccurredAt`'s guards and so cannot sit on a verifying row, and re-deriving the calendar read-back here would duplicate that guard and give it a second way to disagree with itself. Enforced in `canonicalizer.ts` (T2.1) and consumed by `integrity-verifier.ts` (T4.1) — deliberately NOT wired into `canonicalizeEvent` or `verifyRow`, since emitting a verdict from Phase 2 would be T2 code deciding a T4.1 question; pinned by T2.3's `canonicalizer.golden.test.ts`. Cite: `Spec-006 §Canonical Serialization Rules` (`occurredAt` normalized to millisecond precision so ordering is byte-stable); `Spec-006 §Audit Integrity (audit_integrity)` (the sixteen-value `failureMode` enum carrying `occurred_at_not_canonical`); `Spec-006 §Compacted Event Format` (the verbatim timestamp that lets compaction launder the live-path defect); [Security Architecture §Verification Rules](../architecture/security-architecture.md#verification-rules) (rule 2's stored-form precondition).
- **I-006-2-11 — The `pii_payload` ciphertext column is bound to the signature that covers its digest, and the binding is checked.** `Spec-006 §Canonical Serialization Rules` excludes `pii_payload` from the canonical form — the exclusion that lets a Spec-022 crypto-shred leave the hash chain intact — and binds the column indirectly instead, requiring a `pii_ciphertext_digest` member (BLAKE3 over the ciphertext bytes) inside the signed `payload` on every row whose column is non-NULL. **Minting a commitment is not the same as enforcing one, and this is the second instance of that class in this phase.** The digest was signed from the day T2.4's codec shipped and no verification rule ever recomputed it, so the column carried no integrity binding of any kind: substituting, truncating, or deleting `pii_payload` at rest left `row_hash` and `daemon_signature` both green, exactly as an `occurred_at` respelling did under I-006-2-10. `isCiphertextDigestBound` (T2.4, exported beside `embedCiphertextDigest` so mint and check cannot drift apart again) closes it, and it is a POSTCONDITION of `verifyRow` rather than a precondition — the digest lives inside the bytes the Ed25519 check authenticates, so recomputing it against an unverified payload proves nothing. **The predicate decides four states, and two of them are absences rather than mismatches**, which is why the verdict is named `pii_ciphertext_digest_unbound`: bytes with a digest compare; neither present passes (a no-PII row, or a compacted stub, which `Spec-006 §Compacted Event Format` NULLs and re-projects without a digest); bytes with no digest violate the non-NULL MUST; and a digest whose bytes are GONE is the evidence-destruction case, where the signature verifies green over a commitment that outlived its subject — a state a plain hash comparison would never surface (and one a RECEIVED row occupies structurally, which is why the 2026-08-11 received-row provenance amendment provenance-dispatches this check at T4.1 — the four-state compare on origin-authored rows, `received_from_node_id IS NULL`, and a require-absent arm on received rows, where a planted ciphertext is reported unbound rather than compared — Spec-006's amendment blockquote owns the mechanics). **No crypto-shred carve-out exists or is needed:** `Spec-022 §Shred Fan-Out` Path 1 destroys the per-participant key and overwrites no column, so a shredded row's retained ciphertext still hashes to its signed digest and passes unchanged; the shred-state carve-out a reviewer would reach for here is unnecessary, and the `pii_payload IS NOT NULL` guard already covers the one state where the column IS emptied, which is compaction. **Never throwing is load-bearing** for the same reason as I-006-2-10 — T4.1 consumes it inside a range walk — and here it additionally requires a shape guard rather than a type annotation: both inputs arrive from SQLite, where a `BLOB`-affinity read can return a `string` on which the hash primitive throws, so a non-`Uint8Array` non-NULL value is reported unbound (fail-closed) rather than skipped. Residual, characterized rather than closed: the binding's window is the row's UNCOMPACTED life only — compaction discards the ciphertext, so there is nothing left to bind, and unlike I-006-2-10 there is no laundering counterpart to catch afterwards. Enforced in `pii-indirection.ts` (T2.4) and consumed by `integrity-verifier.ts` (T4.1) — deliberately NOT wired into `verifyRow`, which is handed the three integrity columns and never `pii_payload`. Cite: `Spec-006 §Canonical Serialization Rules` (the non-NULL digest MUST and the `pii_payload` exclusion); `Spec-006 §Audit Integrity (audit_integrity)` (the sixteen-value `failureMode` enum carrying `pii_ciphertext_digest_unbound`); `Spec-022 §Shred Fan-Out` (Path 1 destroys the key and overwrites no column); [Security Architecture §Verification Rules](../architecture/security-architecture.md#verification-rules) (rule 2's digest postcondition); `Plan-006 §Cross-Plan Obligations` (CP-006-12 — Plan-022 Tier 5 must preserve the ciphertext bytes or amend that rule in the same change).
- **I-006-2-12 — The PII owner stamp is inside the signed bytes, and the durable stamp is checked against it.** T2.4's codec projects the PII owner's participant id into the signed `payload` under `PII_PARTICIPANT_ID_PAYLOAD_KEY` on every row it writes, so `daemon_signature` commits to WHOSE key sealed the ciphertext and not only to which bytes the row holds; T3.1's additive `session_events.pii_participant_id` column carries the durable copy, and `isPiiOwnerStampBound` compares the two. **This is the third instance in this phase of the same class I-006-2-10 and I-006-2-11 closed** — a value the signature commits to that nothing ever compares back — and the third to take the same shape: PROVIDER exports a pure, total, never-throwing predicate and emits no verdict (T2.4), CONSUMER runs it as a POSTCONDITION of a green `verifyRow` and reports the verdict (T4.1, `pii_owner_stamp_unbound`). Postcondition rather than precondition for the I-006-2-11 reason: the claim lives inside the bytes the Ed25519 check authenticates, so comparing against an unverified payload proves nothing. **The stamp is not discharged by the digest beside it, and the divergence is exactly where it is expensive.** The digest binds WHICH bytes the row holds; the stamp binds WHOSE data they are. Once `Spec-022 §Shred Fan-Out` Path 1 destroys the per-participant key, no decrypt can ever re-attribute the retained ciphertext, so the stamp becomes the SOLE surviving evidence of whose data the row held — and it is not redundant with `actor`, which names who EMITTED the row: an agent-emitted row (`actor` = an `agent_id`) or a system-emitted one (`actor` NULL) can still carry a participant's PII, and there `actor` cannot answer the question Path 1's scope selector asks. A tampered stamp cannot stop the key deletion; it corrupts the SCOPE SELECTOR instead, falsifying the `affectedSessionIds[]` / `piiPayloadsCleared` an `event.shredded` row retains indefinitely as compliance evidence. **The predicate decides four states, two of them absences**, which is why the verdict reads `unbound` rather than `mismatch`: both absent passes (an ordinary no-PII row); both present compares; a stamp with no signed claim violates the non-NULL MUST; and a signed claim whose column has been cleared at rest is the attribution-destruction case, green under every other check. Never-throwing and fail-closed on shape are load-bearing for the I-006-2-10 range-walk reason, and both parameters are `unknown` because both arrive from SQLite. **No crypto-shred carve-out exists or is needed** — Path 1 overwrites no column, so a shredded row keeps both stamp and claim and passes unchanged (CP-006-12, whose obligation covers this column as well as `pii_payload`). **Two dispositions were recorded rather than papered over — Phase 3 closed both.** (1) The column exists: T3.1's additive migration `0007-pii-participant-id.ts` creates `session_events.pii_participant_id`, and the append path writes it from the SAME codec result that produced `pii_payload`, in the same transaction — so the durable copy this predicate compares against is live and the invariant is enforceable. It was registered here before the column shipped, which is what kept the write side and the check side from being designed apart. (2) The COMPACTED-row disposition resolves in three parts, and collapsing them is what makes it look open. The signed CLAIM always retires — settled, no ambiguity: `Spec-006 §Compacted Event Format` replaces `payload` wholesale with a fixed field set carrying no PII members, so the claim is gone whatever becomes of the column. The durable COLUMN's disposition is SET to cleared by that section's 2026-07-27 amendment, on two stated grounds — the stamp and the ciphertext are the two halves of one PII partition, and `Spec-022 §Shred Fan-Out` Path 1 scopes itself to rows whose `pii_payload` is non-NULL, so a stamp outliving the ciphertext has no remaining consumer. It was recorded as a disposition rather than entered in that section's removal list ONLY because the column did not exist; T3.1's migration created it, T3.2's compactor clears it in the same stub-write statement that NULLs `pii_payload`, and the column now stands in that removal list — so this predicate rests on a confirmed resolution rather than an assumed one, matching a stub projection that carries no `pii_participant_id`. Had it resolved the other way and RETAINED the stamp, the fix would have been caller-side scoping to `retention_class IS NULL` at T4.1 — never softening the either-alone-is-unbound state, which is the evidence-destruction case itself. That caller-side shape DID arrive for a different row class (2026-08-11 received-row provenance amendment): T4.1 scopes this call and its digest sibling to origin-authored rows (`received_from_node_id IS NULL`), because a received row carries the origin's signed claim while its stamp column is NULL by the column's own no-`pii_payload`-no-stamp rule — never held, not tampered. Enforced in `pii-indirection.ts` (T2.4) and consumed by `integrity-verifier.ts` (T4.1) — deliberately NOT wired into `canonicalizeEvent` or `verifyRow`, on the I-006-2-10 pattern. **This clarifies I-006-2-05's scope rather than excepting it:** that invariant holds out the PII partition's CONTENT, never the identifiers that reference it. Cite: `Spec-006 §Canonical Serialization Rules` (the `pii_participant_id` non-NULL MUST, the included-stamp / excluded-ciphertext asymmetry, and the T3.1 column assignment); `Spec-006 §Audit Integrity (audit_integrity)` (the sixteen-value `failureMode` enum carrying `pii_owner_stamp_unbound`); `Spec-006 §Compacted Event Format` (the stub projection that carries no stamp, and the 2026-07-27 amendment setting the durable column's compacted disposition to cleared — confirmed by T3.1's migration and T3.2's clearing stub-write, and the column named in that section's removal list); `Spec-022 §Shred Fan-Out` (Path 1 destroys the key and overwrites no column, which is both why the stamp survives and why it is the only surviving attribution); [Security Architecture §Verification Rules](../architecture/security-architecture.md#verification-rules) (rule 2's owner-stamp postcondition).
- **I-006-3-01 — Three-layer `audit_integrity` + `event_maintenance` non-compaction / non-shred enforcement.** Layer 1: compactor selector excludes (T3.2). Layer 2: `pii-indirection.ts` refuses (Phase 2). Layer 3: shred fan-out Path 1 selector excludes (Plan-022). Evidence is per layer, and only two layers have any: T3.5's `shred-safety-e2e.test.ts` covers layer 1 (a second compaction pass spares both never-compacted categories); layer 2's refusal is covered by the shipped post-shred-verify suite, which drives an `audit_integrity` event into the codec and asserts it is refused before the encrypt step; layer 3 has no shipped evidence and can have none here — its selector lands with Plan-022, so that layer stays unverified until then and this invariant is verified in two of its three parts. Cite: `Plan-006 §Audit Integrity Invariant`.
- **I-006-3-02 — `event_log_anchors` upload is metadata-only at the type level.** `AnchorPayload` (T3.3) has no `payload` field, no `events` field, no `pii_payload` field. Structurally enforces ADR-017's "control plane sees only ciphertext-derived hashes". T3.5 structural-type test asserts the exact seven fields. Cite: `ADR-017 §Decision`; [shared-postgres-schema.md §Event Log Anchors (Plan-006 — Integrity Witness)](../architecture/schemas/shared-postgres-schema.md#event-log-anchors-plan-006--integrity-witness).
- **I-006-3-03 — Compaction is anchor-before-stub and chain-commitment-frozen.** T3.2 enforces the anchor-before-compaction protocol per [Spec-006 §Post-Compaction Integrity](../specs/006-session-event-taxonomy-and-audit-log.md#post-compaction-integrity): before mutating any row in range `[start_seq, end_seq]`, the compactor verifies (or force-fires + durably queues) a covering Merkle anchor with daemon Ed25519 signature, computes a per-row `stub_signature` over the canonical bytes of each audit-stub projection, then in one transaction **replaces** `payload` with the JCS-canonicalized audit-stub projection (column is `NOT NULL` — rewritten, not nulled), NULLs `correlation_id`/`causation_id`/`pii_payload`/`pii_participant_id` (the owner stamp clears with the ciphertext it names — `Spec-006 §Compacted Event Format`'s removal list), sets `retention_class = 'audit_stub'`, and writes `stub_signature`. Chain-commitment columns (`prev_hash`/`row_hash`/`daemon_signature`/`participant_signature`/`monotonic_ns`/`version`) are NEVER mutated. T4.1 verifier handles `retention_class = 'audit_stub'` rows via THREE checks (all must pass): (a) anchor-existence + anchor-signature (NOT chain-recomputation against the original, which is impossible after canonical bytes are discarded) — anchor-missing → `failureMode: 'anchor_missing_for_compacted_range'`, anchor-signature-invalid → `failureMode: 'anchor_signature_invalid'`; (b) `stub_signature` re-verification directly over the canonical bytes stored in `payload` (the exact bytes signed at compaction) — tampered/replayed/missing → `failureMode: 'stub_signature_invalid'`; (c) scalar-binding — decode the projection from the verified `payload` bytes and assert each surviving scalar column (`id`/`session_id`/`sequence`/`occurred_at`/`category`/`type`/`actor`) equals its projection field — divergence → `failureMode: 'stub_scalar_mismatch'` (the surviving scalar columns are not covered by `stub_signature`, so an at-rest scalar edit would otherwise forge a filter/reconstruction value). All four are additive-MINOR enum extensions per `ADR-018 §Decision` #8 (sanctioned). Cite: `Spec-006 §Post-Compaction Integrity` (anchor-before-compaction protocol + per-row stub_signature commitment + scalar-binding check + threat model); `Spec-006 §Integrity Protocol` (two-tier integrity); `ADR-018 §Decision` #8.
- **I-006-3-04 — Anchor cadence: earlier-of (1000 events) OR (300 seconds).** Constants `ANCHOR_INTERVAL_EVENTS = 1000` and `ANCHOR_INTERVAL_SECONDS = 300`. T3.5 cadence test exercises both thresholds independently. Cite: `Spec-006 §Anchoring Cadence`.
- **I-006-3-05 — `pii-indirection.ts` is the sole write path for `content_payload`, and the column is unconstructible for the never-destroyed categories.** The same module and the same branding discipline that hold for `pii_payload` (I-006-2-02) hold for the machine-authored sibling: a branded `ContentPayloadCiphertext` is constructible only inside that module, and the discriminated union on `RawEventInput` that denies `pii_payload` to `audit_integrity` and `event_maintenance` (I-006-2-07) denies this column to them too — a destroyable body on a never-compacted, never-shredded row would be a body no mechanism in this plan may ever destroy. Enforced by the type system plus T3.7's type-level assertion; falsified by a compile that accepts either construction.
- **I-006-3-06 — The `content_payload` ciphertext is bound to the signature that covers its digest, is excluded from the canonical bytes, and is cleared at compaction.** `contentCiphertextDigest` sits inside `payload` and therefore inside the signed canonical bytes; the ciphertext itself never does, so the same length-and-structure-oracle argument that excludes `pii_payload` (I-006-2-05) applies unchanged. The pairing is one-directional in a way the PII pairing is not, and the difference is the invariant's substance: because compaction removes the ciphertext **and** replaces the `payload` carrying its digest, the two vanish together and no post-compaction check straddles them — there is deliberately no stub field for the digest and no `content_owner_stamp_unbound` analogue, the owner binding being `session_id`, already canonical. T3.7's canonical-bytes and post-compaction tests falsify it.
- **I-006-3-07 — The stored content ciphertext is verified against its signed digest on read, and the check is provenance-dispatched.** The sixteenth verification mode `content_ciphertext_digest_unbound` runs the fourteenth's four-state compare over (`contentCiphertextDigest` present/absent × `content_payload` present/absent), fail-closed on a column value it cannot digest, and dispatches on `received_from_node_id` exactly as the fourteenth does: compare on origin rows, **require-absent** on received rows, because the column is never relayed and a carried origin claim is precisely what a planted local value would be made to match. Without this mode a ciphertext replaced after signing leaves the signature green and surfaces only as an unreadable body — turning tampering into `'turn_content_unavailable'`, a loss report, which would make the loss vocabulary unsound. It is a verifier `failureMode`, not a wire error code. Enforced by T3.8; falsified by a replaced-ciphertext row or a digest-with-column-missing row that verifies clean, or by a clean received row reported tampered.
- **I-006-3-08 — The session content key is stored wrapped, survives master-key rotation, and is never a per-participant erasure path.** The key that seals `content_payload` is a random 32-byte AES-256 DEK persisted only as an XChaCha20-Poly1305 envelope in `session_content_keys` — the `participant_keys.encrypted_key_blob` custody shape — and is **never derived** from the daemon master key. Derivation was the round-1 design and is now prohibited, because [Spec-022](../specs/022-data-retention-and-gdpr.md)' rotate-on-shred destroys that master on any participant's erasure: a derived key would make every existing body on the daemon permanently unreadable the first time an unrelated participant exercised erasure, destroying co-owned work product the request has no claim on. Rotation therefore re-wraps this table **inside the same `BEGIN EXCLUSIVE`** that re-wraps `participant_keys`, so the all-or-nothing guarantee covers both and no crash window leaves content keys under a destroyed master. Destroying this key is correspondingly **not** a shred: it seals content every session member co-owns, and it dies with its session rather than with any participant. The wrap itself **binds session and key version**: XChaCha20-Poly1305, 24-byte random nonce, wire `nonce || ciphertext || tag`, `AAD = session_id || "ais.session-content-wrap.v1" || key_version` — the [Plan-022 I-022-9](./022-data-retention-and-gdpr.md#invariants) participant-wrap form with its own info string. Without that AAD the envelope authenticates under the master key alone, so a blob swapped between two rows or replayed under a superseded `key_version` unwraps cleanly and merely fails to open that session's bodies — a key substitution indistinguishable from an ordinary `'turn_content_unavailable'`, with the ciphertext-digest verifier still green because the event ciphertext was untouched. Enforced by T3.6's key store and the rotation entry point it exposes; falsified by a key derivable from the master, by a re-wrap pass outside rotation's transaction, by a participant-erasure path that destroys it, or by a wrapped blob that unwraps after being moved to another session's row or replayed under another `key_version`.

- **I-006-4-01 — Exactly-one verifier emission per range.** `verifyRange({sessionId, fromSeq, toSeq})` emits exactly ONE of `audit_integrity_verified` or `audit_integrity_failed`; never zero, never two. Consumers dedupe **within one `sessionId`** by `(verifierNodeId, fromSeq, toSeq, verifiedAt)`; T4.1's `compensable` three-member `(verifierNodeId, fromSeq, toSeq)` key is read under that same scope. The scope is load-bearing rather than implied (Codex PR #300 round 1): sequence numbers are session-local, so one verifier finishing the same numeric range for two sessions yields tuples that collide, and an unscoped consumer would discard one session's verdict outright. Both keys are consumer-side dedupe conventions, not durable constraints — the durable uniqueness in this plan is T3.3's `UNIQUE(session_id, node_id, start_sequence, end_sequence)`, which carries the session explicitly for the same reason. Cite: `Spec-006 §Audit Integrity (audit_integrity)` grounds what each verdict MEANS — the verified arm's successful completion of hash, signature, and anchor checks over a range, the failed arm's detected failure — and its session-binding paragraph grounds the per-session scoping above, both verdict arms carrying the real `session_id` of the verified range. The emission CARDINALITY is **plan-owned**: those rows define each verdict's meaning and no spec text requires the verifier to append a verdict or forbids it appending twice, so never-zero-never-two is this plan's contract on T4.1 rather than a restatement of Spec-006 (Codex PR #300 round 1 correctly refused the reverse reading, that the cardinality could be read out of the row definitions as an erratum).
- **I-006-4-02 — Verifier rows are themselves chain-and-signature-protected.** A tampered-after-the-fact integrity-failure record cannot be silently appended without breaking the chain. T4.1 fault-injection test verifies. Cite: [Security Architecture §Verification Rules](../architecture/security-architecture.md#verification-rules) (the `audit_integrity_failed` event is itself a `session_events` row and is therefore covered by the chain/signature/anchor protocol going forward).
- **I-006-4-03 — `refuse_on_rotation` invariant enforcement (observable scope narrowed + halt sharpened by the 2026-08-01 delta; halt authority scoped local at Codex PR #278 round 3).** Key-reuse detection halts ingest from every locally-hosted identity the colliding key appears under; `key_reuse_detected` payload carries `rotationInvariantViolated: 'refuse_on_rotation'` verbatim. Observable scope, stated honestly for a daemon-local observer: the T4.2 observer compares the daemon-local `daemon_signing_keys` rows and the roster snapshots resolvable from THIS daemon (the control-plane snapshot via T4.2's thin `runtimenode.signingkeyroster` caller under the T3.3-declared credential provider — CP-006-13's third consumer, Codex PR #278 round 1), so it detects a collision exactly where at least one colliding identity is locally resolvable — a collision spanning two sessions with no common member is structurally invisible to every daemon-local observer, a blindness recorded as a §5 V1.1 node-identity extension rather than papered over with control-plane uniqueness monitoring (REJECTED: under global uniqueness, an attacker registering a victim's readable public roster key into an attacker-owned slot would permanently lock the victim out — no V1 rotation ceremony exists — and the probe reintroduces the key-existence oracle the register path's refusal collapse prevents). Halt rule, fail-closed on BOTH identities: on detection the observer halts ingest from EVERY locally-hosted `(session_id, node_id)` identity the colliding key appears under (the enforcement point is this daemon's own append path — V1 has no daemon-to-daemon ingress and the envelope carries no `NodeId`, so a remote identity presents nothing local to halt; remote identities are halted by their own hosting daemons' observers over the same shared roster and refused new slots by the register-once conflict arm — Codex PR #278 round 3) — never first-wins, because when the threat model is "attacker registered first," the first-seen identity is exactly the attacker's; disambiguation is operator work over the durable `key_reuse_detected` record (re-emitted with the complete current set whenever the observed identity set expands, per T4.2's set-keyed dedupe — Codex PR #278 round 2 — so the latest record always enumerates the full halted set), and un-halting is an explicit operator action, never an automatic winner-pick — in V1, resolving the collision in the durable stores (session teardown; a successor session mints a fresh key), never alarm suppression, since no rotation ceremony can make a still-colliding identity trustworthy. The append-gate SEAM this halt enforces through — T3.1's injected `IngestHaltSource`, consulted by `EventLogService.append` before admission, vacuous `NeverHaltedIngestHaltSource` default until T4.2's wiring hands the service T3.1's `IngestHaltRegistry` (the cycle-breaking halt-state carrier the observer populates) — is declared at T3.1 as of the 2026-08-02 follow-up amendment PR #281 (Codex PR #278 round 3's routed mechanism, sequenced before Phase 3 dispatch), with the typed `daemon.ingest_halted` refusal at the gate. T4.2 test verifies both halves plus the expansion re-emission. Cite: `Spec-006 §Audit Integrity (audit_integrity)` grounds the DETECTION half — the `key_reuse_detected` row, its payload, and the `refuse_on_rotation` posture the collision evidences — and grounds it as detection only: that row reads "An observer/monitor detected …", and its Sigstore precedent is framed as monitoring rather than enforcement. The ENFORCEMENT half — halting ingest from every locally-hosted colliding identity, never first-wins, un-halting as an explicit operator action — is **plan-owned**, and deliberately so (Codex PR #300 round 1): neither Spec-006 nor [Security Architecture §Verification Rules](../architecture/security-architecture.md#verification-rules) states an ingest-halt duty anywhere, and the one halt Spec-006 does state belongs to `audit_integrity_failed` halting REPLAY — a different operation on a different surface. This plan designs the enforcement on top of the spec's detection-only signal rather than restating spec text, which is what the [plan template §Invariants](./000-plan-template.md#invariants) grounding rule's plan-owned arm exists for; promoting it into Spec-006 would be a new Required-Behavior duty and would flip that spec to `review`.
- **I-006-4-04 — Replay across compacted regions returns audit stubs, never silent omission.** `readAfterCursor` / `readWindow` return all sequence positions; compacted positions carry `payload.retentionClass === 'audit_stub'`. T4.3 test asserts. Cite: `Spec-006 §Fallback Behavior`; `Spec-006 §Replay Interaction with Compacted Regions`.
- **I-006-4-05 — `EventSubscription` replay-then-live cursor handoff is monotonic.** Strict sequence order; no duplicate at boundary; no gap at boundary. T4.5 test verifies. Cite: `Spec-006 §Interfaces And Contracts`; `Spec-006 §Fallback Behavior`.
- **I-006-4-06 — Zod-validated wire envelopes at SDK seam.** Malformed responses rejected at the SDK boundary, not silently coerced. T4.6 + T4.7 tests verify. Cite: Plan-005 Phase 4 precedent.
- **I-006-4-07 — Audit stubs render as visible summarized segments.** `<CompactedStubSegment>` never renders null or empty; empty `summary` still produces a visible "(compacted, no summary)" segment. T4.8 + T4.9 tests verify. Cite: `Spec-006 §Fallback Behavior`; `Spec-006 §Compacted Event Format`.
- **I-006-4-08 — Signing-key roster registration is register-once, and a different-key registration is refused before any row mutation.** The control-plane register path (`signing-key-service.ts`, T4.10) admits at most one key per `(session_id, node_id)`: a first registration INSERTs, a same-key replay is an acknowledged no-op, and a different-key registration is refused with the typed `runtimenode.signingkeyregister_conflict` (HTTP 409 / tRPC `CONFLICT`) and no row written — never a silent overwrite, and never a raw driver unique-violation surfacing in place of the typed refusal under a concurrent race. V1 specifies no daemon signing-key rotation ceremony, so refusal IS the rotation policy — the control-plane mirror of I-006-4-03's daemon-side `refuse_on_rotation` posture on a disjoint surface (the two enforcement sites share no code, types, or wire contract; minted at PR #274 round 3 to close the T4.2-symmetry gap rather than leaving the posture invariant-less on one of its two surfaces). T4.10 tests verify: register-once, same-key idempotence, different-key typed refusal, and the concurrent different-key race resolving to exactly one stored key plus one typed conflict. Cite: [Security Architecture §Per-Event Daemon Signature](../architecture/security-architecture.md#per-event-daemon-signature) (the register-once / different-key-refusal posture); `Spec-006 §Canonical Serialization Rules` (the `NodeId`-keyed roster the register path feeds).
- **I-006-4-09 — The registrar's refusal collapse and attempt preconditions are conjunctive and cannot silently narrow (minted by the 2026-08-01 targeted readiness-audit delta; predicate set re-derived 2026-08-11 by the admission-time decoupling).** Two surfaces where a dropped predicate is silent are pinned. (1) The register path's collapsed authorization refusal spans its whole negative space — inactive membership, absent membership, and an unknown session all refuse with the ONE typed `runtimenode.permission_denied`, byte-indistinguishable (the 2026-08-11 decoupling deleted the former attachment-ownership and `attachmentId`-equality predicates with the delivery machinery; the producer binding is the daemon-bound CP-006-13 credential at participant granularity, the widening named at [Security Architecture §Per-Event Daemon Signature](../architecture/security-architecture.md#per-event-daemon-signature)); a regression distinguishing any arm — or softening one into tRPC `NOT_FOUND`, the reserved old-control-plane discovery signal — still passes every register-once test, so the T4.10 suite asserts each refusal arm plus the collapse (byte-indistinguishable responses). (2) The daemon registrar never attempts registration before BOTH attempt preconditions hold — the injected credential provider has produced a token and the daemon's node identity has resolved from the injected node-registry read (`node_trust_state`, populated at daemon startup by Plan-003's own ordering; the 2026-08-11 decoupling replaced the delivered-`attachmentId` precondition, deleting the renderer delivery dependency) — the plan's own round-3 two-surface reasoning made durable as an invariant rather than left as task prose. Enforced in `signing-key-service.ts` + `signing-key-registrar.ts` (T4.10); T4.10 tests verify. Cite: [Security Architecture §Per-Event Daemon Signature](../architecture/security-architecture.md#per-event-daemon-signature) (the admission-time registration posture and its named producer-binding widening); [error-contracts.md §Runtime Node](../architecture/contracts/error-contracts.md#runtime-node) (the no-info-leak collapse header).

## Audit Reconciliation

Transcription errors and cross-doc inconsistencies discovered during the 2026-05-28 Plan-006 audit, all resolved in this PR or named follow-up.

- **Plan-006 working-copy 120/18 vs canonical 123/19.** Pre-audit working-copy transcriptions carried the wrong count. Reconciled to the then-canonical 123/19 (`Spec-006 §Event Type Summary` enumerates the 19 categories and the event-type total). The canonical total subsequently advanced to **125/19** at the 2026-05-30 Tier-5 swap — Plan-022 D-022-5 registered `daemon.master_key_source` + `daemon.pii_split_ambiguous` under `security_events` — to **126/19** at the 2026-06-10 Tier-6 swap — Plan-012 D-012-8 registered `approval.canceled` under `approval_flow` (`Spec-006 §Approval Flow (approval_flow)`) — and to **130/19** in the same Tier-6 swap — Plan-016 registered `usage.budget_warning` (A-016-6), `moderation.review_flagged` (D-016-10), `orchestration.rejected` (D-016-11), `channel.unmuted` (D-016-12); all mirrored into §Event Taxonomy Coverage above. Working-copy updated in this audit PR.
- **`events/` subdirectory non-existent in `packages/contracts/src/`.** Plan-006 working-copy referenced `packages/contracts/src/events/envelope.ts` + `events/taxonomy.ts`; the directory does not exist (Plan-001's contracts package uses top-level co-location). Reconciled per the directory-placement decision: extend top-level `event.ts` in place. Working-copy Target Areas updated.
- **api-payload-contracts.md Plan-006 §EventEnvelope listed 16 categories.** Reconciled per T1.6: widened to 19 with the three missing entries (`channel_arbitration`, `onboarding_lifecycle`, `cross_node_dispatch`) and the comment updated from "16 categories total" to reference `Spec-006 §Event Type Summary` with the canonical event-type count (123 at this audit; advanced to 125 at the 2026-05-30 Tier-5 swap per Plan-022 D-022-5, then 126 at the 2026-06-10 Tier-6 swap per Plan-012 D-012-8, then 130 at the same Tier-6 swap per Plan-016 — `usage.budget_warning` A-016-6, `moderation.review_flagged` D-016-10, `orchestration.rejected` D-016-11, `channel.unmuted` D-016-12). Applied in this audit PR.
- **`docs/architecture/security-architecture.md §Verification Rules` used superseded `failureKind: 'chain_break'` interim term.** `Spec-006 §Integrity Events` explicitly says the prior interim registration is superseded; `Spec-006 §Audit Integrity (audit_integrity)` enumerates the canonical `failureMode` enum (7 values at audit time, extended to 11 in this audit PR — see post-compaction integrity entries below). Fixed in this audit PR (`docs/architecture/security-architecture.md §Verification Rules` updated to use `failureMode` + `failurePath`).
- **`Spec-006 §Canonical Serialization Rules` prose amendment** — Pre-amendment wording "Fields included, in this order:" was ambiguous against RFC 8785 §3.2.3's mandate. Amended in this audit PR to "Fields included (the canonical set; serialized order is mandated by RFC 8785 §3.2.3 UTF-16 code-unit lex-sort of member names, not the order listed here)" with explicit RFC 8785 cite.
- **Post-compaction integrity gap surfaced by Codex review on PR #124 (T5 P1 finding, 2026-05-28).** Pre-amendment Plan-006 I-006-3-03 stated "Verifier short-circuits chain recomputation on `retention_class = 'audit_stub'` rows" — violating `Spec-006 §Integrity Protocol`'s "append-only AND tamper-evident" guarantee + §Acceptance Criteria #3 ("visible in audit history even after payload compaction"). Resolved by introducing the **anchor-before-compaction protocol**: Spec-006 amended with a new §Post-Compaction Integrity sub-section under §Event Compaction Policy, §Integrity Protocol amended with two-tier integrity statement, `failureMode` enum extended from 7 to 9 values (`anchor_missing_for_compacted_range` + `anchor_signature_invalid`, additive-MINOR per `ADR-018 §Decision` #8), and a 4th Acceptance Criterion added. Plan-006 I-006-3-03 rewritten; T3.2 compactor + T4.1 verifier updated; `Plan-006 §Target Areas` + `Plan-006 §Implementation Steps` enum-count references swept to 9. Hardened-mode path; no V1.1 deferral. Resolution surfaces the compacted-range integrity guarantee at anchor-existence + anchor-signature granularity (per-row recomputation is impossible after canonical bytes are discarded, but the daemon-signed Merkle anchor — durably persisted to both `pending_anchor_uploads` and `event_log_anchors` — provides range-level commitment to the pre-compaction state).
- **Post-compaction stub authenticity gap surfaced by Codex re-review on PR #124 (round-2 P1 finding, 2026-05-28).** The round-1 anchor-before-compaction protocol (above) commits to the ORIGINAL pre-compaction bytes (via the frozen `row_hash` the anchor's Merkle root covers) but left the POST-compaction audit-stub bytes — now the only visible `payload` — unauthenticated: a local tamper of the stub's `summary`/`actor` while leaving `row_hash` + the signed Merkle root untouched still verified. Resolved (hardened-mode, no V1.1 deferral) by adding a per-row **`stub_signature`** — an Ed25519 signature over `canonical_bytes(audit-stub projection)` minted by the compactor at compaction time (T3.2) and re-verified by T4.1 against the row's CURRENT stub bytes. `Spec-006 §Compacted Event Format` + `Spec-006 §Post-Compaction Integrity` amended (per-row commitment + full threat model: stub-edit, cross-row replay, signature-strip, and both `retention_class`-flip directions all detected); `failureMode` enum extended 9 → 10 (`stub_signature_invalid`, additive-MINOR per `ADR-018 §Decision` #8); `Spec-006 §Compacted Event Format` clarified that `payload` is REPLACED with the stub projection (the column is `NOT NULL`), not nulled — coupled fix for the replay/renderer "never silent omission" contract (Codex round-2 P2 on T3.2). New additive column `session_events.stub_signature BLOB` (migration `0NNN-retention-class-and-stub-signature.ts`); I-006-3-03 + T3.2 + T4.1 + `Plan-006 §Target Areas` + `Plan-006 §Implementation Steps` enum-count references swept to 10; security-architecture.md §Verification Rules extended with the stub-signature check. Marked a deliberate security amendment to an approved spec (anchor = external proof of original existence; `stub_signature` = at-rest proof of stub authenticity; both required, neither sufficient alone).
- **Post-compaction scalar-binding gap surfaced by Codex re-review on PR #124 (round-5 P1 finding, 2026-05-28).** The round-2 `stub_signature` (above) authenticates only the `payload` projection bytes, but the surviving scalar columns (`id`/`session_id`/`sequence`/`occurred_at`/`category`/`type`/`actor`) are neither nulled nor frozen nor signed — a denormalized cache that SQL filters (`idx_session_events_type`) and envelope reconstruction read. An at-rest edit to a scalar column (e.g. `actor`, `type`) left `payload` + `stub_signature` + the anchor all verifying while a filter/reconstruction surfaced the forged value. Resolved (hardened-mode, no V1.1 deferral) by a per-row **scalar-binding check**: T4.1 decodes the projection from the verified `payload` bytes and asserts each surviving scalar column byte-equals its projection counterpart — divergence → `stub_scalar_mismatch`. `Spec-006 §Post-Compaction Integrity` amended (verifier "two checks" → "three checks" + new threat-model scenario + new Acceptance Criterion), `failureMode` enum extended 10 → 11 (`stub_scalar_mismatch`, additive-MINOR per `ADR-018 §Decision` #8); security-architecture.md Rule 4 + cross-plan-dependencies.md `stub_signature` row + I-006-3-03 + T4.1 + `Plan-006 §Target Areas` + `Plan-006 §Implementation Steps` enum-count references swept to 11. The signed `payload` projection is affirmed as the authoritative source for a compacted row's envelope fields; the scalar columns are an index/filter cache trustworthy only post-verification.

## Parallelization Notes

- Contracts (envelope + taxonomy + error codes) block all runtime-daemon work; author first.
- Canonicalizer + signer + `pii-indirection` require contracts only and produce golden-vector artifacts the rest of the stack validates against.
- Merkle-anchor service, integrity verifier, compactor, and replay service can proceed in parallel once the append path is stable.
- Desktop timeline audit-stub rendering waits for replay-cursor + compacted-region detection.

## Test And Verification Plan

- RFC 8785 JCS golden-vector conformance tests (standard vectors + project-specific edge cases: null-vs-absent, nested object ordering, numeric canonicalization, millisecond-precision `occurredAt`).
- Hash-chain tests: genesis row, multi-row chain integrity, chain-break detection produces `audit_integrity_failed` with `failureMode = 'hash_mismatch'`.
- Signature tests: daemon-only and dual (daemon + participant) signatures, `refuse_on_rotation` posture enforcement — one Ed25519 public key observed under two distinct `(session_id, node_id)` identities produces `key_reuse_detected` (corrected at Codex PR #274 round 5, 2026-08-01: register-once means no rotation history exists, so a "rotated-out key" is a condition V1 can neither reach nor evidence — the detectable violation is the cross-identity collision).
- Version-stub round-trip: an unknown-MAJOR event is persisted verbatim; the log row's `.version` remains the producer's original; an upcaster chain re-interprets on upgrade without rewriting the row.
- PII indirection tests: post-shred signature still verifies over canonical bytes; a shredded event surfaces `pii_ciphertext_digest` but no plaintext and replaces the participant's whole PII partition with a single `<pii-shredded>` marker at one reserved payload key (partition-level, not per field).
