- Encrypt-order regression tests: a deliberately-misordered write path (signing before digest-embedding) is rejected by contract tests.
- Compaction tests: all three triggers fire independently; `audit_integrity` and `event_maintenance` categories are excluded; audit-stub format preserves envelope-level fields (id, sessionId, sequence, occurredAt, category, type, actor).
- Merkle-anchor tests: earlier-of cadence fires correctly; anchor payload is metadata only; `root_signature` verifies under the daemon public key over the anchor claim (`Spec-006 §Anchoring Cadence`, 2026-08-11 — with a negative control: a queued record whose coordinates are rewritten after signing fails verification).
- Replay tests: `EventReadAfterCursor` surfaces `retentionClass: 'audit_stub'` for compacted regions; `EventReadWindow` is bounded.
- End-to-end lifecycle: session.created → 50+ events → compaction pass → crypto-shred → integrity verification succeeds over the full chain, confirming the signature-safety property under real shred conditions.
- Content-partition tests (Phase 3B): the codec's three routing cases (participant partition alone, machine content alone, both on one row) each land the columns and signed members they owe and no others; the wrap's TWO required negatives — a blob moved to another session's row and a blob replayed under another `key_version` each fail to unwrap; the plaintext bound cuts at a codepoint boundary with `contentTruncated: true` beside a PRE-truncation `contentLength` and no sentinel in the text, while a complete body OMITS the marker entirely (writing `false` would change the canonical bytes of every complete row); compaction NULLs `content_payload`, drops `contentCiphertextDigest` from the stub, and preserves `contentLength` / `contentTruncated`; both byte-accounting statements count the sealed column, proven by a storage-threshold pass that fires on content-heavy rows whose `payload` columns alone are far under the threshold and does NOT fire on the same rows without their bodies; and the read projection reports a replaced ciphertext as tampering rather than loss, reports a non-`Uint8Array` non-NULL column unbound rather than skipping it, passes a clean row whose column is NULL, and never fabricates an empty body on any unavailable path.

## Rollout Order

**Prerequisite (re-anchored 2026-07-29 — the CP-006-7 two-leg correction; supersedes the "Plan-002 amendment-via-extension PR must land before step 4 begins" form, whose vehicle misattribution is recorded at CP-006-7)** — the provisioning call-site (`DaemonSigningKeyProvisioner.create(sessionId)`, T2.7) binds at the FIRST production composition root that wires T3.1's durable append path, not at any fixed step boundary: today no composition root wires a real database, so every step-4 caller is a test suite provisioning its own keys, and the fail-closed null-key throw in `EventLogService.append()` is the backstop that keeps the gap loud until the wiring PR lands the call-site + runtime assertion per the CP-006-1 assertion pattern (CP-006-7 leg A). The 2026-07-26 correction stands on its own terms: **step 4 (Phase 3) is where a signing gate would bind, not step 2** — T3.1's `EventLogService` is the first code that durably writes a row and therefore the first production reader of a session signing key; Phase 2 ships the signer and the key source as modules whose only callers are unit suites generating their own keypairs, so gating step 2 blocks a PR whose failure mode is unreachable and trains reviewers to waive the gate. Phase 3 dispatch is NOT gated on an off-plan PR: the append path signs with keys its own callers provision, and the production call-site obligation travels with the composition root (leg A — and, per the round-3 trigger re-anchor, that same composition root STARTS leg B's registrar at session establishment, the two-legged CP-006-1 assertion recorded at T4.10). Leg B (T4.10 post-attach signing-key registration) lands in Phase 4 — the read-side/verifier phase, step 6 below — and is what makes the step-4 session-scope anchor uploads independently verifiable after the fact: it gates the verifiability claim, not Phase 3 dispatch. (Dispatch timing was separately governed by the plan's Status: the 2026-07-29 amendment's new-contract-surface growth flipped this plan `approved → review`; the T4.10 targeted readiness-audit delta restored `approved` on 2026-08-01 per §Preconditions' Delivered clause, unblocking every lane-1 dispatch — Phase 3 included — an on-plan status gate, not an off-plan-PR gate.) Tracked under CP-006-7. No Plan-002 edit exists or is owed — Plan-002's file carries no reciprocal and never did.

1. Land envelope contracts + taxonomy enum + error-contract version codes (Phase 1).
2. Enable the append-only write primitives — BLAKE3 chain, Ed25519 signer, signing-key source — behind internal feature gating (Phase 2 — depends on step 1).
3. Enable PII indirection (encrypt → digest → embed → sign order) (Phase 2 — depends on step 2).
4. Enable the durable append path (T3.1) + compaction + Merkle anchor emission (Phase 3 — depends on step 3; the Prerequisite's call-site obligation binds at the production composition root, not at this step's start).
5. Enable replay reads and live subscription catch-up (Phase 4 — depends on step 4).
6. Enable integrity verifier + observer-pattern `key_reuse_detected` + admission-time signing-key registration (T4.10 — makes the step-4 session-scope anchor uploads independently verifiable) (Phase 4 — depends on step 5).
7. Enable the durable encrypted home for machine-authored prose — the `content_payload` column and its wrapped `session_content_keys` home, the second codec inside `pii-indirection.ts`, the compaction and byte-accounting legs, and the verify-on-read projection (Phase 3B, T3.6 → T3.7 → T3.8 in that order). **Depends on step 4 and on nothing later**: it extends the durable append path T3.1 owns, and is listed last because it was added after steps 5-6 rather than because it must follow them — it may land before, between, or after either. Within the supplement the order IS binding: T3.7 clears a column T3.6 creates, and T3.8 verifies a digest T3.6 mints.

## Rollback Or Fallback

- If a new envelope MAJOR regresses, freeze new event-category adoption; `ADR-018 §Decision` #5 version-stub behavior keeps older readers consuming the log as opaque rows until the rollback lands.
- If compaction regresses (audit stubs malformed, wrong categories compacted), disable the compactor — full event retention remains correct without compaction.
- If Merkle anchor emission regresses (upload failure, signature mismatch), disable the anchor service — the local hash chain and row signatures retain tamper-evidence on each daemon's log.
- If the integrity verifier produces false-positive `audit_integrity_failed`, disable the verifier; chain and signatures remain durable on disk for later re-verification.

## Risks And Blockers

- **Canonicalization drift.** Any divergence from RFC 8785 JCS (field ordering, null-vs-absent, numeric canonicalization) produces hashes and signatures that two honest daemons cannot reconcile. A single shared canonicalizer implementation plus golden-vector tests are load-bearing.
- **PII write-path order.** Reversing the encrypt → digest → embed → sign order breaks `Spec-022 §Signature Safety Under Shred`. Enforced by making `pii-indirection.ts` the sole write path and by encrypt-order regression tests.
- **Category drift.** Event-type renames or category reassignments on existing wire types violate `ADR-018 §Decision` #8. Enforced by the taxonomy enum + contract tests; new types are additive-only.
- **Anchor cadence under partition.** If the daemon cannot reach the control plane for >300 seconds, anchors queue locally. The per-daemon hash chain + signatures retain tamper-evidence on each local log; the anchor tier provides external cross-observer consistency that catches up on reconnect.
- **Invariant enforcement regressions.** If the compactor or shred selector ever mis-categorizes an `audit_integrity` or `event_maintenance` event, the invariant is violated silently. Enforced at three layers (compactor, pii-indirection, shred selector) to prevent single-layer regressions.
- **CP-006-7 sequencing (re-vehicled 2026-07-29; formerly "Plan-002 amendment sequencing" — the vehicle misattribution is recorded at CP-006-7).** The provisioning call-site (leg A) binds at the first production composition root that wires T3.1's durable append path — T3.1 being the intended sole durable writer and so the first production signing-key reader — "intended" rather than "first" because, when this was written, Plan-001's `SessionService.append` could write a durable row unguarded and Plan-003's shipped emitter routed through it, which is why T3.1 carries a precondition to retire or guard that path (discharged 2026-07-28, PR #272 — append guarded test-only; emitter on the structural `SessionEventLog` seam); the [§Rollout Order](#rollout-order) Prerequisite paragraph carries the re-anchored gate. The registration leg (B) is this plan's own T4.10 (Phase 4), post-attach. An unprovisioned session surfaces as a null-signing-key throw on the first `EventLogService.append()` — fail-closed by design, a load-bearing sequence dependency, not a runtime bug.

      Full-phase single-PR ship less T2.6, which was DELETED during review rather than built: its retro-signing repair path was replaced by the fail-closed `signature_placeholder` verdict now specified on T2.2's `verifyRow` (`Spec-006 §Integrity Protocol`, 2026-07-26 amendment). Phase D closed unanimous; Codex ran the full five rounds, acking clean on `f530c71`.
      The rounds closed one recurring class in exactly THREE instances — a value the audit trail depends on that nothing in the signed form commits to — each answered by a pure, total, never-throwing read-side predicate that emits no verdict, plus one new `failureMode` arm: the stored spelling of `occurred_at` (`isCanonicalOccurredAt` / `occurred_at_not_canonical`, I-006-2-10), the PII ciphertext bytes (`isCiphertextDigestBound` / `pii_ciphertext_digest_unbound`, I-006-2-11), and the PII owner stamp (`isPiiOwnerStampBound` / `pii_owner_stamp_unbound`, I-006-2-12). All three are deliberately unwired here on the provider/consumer split — Phase 2 decides, T4.1 reports. The owner stamp was the P1: an at-rest edit left hash, signature, and the digest check green while corrupting the `Spec-022 §Shred Fan-Out` Path-1 scope selector, and because Path 1 destroys the participant key, no later decrypt can re-attribute the ciphertext — the stamp is the sole surviving evidence of whose data a row held.
      Also closed: the canonicalizer now REFUSES a callable `toJSON` at any depth including the prototype chain (a stateful `toJSON` makes canonicalization non-deterministic, which no output-side check can repair); `canonicalizeEvent` refuses an unsafe-integer `sequence`; the injected sealer is shape-guarded against an empty blob and against echoing the private key back in cleartext. With `signature_placeholder`, these take the `failureMode` enum from 11 values to 15.
      Governance corpus moved in the same PR: I-006-2-09 through I-006-2-12 minted; CP-006-11 registered for the previously unowned `DaemonSigningKeySealer` seam; CP-006-12 widened to both PII columns across four surfaces; `session_events.pii_participant_id` registered as a §1 Contested Table row owned by T3.1 with Plan-022 as a read-only Path-1 consumer bound to preserve it. A late follow-up commit corrected five PII-commitment claim sites the owner stamp had falsified across Plan-022 and Spec-022 — one of them an invariant that shipped false and was caught post-push. NS-41 auto-created in the housekeeping PR.

Phase D closed at formal round 1: 4 ACTIONABLE + 15 POLISH fixed in one consolidated three-cluster pass. The headline ACTIONABLE: the compactor resolved the SESSION signing key before its row loop but the SENTINEL key only inside the post-loop event.compacted append, so an age-triggered pass could irreversibly destroy every payload in range with no durable record — fixed by probing sentinel-key resolvability at the top of the session pass (before anchorRange), refusing with zero rows mutated; probe-not-hold, no key material reaches any message. Disclosed consequence: compaction is INERT until CP-006-7 provisions the sentinel session's signing key. Two hardened wire-ins beyond the findings' minimum: the compactor consults T3.1's ingest-halt seam (SKIP semantics — stub-signing is attestation under a key the halt declared repudiable; the anchor drain deliberately NOT wired, an anchor signs pre-halt evidence and destroys nothing) and tick() refuses entry from inside a live append-lock hold via the new isWithinSessionAppendLockHold. Capability-writer internals moved onto T3.1's append seam (recorded at Plan-005 §Cross-Plan Amendments; external contract preserved — fails every status-flip prong). Dependency-accuracy doc true-ups landed in-PR (a3facc1-class, no status flips): the pii owner-stamp clauses restated as landed facts across Spec-006 / security-architecture.md, pii_participant_id joined the compaction removal list, and I-006-2-04 / I-006-3-01 evidence clauses now carry the by-construction argument and honest per-layer attribution. Two residuals ride the queued post-merge Plan-006/Spec-006 amendment PR: the bootstrap wiring enumeration (the compactor now a SECOND consumer of the sentinel-key precondition beside T4.2's emitter) and the partition-level <pii-shredded> marker true-up. NS-46 auto-created in the housekeeping PR, which also carried the five-migration 0NNN-to-concrete true-ups (daemon 0006/0007/0008/0009 + control-plane 0004) across the plan, the ownership map, and both schema docs.

Codex round 4 added three, all fixed in-PR: (12) `sourcePosition`'s value source is corrected from the binding's frozen delivery position to the straggler's per-event originating turn (the operation-to-turn association recorded at operation open, pre-fence; frozen-position fallback for association-less stragglers, always-superseding by construction) — the frozen scalar was superseded-by-construction for every straggler (the fence freezes at the run's then-current position, which admission requires strictly above `targetPosition`), vacating the surviving-prefix ranking the pair exists for; (13) the uncompacted-rewind-span check is serialized against idle compaction — span check + write-ahead intervention row atomic under the append lock, `attributeAtCompaction` gains the `'defer'` disposition for spans of dispatched-but-unconcluded rollbacks, and the compactor's per-row attribute-and-stub runs under the same lock — closing the check-then-compact race that would stub the rewound tail unstamped before the boundary lands; (14) T3.14 ships the seam's composition-root wiring plus a default-rejection runtime assertion (the CP-006-1 pattern), so the projection reaches the production `Compactor` rather than only manually-injected test instances. Codex round 5 added two, both fixed in-PR: (15) the operation association carries **(epoch, turn)**, not the turn alone — an in-place rollback (Codex `thread/rollback`, no fork) continues the same binding into the new epoch, so the binding **rotates its delivery generation at the fence** (closed generation's pair retained for stragglers; post-fence deliveries current at the confirmed floor) and reused ordinals across epochs are disambiguated by the association's recorded epoch — correcting round 4's binding-never-spans-epochs claim, which held only for the fork mechanism; (16) every run-scoped row's stub preserves `runId` (payload-read, vacuous-default era included) plus the source-resolved `originPosition` under the new T1.9-pinned `ORIGIN_POSITION_STUB_KEY` — without them an ordinary current row compacted before any rollback was invisible to the uncompacted-rewind-span check (admission could not attribute the stub to the target run) and permanently unattributable after a wrongly-admitted rollback; position-less legacy stubs of the target run are a standing refusal, and the `'current'` disposition now carries the resolved position.
- 2026-07-26 — **T2.6 (genesis-row backfill migration) deleted; replaced by a fail-closed `verifyRow` check.** The task is gone, its id is retired rather than reused (T2.7 keeps its number — it is cited from `signing-key-source.ts` and from six other places in this plan), and it must not be re-added. Four findings closed the question. (1) _The rows T2.6 would have backfilled do not exist and never have._ Every caller of `openDatabase` is under `__tests__/`; `runtime-daemon` declares no `bin` entry and is a library, not a running program; the desktop app never opens a database; no database file exists on disk; tests use `:memory:` or a temp dir removed at teardown. The first code that will ever write a durable row is T3.1, which signs — so the unsigned-row case is prevented by sequencing, not cleaned up afterwards. (2) _It could not have worked as specified._ `applyMigrations(db): void` is synchronous while `DaemonSigningKeySource.read()` is async against the OS keystore. Confirmed empirically against the pinned better-sqlite3 12.9.0: a promise-returning transaction callback throws `TypeError` **after** `BEGIN`, rolling back the pre-`await` writes while the post-`await` continuation lands outside any transaction — split-brain. Separately, `Spec-022 §Daemon Master Key`'s custody ladder terminates in **Tier 3 — refuse** ("the daemon must exit with a non-zero status and a clear error. It must not fall back to a plaintext master"), so a migration that depends on the signing key inherits that failure mode: on any machine where custody cannot be established, `openDatabase` throws and bricks both daemon boot and the test suite. (3) _Signing old rows with a later key is a documented attack, not a repair._ Felix Dörre and Jonas Ottenhues, [_Security Analysis of Forward Secure Log Sealing in Journald_](https://eprint.iacr.org/2023/867) (Applied Cryptography and Network Security 2025; Cryptology ePrint Archive Report 2023/867) — a real systemd vulnerability patched in v255 — state verbatim: _"This check does not prevent old log messages from being signed with a newer key. An attacker can take a journal, remove seals, adjust log messages as desired, and re-create a seal with the current log sealing key."_ No conforming verifier detects the substitution after the fact: raw Ed25519 verification takes no time parameter at all, and this stack uses raw Ed25519 per ADR-010 with no X.509 validity windows anywhere. A backfill migration is that procedure, run by us. (4) _Key rotation does not reintroduce the problem._ [security-architecture.md §Per-Event Daemon Signature](../architecture/security-architecture.md#per-event-daemon-signature) already keeps superseded daemon public keys resolvable in the participant roster with validity windows, so rows signed before a rotation stay verifiable and no epoch boundary is created. **Replacement:** T2.2's `verifyRow` fails closed on the 64-zero-byte `daemon_signature` placeholder and returns the new twelfth `failureMode` value `signature_placeholder` — checked before Ed25519 verification, deliberately distinct from `signature_mismatch` (a mismatch is possible tampering warranting security incident response; a placeholder is an engineering sequencing bug, and conflating them pages the wrong on-call), and never "repaired" by signing. Spec-006 rides the same change: the twelfth value on the `audit_integrity_failed` row, an amendment paragraph under `Spec-006 §Integrity Protocol` carrying the never-retro-sign rule, the enum count re-derived to 12 under `Spec-006 §Post-Compaction Integrity` (the four historical arithmetic records in §Audit Reconciliation are provenance and stay as written — the three chain steps 7→9, 9→10 and 10→11 in the post-compaction integrity / stub-authenticity / scalar-binding entries, plus the 7→11 summary in the §Verification Rules `failureKind` entry), a matching Acceptance Criterion, and the journald paper added to §References — additive-MINOR per `ADR-018 §Decision` #8, recorded as a deliberate amendment to an approved spec on the 2026-05-28 precedent, status unchanged (`approved`). I-006-2-04's enforcement clause is re-pointed off the migration onto write-time enforcement (`GENESIS_PREV_HASH` + T3.1's append assertion + T2.3's golden vectors), which is strictly stronger. Housekeeping riding this entry: T2.3's row is widened to the two suites it actually shipped (`canonicalizer.golden.test.ts` + `signer.golden.test.ts` — the latter, 36 tests over ZIP-215 strict verification, the observable FOUR-stage check order, the `signature_placeholder` fail-closed verdict, the throw-vs-verdict error-channel split, and daemon/participant key separation, was owned by no task row) with I-006-2-04 and I-006-2-06 added to its verified set; and the five "RFC 8785 Appendix-A" citations in this plan are corrected to Appendix B Table 1 — Appendix A is the ECMAScript sample canonicalizer and carries no test vectors, while the ~26 number-serialization samples live in Appendix B Table 1, which the shipped tests already cite correctly. The same correction is recorded at the Spec-006 §Integrity Protocol amendment this entry seeded.)

Status unchanged (`approved`).
- 2026-07-27 — **T2.1's SECOND refusal (RFC 8785 §3.2.2.2 Unicode well-formedness) shipped, and CP-006-3 re-registered on Plan-027 to match.** `canonicalizeJson` now refuses any string value or property name carrying an unpaired UTF-16 surrogate (`assertWellFormedStrings` / `assertNoLoneSurrogate` / `LONE_SURROGATE_PATTERN`), alongside the existing depth ceiling — both on the GENERIC entry point, so `canonicalizeEvent` inherits them with no second call site. The guard is not discretionary: RFC 8785 §3.2.2.2 makes terminating on such data a normative MUST ("occurrences of such data MUST cause a compliant JCS implementation to terminate with an appropriate error"), and the pinned `canonicalize@3.0.0` does not — it escapes the surrogate through `JSON.stringify` and keeps going, which would leave this module branding, chaining, and signing a byte string no conforming verifier will ever agree is that event's canonical form, the cross-implementation divergence `Spec-006 §Canonical Serialization Rules` exists to prevent. Reachable rather than theoretical, which is where it parts company with the library's `NaN` / `Infinity` / cycle refusals: `JSON.parse` materializes a lone surrogate out of a perfectly well-formed wire document, and `EventEnvelopeSchema` admits one in `actor`, `correlationId`, `causationId`, and in `payload` values and keys. **Cross-plan amendment under [cross-plan-dependencies.md §Ownership Rule](../architecture/cross-plan-dependencies.md#ownership-rule)'s Housekeeping Exception, recorded in full at §Cross-Plan Amendments below:** CP-006-3 described the shared entry point as bounded by the depth ceiling ALONE, so Plan-027 carried no rejection reason for an ill-formed `action_payload` — shipping the refusal without registering it reopens exactly the gap the 2026-07-26 round closed; this round adds that third obligation to `Plan-027 §Preconditions` and extends the dep-map §3 Plan-027 row's CP-006-3 parenthetical to both refusals. The load-bearing distinction the round records: the depth ceiling is Plan-006 POLICY and renegotiable (a two-way door — forking to escape it is the violation), while the well-formedness refusal is RFC-MANDATED and renegotiable with T2.1 in neither direction, so all three live surfaces now label the two `(i) policy ceiling` / `(ii) RFC-mandated refusal` and scope the renegotiation affordance to (i) explicitly. Criteria (a)–(d) are re-derived in that entry rather than carried forward from the 2026-07-26 round — (a) differs materially, since T2.1 shipped code at this seam in this PR. Status unchanged (`approved`).
- 2026-07-27 — **I-006-2-09 minted over T2.1's refusal set and refusal order; four surfaces trued up (doc-only; no code, no behavioural change).** The same gap class the 2026-07-26 entry recorded and closed for the signer half, one day later on the canonicalizer half: `canonicalizer.ts` raises five refusal families of its own across two entry points with a documented observable order, T2.3's `canonicalizer.golden.test.ts` pins every one of them across nine `describe` blocks, and exactly one of those block titles named an I-006 id (I-006-1-03) while T2.1's `Verifies invariant:` line still read `none` — behaviour pinned by tests but unnamed by any invariant, which is what the I-006-2-08 precedent refuses to leave standing. **New I-006-2-09** names the closed set (`normalizeOccurredAt`'s four guards, `assertRepresentableSequence`, the `CANONICAL_JSON_MAX_DEPTH` ceiling, `assertWellFormedStrings`, the no-JSON-representation guard), the entry-point split RFC 8785 conformance forces in both directions, the observable order on each entry point, and depth-before-well-formedness as a CORRECTNESS constraint rather than a diagnostic preference — the depth walk is what makes the well-formedness walk terminate on cyclic input, so reversing them turns a refusal into a hang. Four surfaces trued up around it: T2.1's Provides gains the exported `normalizeOccurredAt`, which T3.1's row already required by name and which no provider row declared — the same orphaned-artifact class the 2026-07-26 round repaired for `EVENT_ENVELOPE_SEQUENCE_MAX` — plus a compact statement of the refusal boundary CP-006-3 binds Plan-027 to; T2.1's `Verifies invariant:` line moves off `none` (that parenthetical was true when T2.1 was a serializer wrapper and false once it grew refusals; the I-006-2-03 byte-stability and I-006-2-06 single-use clauses survive unchanged); T2.3's canonicalizer sentence is widened to the suite it actually ships — it was stale by five `describe` blocks (the depth ceiling, the lone-surrogate refusal, the event entry point inheriting it with no second call site, the no-JSON-representation guard, and the sequence ceiling), the exact repair that entry applied to T2.3's signer half; and I-006-2-09 joins T2.3's verified set. Two further stale claims surfaced in that same sentence and are fixed with it: it credited the suite with the Appendix B Table 1 vectors alone where the suite transcribes all three published RFC 8785 vector sets (the §3.2.2 sample document's §3.2.4 bytes and the §3.2.3 property-sorting order join it), and it described nothing that pins the canonical eleven-member projection even though its own `Verifies invariant:` line claims I-006-1-03 — the set-equality is derived against `EventEnvelopeSchema` rather than transcribed, and now says so. The claim is deliberately SCOPED to the own-property tree and does not close the two bypasses the module documents in its own JSDoc: neither walk invokes `toJSON`, so a shallow object whose `toJSON()` returns a deeper or ill-formed tree clears both and is serialized anyway (verified empirically), and a non-idempotent getter puts the walks and the serializer on different trees. An invariant contradicting the module's own residuals would be worse than no invariant; the scope holds because neither bypass is reachable on the untrusted CP-006-3 path, where every value arrives from `JSON.parse` and therefore carries no `toJSON` and no accessors. No §Cross-Plan Amendments entry is owed — every edit here is Plan-006 editing its own file, so there is no cross-plan touch and no Housekeeping Exception to invoke. Status unchanged (`approved`): an invariant added to an in-flight phase on the I-006-2-08 precedent one day earlier — a mint plus a join to an existing task's verified set, adding no task, phase, Required ADR, or cross-plan obligation.
- 2026-07-27 — **`occurred_at_not_canonical` added as the thirteenth `failureMode`; I-006-2-10 minted; T2.1 exports `isCanonicalOccurredAt` (code + spec + plan).** Codex round 2 on PR #256 surfaced a tamper-evidence hole and the re-derivation found it wider than reported. `canonicalizeEvent` normalizes exactly ONE canonical member — `occurredAt` — and passes every other through byte-for-byte, so `daemon_signature` commits to that column's INSTANT rather than to its stored bytes: an at-rest respelling into another spelling of the same instant (`+00:00` or `-05:00` for `Z`, appended trailing zeros) re-canonicalizes identically, keeps hash AND signature green, and drops the row out of the lexical date-range scans the audit walk itself uses. Two `post-shred-verify.test.ts` controls pinned the attack green and asserted it was acceptable; their prose is corrected and their assertions deliberately left alone, because those rows genuinely do verify and `verifyRow` is not the layer that catches this. **The verdict is a new enum value rather than a reused one.** `hash_mismatch` was the tempting arm and is wrong — the hash genuinely matches — and `Spec-006 §Audit Integrity (audit_integrity)`'s own 2026-07-26 reasoning for splitting `signature_placeholder` out of `signature_mismatch` is that conflating verdicts pages the wrong on-call. Extension is additive-MINOR per `ADR-018 §Decision` #8, the same sanction the four post-compaction modes and `signature_placeholder` took. Paired `failurePath: 'signature'` — that field names the GUARANTEE that failed, the signature binding the stored bytes, not the column the defect occupies. Two precedents already bound a `failureMode` to that same path and this round follows both: `Security Architecture §Verification Rules` rule 4's `stub_scalar_mismatch` is the nearest sibling in KIND — a scalar-column defect that survives signature verification, which is why the spec text cites it as the model — and `signature_placeholder`'s own 2026-07-26 ratification inside `Spec-006 §Audit Integrity (audit_integrity)` is the precedent for stating the assignment in the spec rather than leaving implementations to infer one, so this pairing is stated there too. **Compacted rows were the half the first pass got wrong.** I-006-3-03 check (c) byte-binds `occurred_at` to the signed projection, which catches a respelling applied AFTER compaction, but `Spec-006 §Compacted Event Format` preserves the original timestamp verbatim — so a row respelled while LIVE has the bad spelling copied into the projection, signed into the stub bytes, and byte-equal to its column forever. Compaction launders the live-path defect into signed bytes, making the live-path hole permanent; the new check runs on both retention classes for that reason. T2.1 exports the predicate and deliberately emits no verdict (a verdict from Phase 2 would be T2 code deciding a T4.1 question); T4.1 is the registered consumer. Never-throwing is a contract, not style: T4.1 walks a range, and one throw would abort the walk and silence audit of the whole tail. Surfaces trued up: the enum's four live count sites (`§Target Areas`, the Phase-4 summary, the T4.1 heading, the T4.1 `Provides:` union), T2.1's `Provides:` and both tasks' `Verifies invariant:` lines, `Security Architecture §Verification Rules` rule 2, and `signer.ts`'s subset comment — whose "twelve-value … the other nine" re-derives to thirteen and ten, and whose "TWO different reasons" becomes three, the new mode being per-row across BOTH retention classes rather than range-level or compacted-only. Doc-and-code, no cross-plan touch and no Housekeeping Exception to invoke: `security-architecture.md` carries no ownership-map row and was amended by this plan's own 2026-07-26 `signature_placeholder` round for the same reason. Status unchanged (`approved`) on that same one-day-old precedent — an additive-MINOR enum value plus an invariant on an in-flight phase, adding no task, phase, Required ADR, or cross-plan obligation.
- 2026-07-27 — **CP-006-11 minted for T2.7's `DaemonSigningKeySealer` seal boundary; three self-containment claims trued up (doc-only plus one code comment; no behavioural change).** Codex round 2 on PR #256 called the T2.7 deliverable "unusable outside tests" because it reads as promising a self-contained OS-keystore-sealed implementation — and the quoted plan text was the defect, not the code. T2.7 ships `OsKeystoreSealedDaemonSigningKeySource` (the class exists and keeps its name) but the SEALING is an injected `DaemonSigningKeySealer` and the module carries no keystore code, because **no document specifies the byte format of `daemon_signing_keys.sealed_private_key`**: `Spec-022 §Daemon Master Key` covers the MASTER key's custody and Plan-022 covers the `participant_keys.encrypted_key_blob` wrap, neither this column, and minting a third format inside Plan-006 would pre-commit every later reader of it — a master-key-rotation re-wrap included — on a guess. The seam was real and correct; what was missing was its registration. **New CP-006-11** binds Plan-022 Tier 5 to ship the implementation AND specify the format, and carries the two things the interface can request but not enforce: the `sessionId` AAD binding (an implementation that ignores the argument still satisfies the types; `read`'s public-key derivation check is a backstop, not a substitute, since an AEAD that will not open never produces key material at all) and composition-root injection of the real sealer with a runtime assertion against the fake outside tests. Three surfaces asserted self-containment and all three are corrected the same way — **the no-tier-inversion CONCLUSION is kept and only its PREMISE moves**, from "no Plan-022 dependency" to "the dependency runs through an injected interface", which is what keeps `@napi-rs/keyring` out of the import graph so no native binding reaches the append path's consumers on CI legs where `Spec-023 §Native Keystore` requires a headless box with no Secret Service to refuse rather than silently fall back. The 2026-05-28 user ratification at §Open Questions is preserved verbatim and marked superseded in part rather than rewritten — it records what was decided then. Reciprocal registered on Plan-022 (§Preconditions + §Target Areas) for the reason `Plan-007 §Cross-Plan Obligations` CP-007-8 states: one-directional registration makes the other plan's reviewers reverse-search. Surfaces trued up: T2.7's `Provides:` and `Spec coverage:` lines, the §Open Questions ratification, dep-map §1's `daemon_signing_keys` row, §2's `src/events/` row (both cells — the sealing attribution and the consumes-via-imports list Plan-022 now joins), and both §3 rows; `signing-key-source.ts`'s "What is NOT here" header now cites the CP id instead of describing an unregistered seam. Cross-plan touch under the Housekeeping Exception, recorded in full at §Cross-Plan Amendments with criteria (a)–(e) re-derived — (a) differs from both CP-006-3 rounds, which could say no code existed at the seam, where here T2.7 shipped it in this PR and (a) holds only because nothing executable moved. Status unchanged (`approved`) on both plans: this names an obligation the shipping code already created rather than introducing one.
- 2026-07-27 — **`pii_ciphertext_digest_unbound` added as the fourteenth `failureMode`; I-006-2-11 minted; T2.4 exports `isCiphertextDigestBound`; CP-006-12 registered; a Spec-006/Spec-022 shred-mechanism contradiction corrected.** Codex round 3 on PR #256 alleged that nothing verifies `pii_payload` against its signed digest. It is right, and the class is the one I-006-2-10 closed a day earlier: **a signature committing to a value nothing ever compares back.** The digest has been minted and signed since T2.4 shipped; [Security Architecture §Verification Rules](../architecture/security-architecture.md#verification-rules) enumerated four checks and none recomputed it, so substituting, truncating, or deleting the ciphertext at rest left `row_hash` and `daemon_signature` both green. Finding the second instance took sweeping for the class rather than fixing the reported instance — which is the durable lesson from this round. **The corpus disagreed with itself about crypto-shred, and resolving that is what made the rule shippable.** Spec-006's `event.shredded` census row and §Canonical Serialization Rules bullet both said shredding ZEROES the ciphertext column; [Spec-022 §Shred Fan-Out](../specs/022-data-retention-and-gdpr.md#shred-fan-out) Path 1 — the owner — destroys the `participant_keys` row and overwrites nothing, and the [GDPR Manual Erasure Runbook](../operations/gdpr-manual-erasure-runbook.md) issues no `UPDATE` against `session_events` at all, its Path-1 verification step spot-checking that a previously-readable `pii_payload` no longer DECRYPTS, which presupposes the ciphertext is still there. Six further Spec-022 statements agree with the owner. Both Spec-006 sentences were a consumer overstating another spec's mechanism and are corrected. That resolution is load-bearing rather than cosmetic: under the owner's mechanism a shredded row's bytes still hash to its digest, so **the check needs no shred-state carve-out** — the carve-out the review asked for ("continue to skip after the column is cleared") would have been wrong, and the one state where the column IS emptied is COMPACTION, which `Spec-006 §Compacted Event Format` NULLs while re-projecting without a digest. A single `pii_payload IS NOT NULL` guard therefore covers every state. **The predicate decides four, not two, and the fourth is the one a plain hash comparison cannot reach:** a digest whose ciphertext has been removed at rest, where the signature verifies green over a commitment that outlived its subject — evidence destruction. It is a POSTCONDITION of the signature check rather than a precondition, since the digest lives inside the bytes that check authenticates, and it fails closed on a `BLOB`-affinity `string` rather than letting noble's `abytes` throw inside T4.1's range walk. Sited beside `embedCiphertextDigest` so mint and check cannot drift apart again. **CP-006-12** binds Plan-022 Tier 5 to preserve the bytes or amend the rule in the same change — the collision is the point, since an unwritten rule lets a future zeroing land silently. Surfaces trued up: the enum's four live count sites, `signer.ts`'s subset comment (thirteen→fourteen, "the other ten"→eleven, and THREE reasons→four, the new mode being the only one that is BOTH a green-verdict finding and outside what `verifyRow` is handed), T2.4's `Provides:` + `Verifies invariant:`, T4.1's heading, `Provides:` and `Verifies invariant:`, and Security Architecture rule 2 + its verifier-roles paragraph (where THREE post-compaction shapes stays THREE on purpose — the digest binding is a live-row fourth that compaction retires, there being no ciphertext left to bind). Also in this round, from the same review: T3.1 gains the **precondition** that Plan-001's `SessionService.append` be retired or guarded and Plan-003's shipped emitter re-pointed before any durable writer is wired — "T3.1 is the sole durable writer" is a sequencing obligation, not a property the tree has — and Plan-001's placeholder-convention bullet is corrected from forecasting that to stating the verified state (no dependents, no `bin`, `private` and unreleased, every `openDatabase` caller under `__tests__/`). Two further round-3 findings are declined with evidence rather than accepted: refusing `-00:00` (RFC 9557 §2.2, Standards Track, `Updates: 3339`, reassigns the unknown-local-offset meaning to `Z` itself, so the fold is now the spelling the standard prescribes — a golden vector and a rationale comment are added so the finding cannot recur), and routing the Spec-006 amendment through a separate doc-first PR (the 2026-05-28 precedent recorded under §Post-Compaction Integrity is itself a P1 review finding amending an approved spec mid-review, twice in one day; the genuine residual — the thirteenth arm lacking the provenance marking the twelfth carries — is fixed). Status unchanged (`approved`) on that same precedent: additive-MINOR enum values plus an invariant on an in-flight phase.
- 2026-07-27 — **T2.1's THIRD refusal (callable `toJSON`) shipped; I-006-2-09's closure claim widened from the own-property tree to the serialized tree; the entry point's refusal count re-derived to three-of-which-two-bind.** Codex round 4 on PR #256 reported the `toJSON` bypass that I-006-2-09 had documented as an open residual one day earlier, and the correct response was to CLOSE it rather than re-state the scope. `assertNoToJsonOverride` refuses any value carrying a callable `toJSON` at any depth, testing `typeof node.toJSON === "function"` down the whole prototype chain rather than `Object.hasOwn` — an inherited `toJSON` diverts the serializer exactly as an own one does — and it runs SECOND, between the depth ceiling and well-formedness. **Both positions are correctness constraints, not diagnostic preferences.** Depth stays first because neither walk behind it carries cycle detection or a bound of its own; the `toJSON` refusal must precede well-formedness because sited any later it would be inspecting a tree the well-formedness guard had already been diverted away from. **It REFUSES rather than inspecting the diverted output, and non-determinism is what decides that.** A `toJSON` is arbitrary caller code re-invoked on every canonicalization, so one value can serialize to different bytes at signing time and at verification time — fatal for a protocol whose verification step re-canonicalizes a row and compares bytes — and a guard that walked the `toJSON` RESULT would still have to assume the serializer later saw that same result. Depth-ceiling evasion and §3.2.2.2 evasion are the second and third motivations, both subsumed by refusing the carrier. Counts re-derived from the set rather than incremented: refusal families five → **six** (I-006-2-09's opening, T2.1's `Verifies invariant:` enumeration, T2.3's `Verifies invariant:` parenthetical); getter invocations on the surviving accessor residual five → **six** per object member through `canonicalizeJson`, all measured; and the generic entry point now carries **three** refusals of which exactly **two** are CP-006-3-bound — different numbers, labelled as such at every surface stating either (T2.1's `Provides:`, I-006-2-09, CP-006-3, this entry). Two claims flip outright. The library's `Circular reference detected` is now SHADOWED in both shapes that could reach it — an own-property cycle reports as depth exhaustion, a `toJSON`-only cycle is refused before the serializer runs — so it survives only through the accessor residual; and the third over-refusal shape I-006-2-09 listed (a `toJSON` returning a subset, leaving the surplus checked but unserialized) is DELETED as unreachable, its carrier now being refused outright. **The refusal adds NO third CP-006-3 obligation on Plan-027**, and the ground is auditable rather than asserted: Spec-024 bodies reach `canonicalizeJson` through `JSON.parse`, whose output can carry no function-valued member — a wire `{"toJSON":"x"}` parses to a STRING, and `"__proto__"` parses as an ordinary own data property — so the predicate is unsatisfiable on parsed input. Its KIND is a third one besides POLICY and RFC-MUST: it enforces the DETERMINISM the integrity protocol assumes. **That argument is scoped to the parsed-INTAKE path, and CP-006-3 now says so**, because the counterexample is real rather than hypothetical: a sender-side `request_body_hash` over a body Plan-027 CONSTRUCTS instead of parsing (an object literal carrying a `Date` or a `Buffer`) sits outside the argument's reach and would meet a live refusal. Whether that owes a fourth reciprocal is left as a named question for the Spec-024 intake-surface census rather than answered by assumption from this side. **One cross-plan consequence is CLOSED in this round rather than booked as debt.** Two mirror surfaces restated the old count — `Plan-027 §Preconditions` as "bounded by two refusals that differ in kind", and `cross-plan-dependencies.md` §3's Plan-027 row as "two refusals of different kinds" — and both are corrected in place under the Housekeeping Exception, with the §Cross-Plan Amendments (e) entry below. The correction deliberately carries two different numbers: the entry point holds THREE refusals, exactly TWO bind Plan-027, and Plan-027's three reciprocal obligations are UNTOUCHED. No Plan-027 obligation moves, because the `toJSON` predicate is unsatisfiable on the `JSON.parse` output a dispatch body arrives as — a reader who took only "three" from the count would wrongly conclude a third rejection reason is owed, which is why both mirrors now state the non-obligation explicitly rather than leaving it inferable. Status unchanged (`approved`) on the I-006-2-08 / I-006-2-09 precedent: an invariant amended on an in-flight phase, adding no task, phase, Required ADR, or cross-plan obligation.
- 2026-07-27 — **`pii_owner_stamp_unbound` added as the fifteenth `failureMode`; I-006-2-12 minted; T2.4 projects and exports the PII owner stamp; T3.1 gains the durable stamp column; CP-006-12 widened to a second column; I-006-2-05's scope clarified in place.** Codex round 4 on PR #256 found the third instance of the class the two rounds before it closed — **a value the signature commits to that nothing ever compares back** — except this one was worse, because the value was not committed to at all: `daemon_signature` bound WHICH ciphertext bytes a row held (I-006-2-11) and never WHOSE they were. T2.4's codec now projects the PII owner's participant id into the signed `payload` under `PII_PARTICIPANT_ID_PAYLOAD_KEY`, so the stamp is inside the canonical bytes, and exports `isPiiOwnerStampBound` for the read side. **What the binding buys is post-shred attribution, and that is why it is not discharged by the digest beside it.** Once `Spec-022 §Shred Fan-Out` Path 1 destroys the per-participant key, no decrypt can ever re-attribute the retained ciphertext, so the stamp becomes the sole surviving evidence of whose data the row held; a tampered stamp cannot stop the key deletion, but it corrupts the SCOPE SELECTOR instead, falsifying the `affectedSessionIds[]` / `piiPayloadsCleared` an `event.shredded` row retains indefinitely as compliance evidence. It is likewise not redundant with `actor`, the rebuttal the design invites: `actor` names who EMITTED the row, the stamp names whose key SEALED the ciphertext, and they diverge exactly where the divergence is expensive — an agent-emitted row (`actor` = an `agent_id`) or a system-emitted one (`actor` NULL) can still carry a participant's PII. **I-006-2-05 is CLARIFIED in place, not amended and not superseded, and it keeps its id** — the deliberate difference from how this plan handled `signature_placeholder` and the two 2026-07-27 modes before it. That invariant holds out the PII partition's CONTENT (plaintext and ciphertext, which is why it reads "not raw ciphertext"), never the identifiers that reference it, and it could not have been a rule against identifiers: `actor` is one of the canonical eleven members and has signed participant ids since the canonical form shipped. Recording the scope where the invariant lives is what stops the question being reopened. **Provider/consumer split for the third time:** T2.4 exports a pure, total, never-throwing, four-state predicate and emits no verdict; T4.1 runs it as a POSTCONDITION of a green `verifyRow` — the claim lives inside the bytes that check authenticates — and reports `pii_owner_stamp_unbound` with `failurePath: 'signature'`. **Two dispositions are recorded rather than papered over — one OPEN, one SET but not yet final.** The stamp COLUMN does not exist yet: `Spec-006 §Canonical Serialization Rules` assigns it to T3.1's additive migration, no Plan-006 task row owned it, and T3.1's `Provides:` now does — the same orphaned-artifact repair this log applied to `EVENT_ENVELOPE_SEQUENCE_MAX` and to the 0005 migration, made this time BEFORE the artifact ships rather than after. And the COMPACTED-row disposition splits three ways rather than being one open question. The signed CLAIM always retires — settled — the stub projection replacing `payload` wholesale with a field set carrying no PII members. The durable COLUMN is SET to cleared by `Spec-006 §Compacted Event Format`'s amendment this round, on two stated grounds (one PII partition in two halves, and Path 1's scope selector reads the ciphertext column), recorded as a disposition rather than entered in that section's removal list only because the column does not exist yet — T3.1's migration confirms it by creating the column and naming it there. That set resolution is what the predicate assumes and what a stub projection carrying no `pii_participant_id` matches; should T3.1 resolve the other way and RETAIN the stamp, the fix is caller-side scoping to `retention_class IS NULL` at T4.1 — never softening the either-alone-is-unbound state, which is the evidence-destruction case the check exists to catch. **The enum count sites are SIX, not four.** The 2026-07-27 rounds that added the thirteenth and fourteenth modes both said "the enum's four live count sites" and both undercounted: `§Target Areas`, the Phase-4 summary, the T4.1 heading and the T4.1 `Provides:` union are four, and I-006-2-10's and I-006-2-11's `Spec-006 §Audit Integrity (audit_integrity)` cite parentheticals each carry a spelled-out count too. All six are re-derived to fifteen here, and `§Target Areas`' parenthetical additionally completes its enumeration — it named six of the added modes and silently omitted `pii_ciphertext_digest_unbound`, so bumping the number without the instance would have shipped a list naming six of fifteen. **CP-006-12 gains a second column rather than a sibling CP row**, its premise having always been column-agnostic (Path 1 "overwrites no column") while only its obligation named `pii_payload`; the widened obligation is unchanged in KIND, since Path 1 already overwrites nothing, and Plan-022's own §Preconditions checkbox — the surface the obligation's CONSUMER actually reads, a Tier-5 implementer deciding what Path 1 may touch reading their own precondition rather than this plan's CP row — takes the same widening in THIS round rather than an ungated later one, recorded in the §Cross-Plan Amendments entry below. Housekeeping riding this entry: T2.5's row attributed its `pii_payload = NULL` control to "(Path 1)" — the identical stale mechanism this PR corrected in Spec-006 and `security-architecture.md` — and the attribution is corrected while the assertions stay untouched, the test being a legitimate and strictly stronger exclusion control that matches what compaction does. **A §Cross-Plan Amendments entry IS owed for the work in this entry** and is recorded below — for a Spec-022 correction rather than for anything above: `Spec-022 §Signature Safety Under Shred`'s "Why This Matters" paragraph still described a shred as leaving "the ciphertext bytes … zero", contradicting Path 1 in its own file. Status unchanged (`approved`) on the precedent set twice the same day: an additive-MINOR enum value plus an invariant on an in-flight phase, adding no phase, no Required ADR, and no new cross-plan obligation.

- 2026-07-29 — CP-006-7 two-leg amendment (PR #274): Status flipped `approved → review` and the §Preconditions audit checkbox re-opened — T4.10 adds a durable control-plane table (`daemon_signing_public_keys`), mandatory registration behavior (producer-bound register-once + different-key refusal), and a new cross-plan edge (CP-003-5): the NEW-contract-surface shape that re-earns `approved` through a targeted readiness-audit delta (the Plan-014 2026-07-08 rule; campaign B17/B22 precedents). The delta's scope is T4.10 alone — every task the 2026-05-28 audit certified stays audit-covered — and on landing it re-checks the box and restores `approved`; until then no lane-1 code PR (Phase 3 included) dispatches. The leg-B carrier was re-designed inside this PR's own review round: Codex round 1 returned six verified findings, and the original attach-request-field + attachment-column carrier was withdrawn on ADR-018 §Decision #7 strict-parse-skew and Spec-022 §Shred Fan-Out erasure grounds — full adjudication recorded at CP-006-7.
- 2026-08-01 — **Codex PR #274 round 5 (four findings, all adjudicated): the sixteenth `failureMode` `signing_key_slot_conflict` minted; the register transaction gains the `attachmentId` binding; `key_reuse_detected` redefined to the reachable cross-identity collision; the roster hydration claims grounded on primary sources; the Plan-018-status objection answered on precedent.** (1) Cross-node key planting (P1, CONFIRMED): both register-path ownership predicates resolve at PARTICIPANT granularity — the verified PASETO `sub` and the attachment's `participant_id` both name the OWNER, never the calling daemon, and V1 defines no node-identity credential — so a compromised sibling daemon of the same participant could register a key it controls under another of that participant's `(session_id, node_id)` identities in one silent call. A `registrar_jkt` evidence column was drafted and REJECTED on three grounds: a per-attempt ephemeral DPoP key has no evidentiary value (each retry mints a fresh token + proof per the CP-006-13 credential-provider contract); persisting registrar auth material breaches the table's PUBLIC-keys-only erasure rationale and would owe `Spec-022 §Shred Fan-Out` Path-2 + GDPR-runbook dispositions the design exists to avoid; and it falsifies the no-auth-material quantifiers at the shared-postgres-schema and security-architecture surfaces. Adopted instead: (a) the register request presents the attach response's server-minted `attachmentId` (`runtime_node_attachments.id` — returned only in that response, republished nowhere) and the transaction requires equality on the existing level-3 `FOR SHARE` attachment read — zero new locks, zero snapshot skew — converting the silent one-call plant into a loud two-call usurpation that must first rewrite the victim's attach row (a mitigation rather than a closure; the [cross-plan-dependencies.md §5](../architecture/cross-plan-dependencies.md#5-canonical-build-order) V1.1 Plan-018/Plan-003 node-identity leg is the closure — the residual, stated honestly at CP-006-7 and security-architecture.md: a same-participant sibling plant remains possible and, under register-once with no rotation ceremony, permanent); (b) the genuine daemon's different-key 409 now lands as the durable `audit_integrity_failed` `failureMode: 'signing_key_slot_conflict'` (additive-MINOR per `ADR-018 §Decision` #8; `failurePath: 'signature'`; no `offendingSeq`; the first mode whose emitter is not a read-side verifier — the enum stays ONE wire surface while the emitter set widens to T4.10's registrar conflict handler, so the T4.1 verifier emits fifteen of sixteen; the append rides T3.1's `EventLogService` — the same composition root that starts the registrar injects it, and T3.1 joins T4.10's `Depends:` line accordingly). (2) `key_reuse_detected` (P2, accepted with its blast radius corrected): the shipped description named a collision "with a prior rotated-out key" — a condition V1 can neither reach nor evidence, since register-once means no rotation history exists and no store retains a retired key (`artifact_encryption_keys` shows the corpus CAN model retirement and this design declined). Redefined at `Spec-006 §Audit Integrity (audit_integrity)` to the reachable predicate — one Ed25519 public key under MORE THAN ONE `(session_id, node_id)` identity — with the detection SUBSTRATE pinned at T4.2: the key stores, never the event stream (the canonical field set carries no `NodeId` and no key material), the envelope subscription being the sweep trigger only; `rotationInvariantViolated: 'refuse_on_rotation'` stays verbatim and I-006-4-03 stands unchanged. Three disjoint refusal/verdict conditions now read cleanly: same pair + different key → the 409 conflict (I-006-4-08); a signature that fails → `signature_mismatch`; same key under two identities → `key_reuse_detected` (I-006-4-03). (3) The roster paragraph's three hydration claims (P1) now carry content-verified primary sources at the api-payload registry: `pg` delegates result parsing to `pg-types`, whose `lib/textParsers.js` registers OID 1184 to `postgres-date`; PGlite's `types.ts` parses `TIMESTAMPTZ` via `new Date(x)`; and Zod §ISO datetimes validates strings — so a driver-hydrated `Date` fails the wire schema, which is what forces the service-side `toIsoString` normalization the round-4 entry pinned. (4) The Plan-018-at-`review` objection (P1) is answered with reasons plus one clarifying edit rather than a design change: the status-promotion gate binds the docs a PR implements AGAINST and the surfaces it consumes at its tier, not forward-declared CONSUMERS of an interface it ships — the exact shape PR #256 merged under (2026-07-27, with cross-referenced Plan-013 at `review` since 2026-07-20) — and T4.10's dispatch is already gated on this plan's own restoring re-audit; the activation-posture sentence (Tier 4 ships the full surface fixture-authenticated, production activation dormant behind the Plan-018 Tier-5 composition-root wiring) is recorded at T4.10's CREDENTIAL clause. Surfaces trued up in this round: the enum's live count sites re-derived to the emits-fifteen-of-sixteen form (§Target Areas, the Phase-4 summary, T4.1's heading + `Provides:` union, the I-006-2-10/-11/-12 cite parentheticals, and the shipped `signer.ts` / `canonicalizer.ts` subset comments — the former's excluded-modes taxonomy gaining a FIFTH reason, a mode that is not a read-side verification outcome at all), the registrar's conflict and precondition clauses at §Target Areas and T4.10, S2's signature-test bullet (the unreachable "rotated-out key" arm replaced by the cross-identity collision), CP-006-7's §5-objection paragraph (does-not-transfer re-scoped to transfers-in-part), api-payload-contracts.md (predicate + collapse list + request fence + roster citations), error-contracts.md (the `attachmentId` refusal arm), security-architecture.md (refusal-at-both-ends + the durable alarm + the honest residual), Spec-006 (the corrected `key_reuse_detected` cell with dated correction note, the sixteenth-mode prose on `audit_integrity_failed`, the substrate-clarified Sigstore paragraph), and Plan-003's CP-003-5 clause (the read-only `attachmentId` coupling). Status unchanged (`review` — the T4.10 targeted readiness-audit delta remains the restoring gate).
- 2026-08-01 — **PR #276 (the PR #274 round-6 follow-up): the `audit_integrity_failed` payload discriminated on `failureMode`.** Round 6 (post-cap — the session's review-round cap was consumed at round 5, so its findings were adjudicated and routed, not fixed in-PR) confirmed one P1: the `Spec-006 §Audit Integrity (audit_integrity)` Payload Extension required `treeSize`/`expectedRootHash`/`observedRootHash` on every mode while the sixteenth mode's registrar emitter verifies no range — an unsatisfiable contract for the mode PR #274 minted. Researched before fixing (the session's websearch-first directive): Zod's discriminated-union documentation (an arm's discriminator may correspond to "some literal value or set of values, usually `z.enum()`") and the spec's own RFC 9162 precedent (each proof shape is a separate structure carrying only its applicable fields). Adopted: one event type, one wire schema, the payload DISCRIMINATED on `failureMode` — the fifteen read-side verifier modes keep the REQUIRED triple, the `signing_key_slot_conflict` arm carries `failureMode`/`failurePath: 'signature'`/`detail` only. Making the triple optional everywhere was REJECTED (it stops enforcing the roots on the verifier modes a verifier must never silently omit); a dedicated event type was REJECTED (it would ripple the registered type census for no semantic gain — the mode shares the family's category, `failurePath` vocabulary, and operator routing). Surfaces: the Spec-006 fence + sixteenth-mode prose (a second same-day dated amendment to the same cell, spec stays `approved` on the PR #274 round-1 F5 corrective-amendment precedent) + §Session binding's third case (the registrar arm carries the refused registration's real `session_id`; `verifierNodeId` is the refused daemon's own NodeId; `anchorId` absent at emission), T4.1's payload-schema clause (ships both arms as one discriminated union, the verifier arm derived via the Zod enum `.exclude()`), T4.10's conflict clause + test enumeration (the registrar-built event round-trips the registrar arm). The remaining round-6 items stay on the T4.10 targeted readiness-audit delta's docket — the delta now VERIFIES this repair rather than authoring it. Round 2 (same day) scoped the round-5 external-visibility claim on its three carrying surfaces (the sixteenth-mode fence prose, §Session binding's third case, and the `api-payload-contracts.md` registry paragraph): the alarm row is signed with the very key the roster refused, so roster-resolving external verifiers see it only as one more `signature_mismatch` — the durable append is scoped to local replay, the anchor-covered post-repair forensic record, and operator surfacing, and the independently verifiable trust path stays on the T4.10 audit-delta docket as the restated PR #274 round-6 residual. Status unchanged (`review`).

- 2026-08-02 — **PR #281 (the third routed follow-up): the T3.1 ingest-halt seam declared; Status flipped `approved → review`.** T3.1 now declares the I-006-4-03 append-gate seam it consults — `IngestHaltSource` + the vacuous `NeverHaltedIngestHaltSource` default in the new Plan-006-owned `ingest-halt-source.ts`, `append()` refusing a halted session with the typed `daemon.ingest_halted` error before canonicalization. Census + mirrors re-derived in the same PR: README 21 → 20 `approved` / 5 → 6 `review`, CLAUDE.md's reopened-exceptions sentence, and the dependency map's §5 preamble + NS-37/NS-42 dated tails. Codex round 3 (2 P1 + 1 P2, all accepted — the session cap's final round): the lock helper re-homed to the new dependency-leaf `session-append-lock.ts` (round 2's import re-introduced the eager-ESM-cycle class one layer down — `event-log-service.ts` value-imports the sibling's `NeverHaltedIngestHaltSource` default while the sibling would import the lock back; the T1.12 `event-core.ts` hoist shape, with the dep-map §2 row and Plan-004's T3.7 lock-home pin re-pointed in the same pass); T4.2 sweep publication made per-session single-flight with coalesced rerun, closing the stale-sweep `clear`-over-newer-`halt` freshness race the append lock cannot order; and the pre-binding deferred alarm's emitting sweep now fires from T4.10's `recordDelivery` post-commit hook, so a halted single-session daemon emits on the delivery itself rather than awaiting unrelated traffic or restart — each with its T4.2/T3.5 test arms.
- 2026-08-02 — **PR #279 Codex round 3: T3.3's credential presentation corrected to the RFC 9449 `DPoP` authorization scheme.** The T3.3 contract and its §Files mirror described `DaemonCredentialMaterial`'s access token as a PASETO v4.public `Authorization` bearer beside the RFC 9449 `DPoP` proof; [RFC 9449 §7.1](https://www.rfc-editor.org/rfc/rfc9449#section-7.1) requires a DPoP-bound token to present under the `DPoP` scheme (`Authorization: DPoP <token>`) — a conforming resource server rejects the Bearer form, and a lax one accepting it drops proof-of-possession enforcement. Corrected at every carrying surface in the same PR: this plan's T3.3 + §Files bullet, Plan-024 T-024-3B-3 + CP-024-5 + its §References PASETO line, `Spec-023 §Renderer → Shell → Control Plane (via Preload Bridge)`, and Plan-023's two main-process header clauses — with the complement swept (every other corpus `bearer` is a deliberately non-DPoP-bound scheme: relay subprotocol connect tokens, metrics/admin auth, driver-listener auth, MCP provider tokens, the local daemon IPC token). A presentation-scheme correction to the existing declared headers, not new contract surface (no status implication). Post-cap batch, same PR: the proof binding gains the [RFC 9449 §4.3](https://www.rfc-editor.org/rfc/rfc9449#section-4.3) `ath` claim (hash of the presented access token, required when a proof accompanies an access-token presentation) at T3.3 and at security-architecture.md's canonical proof shape — computed inside `mintForAttempt` from the token minted in the same call, so the interface signature is unchanged.

Scope note: the reconciliation tick below is NEW T4.2 design the review round forced, not something the targeted audit surfaced** — a restoring delta grew a timer because two shipped-hook contracts could not carry the correctness they had been given: both P1s were one class — a best-effort post-commit hook carrying sole responsibility for correctness (a T2.7 first-mint hook rejecting after the key row commits left the session unobserved until restart, since enumeration was boot-only; a T4.10 delivery trigger rejecting after `recordDelivery` commits stranded the deferred `key_reuse_detected` alarm, since the acknowledged delivery is never re-sent) — closed by ONE mechanism rather than two patches: a self-quiescing reconciliation tick that re-enumerates keyed sessions and re-triggers sweeps for sessions with deferred alarms, feeding the existing single-flight trigger, disarming when there is nothing to reconcile. Both hooks become latency optimizations; neither operation is held hostage. Plus: the error-contracts pii row no longer claims the `daemon.pii_split_ambiguous` census row characterizes the bypass rejection (that event signals a SUCCESSFUL containment fallback — the spec row merely draws the distinction), and the halted refusal's wire detail shape is pinned as `DaemonIngestHaltedDetailsSchema` (`{ sessionId }`, strict) surfacing as `data.fields.sessionId`, with T3.5 asserting the mapped envelope — a deliberate, reasoned divergence from the `runtimenode.*` code+message-only 409 convention, whose info-leak rationale does not reach a caller's own append target.
- 2026-08-03 — **PR #285 (T1.12 — the five `runtime_node.*` payload variants registered into `SessionEventSchema`): two Spec-006 touches — the `capabilities` gloss trued up to the shipped keyed map, and the `audit_integrity_failed` verifier arm completed with the verified range endpoints.** (a) **Gloss true-up (Phase-D round).** `Spec-006 §Runtime Node Lifecycle (runtime_node_lifecycle)`'s `runtime_node.registered` row carried an informal `capabilities[]` ARRAY gloss while every shipped carrier of that value is a keyed MAP — `RuntimeNodeAttachRequest.capabilities` is `z.record(z.string(), z.unknown())`, and T1.12's `RuntimeNodeRegisteredPayload` mirrors that schema VERBATIM so the attach payload round-trips into the event without a reshape — so the row now reads `base + {capabilities: Record<capabilityKey, details>, nodeVersion, platform}`. A gloss corrected to the code it describes rather than a shape change: no wire value moves and the array form never shipped. Recorded additively in the same pass: the section's base-shape line now states the REDUCED base `{sessionId?, nodeId, actor?}` that the two `runtime_node.capability_*` rows take, because T1.4's shipped rebind puts `CapabilityDetails` snapshots in their `previousState` / `newState` rather than `NodeState` values and the same-name base members would otherwise collide — the spec recording a distinction the shipped contracts already enforce. (b) **Codex round 1 F3 (P1, confirmed): the verifier arm gains `fromSeq` / `toSeq`.** I-006-4-01 has consumers dedupe these rows on `(verifierNodeId, fromSeq, toSeq, verifiedAt)` and T4.1's IdempotencyClass on the first three, and `audit_integrity_verified` has carried both endpoints as REQUIRED since it shipped — while the FAILED arm carried neither, leaving the specified key unconstructible on exactly the events an operator must not be paged for twice. Both are REQUIRED rather than optional: every verifier invocation HAS a request range, the whole-range modes (`log_file_missing`, `anchor_missing_for_compacted_range`) included, which is precisely why those modes may omit `offendingSeq` and still name the range they were asked to walk. The registrar arm stays range-less on its own 2026-08-01 reasoning — it verified no range, and requiring endpoints there would force exactly the fabrication the Merkle-triple arm split exists to prevent. **The residual is NAMED rather than closed:** the key's fourth member `verifiedAt` describes a COMPLETED verification and stays an `audit_integrity_verified` member, no authority asking the failed arm to carry it, so on this arm three key members are payload-resident and the fourth is the envelope's own `occurredAt` — which is why the key T4.1's `compensable` IdempotencyClass specifies for these rows is the three-member `(verifierNodeId, fromSeq, toSeq)`. Mirrored at the `api-payload-contracts.md` registry sketch and enforced in `AuditIntegrityFailedPayloadSchema` in this same PR. The round's other three findings landed entirely in `packages/contracts` and `api-payload-contracts.md` and touched no spec surface and no task row: F4 binds the nine `failureMode` → `failurePath` pairings [Security Architecture §Verification Rules](../architecture/security-architecture.md#verification-rules) already fixes, enforcing them at parse instead of narrating them (the six unfixed modes keep the full three-value latitude — pinning one would mint an authority that does not exist); F2 canonicalizes `sessionId` in the `key_reuse_detected` `observedIdentities` set key, where a case-variant duplicate failed OPEN, `nodeId` staying RAW because it is a bounded free-string brand rather than a UUID; F1 rewords the registrar arm's `anchorId` comment, which read as though the signed payload were later mutated. **Codex round 2 (2 P1 + 1 P2, all adjudicated REAL) rides the same PR and closes the gap F1 opened.** (1) The registrar arm now takes a REDUCED base with `anchorId` EXCLUDED rather than optional: F1's reworded comment called the field permanently absent while the arm still spread the full base, so an offered `anchorId` parsed green — a false anchor association persisted onto a row that is never compacted, never shredded, and never rewritten, and therefore permanent. The arm spreads the shared base and DROPS the one member, so later base-shape drift still reaches it, and `.strict()` is what converts the omission into a refusal rather than into silence. The two `runtime_node.capability_*` rows are the spec's reduced-base precedent, cited as such. (2) `VerifierFailureModeSchema`'s export annotation moves from the erasing `z.ZodType<VerifierFailureMode>` to the enum-typed form, because `.exclude()` lives on Zod's `ZodEnum` surface and T4.1's documented `VerifierFailureModeSchema.exclude(['signing_key_slot_conflict'])` derivation would have compiled inside `event.ts` — off the unannotated module-local twin — while failing at T4.1's own module boundary, the worst shape of type error, invisible to the file that ships the symbol. The twin is retired, the verifier arm derives from the exported symbol, and the suite exercises `.exclude()` across the module boundary exactly as T4.1 will. `VerifierFailurePathSchema` deliberately keeps the narrower `z.ZodType` annotation: no documented consumer derives from its enum surface. (3) T1.12's Files cell is trued up to the SHIPPED in-process cycle tripwire (`vi.resetModules()`, both entry orders, real-parse assertions, plus the `event-core.ts` import-set cause leg), the fresh-process form's Phase-C adjudication standing on its stated grounds and the Vite-SSR-versus-native-ESM residual named rather than left implicit. Status unchanged (`approved`): (a) is a gloss-to-shipped-code correction plus an additive record of the shipped T1.4 rebind, not scope growth, and (b) is two required members on an in-flight-phase payload whose schema ships FIRST in this same PR — so no wire payload predates it and no `ADR-018 §Decision` #8 bump is owed. Round 2's `anchorId` exclusion rides that SAME no-predecessor rationale, which is what keeps a structural NARROWING off the #8 MAJOR path; its annotation and cycle-tripwire legs are a type-surface fix and a test-mechanism true-up, neither reaching the wire. No touch in this PR adds a phase, a Required ADR, or a cross-plan obligation, so there is no README / CLAUDE.md census consequence.

- 2026-08-04 — **Merkle-construction gloss correction (doc-only true-up; no status implication).** T3.3's build surfaced a self-contradictory prescription in the canonical corpus: the `pending_anchor_uploads.merkle_root` DDL comment at [local-sqlite-schema.md §Audit Log Crypto Tables](../architecture/schemas/local-sqlite-schema.md#audit-log-crypto-tables-plan-006), `Spec-006 §Post-Compaction Integrity` step 2, and this plan's T3.3 Provides clause each specified "RFC 9162 §2.1 odd-leaf duplication" — but §2.1.1 of the cited RFC defines `MTH(D_n) = HASH(0x01 || MTH(D[0:k]) || MTH(D[k:n]))` with `k` the largest power of two smaller than `n`, which SPLITS the odd remainder and domain-separates leaves (`0x00`) from interior nodes (`0x01`) — the RFC stating that separation "is required to give second preimage resistance". Odd-leaf duplication is the Bitcoin construction, and it admits the CVE-2012-2459 root collapse (two entry lists, one root) that the RFC's shape-determined-by-leaf-count property exists to prevent. The citation and the wording could not both be honoured; resolved in favour of the cited RFC per user ratification — the citation was the intent, the gloss was the defect. All three surfaces reworded in place (zero net line change on each), and T3.3's `computeMerkleRoot` plus its `merkle-root.test.ts` conformance suite landed against §2.1.1 in the same PR. A FOURTH surface carrying a DIFFERENT wrong construction was found by the post-repair sweep and repaired in the same pass: [Security Architecture §Merkle Anchors (Control-Plane Witness)](../architecture/security-architecture.md#merkle-anchors-control-plane-witness) step 1 described plain `left‖right` concatenation with BLAKE3 "as the internal node-hash function", and its Precedent paragraph stated outright that "RFC 9162's leaf-prefix is omitted because this is an internal log, not a CT log" — the sharpest form of the defect, since that doc is what a verifier implementer reads for this protocol and the omission claim is not deployment-scope-dependent (the RFC ties the leaf/node prefix split to second preimage resistance). Step 1 now states the §2.1.1 construction including the empty-range refusal, and the Precedent paragraph carries a dated correction note; that surface too held line count (its two edited lines reworded in place), its only inbound line-anchored cite (BL-056's resolution record at `docs/archive/backlog-archive.md §BL-056: Resolve Desktop Renderer trust stance`, whose cited passages sit above the edited section) unaffected. With it, no surface in the corpus still describes a construction the code does not implement. **No status flip and no re-opened checkbox** — the correction removes a contradiction rather than minting contract surface: no task, phase, invariant, Required ADR, or cross-plan obligation changes, and the anchor's seven-column payload, its idempotency keys, and the anchor-before-compaction protocol are all untouched. Free to change because no production anchor had been signed and T4.1's verifier is unwritten, so no stored commitment depends on the superseded shape. Status unchanged (`approved`).

- 2026-08-04 — **`Spec-006 §Retention Windows` trigger-independence clarification (spec true-up; the cell-visible delta is the headline, adjudicated below; no status flip).** A third Phase-3 residual beside the two the same-day bootstrap-wiring amendment discharged: PR #287's compactor recorded this one in code and nowhere in the corpus, and it lands as its own amendment so the delta it states is a headline rather than a rider. The `Spec-006 §Retention Windows` Full-event-payloads row read "90 days or 50K events (whichever is more generous)", which as a per-row conjunction is a JOINT floor over both windows, while the shipped compactor compacts the UNION of the per-trigger candidate sets. Resolved in favour of the union on two authorities. Within Spec-006 itself, `Spec-006 §Event Compaction Policy`'s normative trigger definition — "Any one of the following triggers initiates compaction" — already stated union semantics, so the document carried BOTH readings and the summary cell was the drifted one. Outside it, [Spec-022 §Data Retention and Deletion Policy](../specs/022-data-retention-and-gdpr.md#data-retention-and-deletion-policy) records as VERIFIED that the compaction policy "compacts events older than 90 days", its 90-day `session_events.pii_payload` retention entry depending on the age trigger firing independently — under the conjunctive reading a session below 50,000 events retains full PII indefinitely, which that spec makes an ADR-triggering exception rather than the default, and the age trigger is dead code for every session (its candidate set a strict subset of the count trigger's), contradicting this plan's own "all three triggers fire independently" verification row. The cell now defers to the per-trigger Description columns, under a dated clarification recording the superseded reading. **The delta a cell-reader sees is stated rather than buried:** the superseded cell's literal reading protected rows this text admits, so a reader who took the summary cell as normative sees a widening of what compaction may destroy — but the section's own trigger definition never protected those rows, and the narrow reading was never admissible: it contradicted the same section's normative sentence and broke the Spec-022 obligation, so what a cell-reader loses is a defect's promise, not a contract's. **Status Flip adjudication, argued on semantics rather than heading location.** The case FOR flipping `review`: a retention floor is a data-destruction guarantee, and the literal cell reading protected rows this text now admits — a bare location test (the edit lands outside `Spec-006 §Required Behavior`, `Spec-006 §Acceptance Criteria`, and Depends On) would wave that delta through unexamined. The case AGAINST: the operative contract never moved — the same section's normative trigger sentence stated union semantics before and after the edit, the cell being a summary row in internal contradiction with its own section's normative text, so the amendment removes the contradiction rather than changing what the section requires; Spec-022's verified record shows consumers already relied on the union reading; and no stored row depends on the superseded reading, compaction never having run in production (the daemon-scope sentinel key is unprovisioned, per §Bootstrap Wiring). Adjudicated: **no flip** — contradiction removal under the readiness runbook's "narrowing of ambiguity" row, with the cell-reader's delta named here so the adjudication is made against the honest delta, not a softened one. Status unchanged (`approved`) on Spec-006, hence no README or CLAUDE.md census consequence.
