### Cross-Plan Amendments

<!-- Criterion-(e) home for every cross-plan touch this plan lands under the
     cross-plan-dependencies.md §Ownership Rule Housekeeping Exception. Append-only. -->

**2026-07-26 — CP-006-3 depth-ceiling obligations registered on Plan-027 (`docs/plans/027-cross-node-dispatch-and-approval.md` §Preconditions + the [cross-plan-dependencies.md §3](../architecture/cross-plan-dependencies.md#3-inter-plan-dependency-graph) Plan-027 row).**

CP-006-3 bounds the shared RFC 8785 entry point: `canonicalizeJson` refuses any value nesting containers past `CANONICAL_JSON_MAX_DEPTH` (64 as T2.1 ships it, module-local to `canonicalizer.ts` and not an exported symbol, so Plan-027 cannot read the bound at compile time) — a stack-overflow guard on the surface where untrusted dispatch bodies arrive. Two reciprocal obligations fall on Plan-027 as the Spec-024 implementer, and Plan-027 carried neither, nor named the CP id they descend from:

1. **A registered rejection reason for an over-deep `action_payload`.** The refusal is a plain throw at T2.1, so the dispatch intake path MUST catch it and map it, never surfacing the raw throw to a remote caller.
2. **Renegotiation with T2.1 rather than a fork.** A ceiling that proves too tight for a real capability payload is raised by agreement — a two-way door; forking the canonicalizer to escape it is the CP-006-3 violation the obligation exists to prevent.

This round writes both into `Plan-027 §Preconditions` — the `Plan-006 has registered all Spec-024 dispatch.* event types` checkbox, which now states the bound, the depth an `action_payload` starts at inside a dispatch body, and both obligations — and adds the `CP-006-3` id to the dep-map §3 Plan-027 row so the graph edge names the obligation it carries. No Plan-027 task, phase, invariant, CP row, or Required ADR changes, and Plan-027's Status is untouched.

**Cross-plan touch rationale (Housekeeping Exception, [cross-plan-dependencies.md §Ownership Rule](../architecture/cross-plan-dependencies.md#ownership-rule)):** (a) doc-only — governance text with zero runtime or behavioral change; neither plan has shipped code at this seam, and the amended checkbox is an unchecked precondition on an unexecuted plan; (b) first discoverable from the Plan-006 side that minted the ceiling — the depth bound is T2.1's and CP-006-3 is the only place the reciprocal obligation was ever written down, so Plan-027 could not surface a gap in an obligation it had never been handed; (c) sub-30-LOC and sub-day — one Preconditions checkbox rewritten in place plus one id added to a dep-map row, in a single dispatch; (d) one-time correction — Plan-027 retains ownership of its plan file and Plan-006 claims no ongoing edit right on it. **(e) is this block.** The §Ownership Rule's fifth criterion requires the exception be documented inline in the extending plan's §Cross-Plan Amendments section; this plan had none, and the 2026-07-26 §Notes entry above recorded the amendment in the Progress Log while saying so openly. This section is the missing (e) leg, and that entry now points here. Section placement + entry format follow the [Plan-009 §Cross-Plan Amendments](./009-repo-attachment-and-workspace-binding.md#cross-plan-amendments) precedent (an H3 under §Progress Log, after §Notes), joining [Plan-002 §Cross-Plan Amendments](./002-invite-membership-and-presence.md#cross-plan-amendments) and [Plan-005 §Cross-Plan Amendments](./005-provider-driver-contract-and-capabilities.md#cross-plan-amendments).

**Refs:** [Plan-027 §Preconditions](./027-cross-node-dispatch-and-approval.md#preconditions) (the amended checkbox), `Plan-006 §Cross-Plan Obligations` CP-006-3 (the depth-ceiling clause both obligations descend from), [cross-plan-dependencies.md §3 Plan-027 row](../architecture/cross-plan-dependencies.md#3-inter-plan-dependency-graph) (the graph edge the `CP-006-3` id joins).

**2026-07-27 — CP-006-3's SECOND refusal (RFC 8785 §3.2.2.2 Unicode well-formedness) registered on Plan-027 (`docs/plans/027-cross-node-dispatch-and-approval.md` §Preconditions + the [cross-plan-dependencies.md §3](../architecture/cross-plan-dependencies.md#3-inter-plan-dependency-graph) Plan-027 row).**

T2.1 shipped a second refusal on the shared generic entry point in this PR: `canonicalizeJson` refuses any string value or property name carrying an unpaired UTF-16 surrogate (`assertWellFormedStrings`), at any depth, property names included because RFC 8785 §3.2.2.2 legislates over both in one breath and §3.2.3 lex-sorts names into the byte order. It is neither optional nor a policy choice — §3.2.2.2 closes with a normative MUST ("occurrences of such data MUST cause a compliant JCS implementation to terminate with an appropriate error") that the pinned `canonicalize@3.0.0` does not honor: it escapes the surrogate through `JSON.stringify` and keeps going, emitting VALID JSON text that is not CONFORMING, so this module would otherwise brand, chain, and sign bytes no conforming verifier will reproduce. Reachable, not theoretical: `JSON.parse` materializes lone surrogates out of a well-formed wire document, and `EventEnvelopeSchema.safeParse` returns success for them in `actor`, `correlationId`, `causationId`, and in `payload` values and keys.

The 2026-07-26 entry above enumerated **two** reciprocal Plan-027 obligations and described the shared entry point as bounded by the depth ceiling ALONE. Both readings were correct as of that date and stand as written; this round extends the obligation count to **three** and the bounds to **two**, the second refusal having not existed when that entry was written. The added obligation is a **registered rejection reason for an ill-formed `action_payload`** — sibling to the over-deep one, and discharged the same way: both refusals are plain throws at T2.1, so both are caught and mapped on the dispatch intake path and neither is surfaced raw to a remote caller. Refusal order rides the same edit, because it is observable and Plan-027's two rejection paths depend on it: the depth ceiling is checked first — a correctness constraint rather than a diagnostic preference, since the depth walk is what makes the well-formedness walk terminate on cyclic input — so a body defective both ways reports the over-deep reason and never the other.

**The two refusals differ in KIND, and the 2026-07-26 renegotiation clause does not stretch over the new one.** The depth ceiling is Plan-006 POLICY: a module-local stack-overflow guard whose constant is a two-way door, which is exactly why CP-006-3 offers renegotiation as the sanctioned alternative to forking. The well-formedness refusal is RFC-MANDATED: there is nothing for T2.1 to renegotiate in either direction, and an implementer who read "renegotiate rather than fork" and applied it here would be asking Plan-006 to violate a normative MUST. All three live surfaces now label the pair `(i) policy ceiling` / `(ii) RFC-mandated refusal` and scope the renegotiation affordance to (i) explicitly, so the affordance can no longer be read as covering both. No Plan-027 task, phase, invariant, CP row, or Required ADR changes, and Plan-027's Status is untouched.

**Cross-plan touch rationale (Housekeeping Exception, [cross-plan-dependencies.md §Ownership Rule](../architecture/cross-plan-dependencies.md#ownership-rule)):** (a) the CROSS-PLAN touch is doc-only — one Plan-027 Preconditions checkbox plus one dep-map parenthetical, zero runtime or behavioral change on Plan-027's side, and the amended checkbox is still unchecked on an unexecuted plan. The ROUND is not code-free, and the 2026-07-26 entry's "neither plan has shipped code at this seam" is deliberately NOT re-asserted: Plan-006 T2.1 shipped `assertWellFormedStrings` in this PR, which is precisely what creates the registration obligation; (b) first discoverable from the Plan-006 side, and here necessarily so — the refusal was minted by T2.1 in this PR, so Plan-027 could not surface a gap in an obligation that did not exist until now; (c) sub-30-LOC and sub-day, measured on the cross-plan footprint — one checkbox rewritten in place plus one dep-map parenthetical extended, in a single dispatch (the CP-006-3 bullet and the two Progress Log entries are Plan-006 editing its own file, not cross-plan touches); (d) one-time correction — Plan-027 retains ownership of its plan file and Plan-006 claims no ongoing edit right on it. **(e) is this block**, appended per this section's append-only rule rather than folded into the 2026-07-26 entry it extends. Status unchanged (`approved`).

**Refs:** [Plan-027 §Preconditions](./027-cross-node-dispatch-and-approval.md#preconditions) (the amended checkbox, now carrying three obligations), `Plan-006 §Cross-Plan Obligations` CP-006-3 (the amended bullet, which now names both refusals and scopes renegotiation to the ceiling), [cross-plan-dependencies.md §3 Plan-027 row](../architecture/cross-plan-dependencies.md#3-inter-plan-dependency-graph) (the graph edge whose `CP-006-3` parenthetical now carries both refusals), and the 2026-07-26 entry above (the depth-ceiling cascade this one mirrors and extends).

**2026-07-27 — CP-006-11 minted: T2.7's `DaemonSigningKeySealer` seal boundary and the `daemon_signing_keys.sealed_private_key` byte format registered on Plan-022 ([§Preconditions](./022-data-retention-and-gdpr.md#preconditions) + [§Target Areas](./022-data-retention-and-gdpr.md#target-areas), the [cross-plan-dependencies.md §1](../architecture/cross-plan-dependencies.md#1-table-ownership-map) `daemon_signing_keys` row, the [§2](../architecture/cross-plan-dependencies.md#2-package-path-ownership-map) `src/events/` row, and BOTH [§3](../architecture/cross-plan-dependencies.md#3-inter-plan-dependency-graph) rows — Plan-006's and Plan-022's).**

Codex round 2 on PR #256 read the T2.7 deliverable as promising "a self-contained OS-keystore-sealed implementation" and found it unusable outside tests. The quoted plan text was itself the defect. T2.7 as shipped declares `DaemonSigningKeySealer` as an INJECTED boundary and carries no keystore code at all — deliberately, and for a reason that is a corpus fact rather than a preference: **no document specifies the byte format of `daemon_signing_keys.sealed_private_key`.** `Spec-022 §Daemon Master Key` fixes the MASTER key's own custody (the tier ladder, the KEK derivation, the 98-byte envelope) and Plan-022 fixes the `participant_keys.encrypted_key_blob` wrap (XChaCha20-Poly1305, AAD `participant_id || "ais.master-wrap.v1" || key_version`); neither covers this column. Inventing a third format inside Plan-006 would pre-commit every later reader of it — including whatever re-wrap a master-key rotation needs — on a guess. So the OPERATION is declared here and the FORMAT ships with the implementor.

**What was actually wrong was the registration, not the design.** Three surfaces still asserted self-containment: T2.7's `Provides:` had the class "sealing the private key via OS-keystore-managed master key (`@napi-rs/keyring` v1.2.0 …)", which the shipped class does not do; the 2026-05-28 §Open Questions ratification closed "No Plan-022 (Tier 5) dependency → no tier inversion"; and the dep-map §1 `daemon_signing_keys` row read "Self-contained against Plan-022 (Tier 5) — no tier inversion". **The no-tier-inversion CONCLUSION is true on all three and is kept verbatim; only its PREMISE moves.** It rested on there being no Plan-022 dependency; it now rests on the dependency running through an injected interface, so `signing-key-source.ts` imports nothing from Plan-022 and pulls no native keyring binding into the append path's consumers — which is load-bearing beyond tidiness, since on CI legs where a headless Linux box has no Secret Service `Spec-023 §Native Keystore` requires the keystore layer to detect that and refuse rather than silently fall back. The 2026-05-28 ratification is preserved as the record of what was decided then and marked superseded in part, not rewritten. The `@napi-rs/keyring` v1.2.0 ladder and the `keytar`-is-unmaintained supersede move WITH the obligation into CP-006-11 rather than being dropped — losing that parenthetical would re-open the stale-`keytar` hole [Plan-007 CP-007-8](./007-local-ipc-and-daemon-control.md#cp-007-8--daemonkeystore-interface-boundary-owed-to-plan-022-tier-5) exists to close.

CP-006-11 also carries the two obligations the interface can ask for but cannot enforce: the `sessionId` AAD binding (passed on both sides precisely so an implementation CAN bind it, but unenforceable from a contract that has fixed no format — Plan-006's `read` public-key check is a backstop, not a substitute, because an AEAD that will not open never produces key material at all), and composition-root injection of the real sealer with a runtime assertion against the test fake outside tests. Same interface + stub + injection shape as CP-006-1, and it reciprocates the same Plan-022 keystore boundary Plan-007 registers as CP-007-8 — bidirectional for the reason CP-007-8 states: one-directional registration forces the other plan's reviewers to reverse-search.

**Cross-plan touch rationale (Housekeeping Exception, [cross-plan-dependencies.md §Ownership Rule](../architecture/cross-plan-dependencies.md#ownership-rule)):** criteria re-derived for this round rather than carried forward from the 2026-07-26 and 2026-07-27 CP-006-3 entries above — **(a) differs materially from both.** Those rounds could say no code existed at the seam; here T2.7 shipped the seam in this very PR. (a) still holds on the operative test — every edit in this round is governance text plus one code COMMENT, so the change is structural-only with zero runtime or behavioral change — but it holds because nothing executable moved, not because nothing executable exists. (b) first discoverable from the Plan-006 side: the seam did not exist until this PR created it, so Plan-022 could not have surfaced a gap in an obligation it had never been handed; Codex surfaced it here, against this diff. (c) sub-30-LOC and sub-day: one new CP bullet, three Plan-006 in-place true-ups, four dep-map cell edits, two Plan-022 edits, one code comment — a single dispatch. (d) one-time correction: Plan-022 retains ownership of its plan file and Plan-006 claims no ongoing edit right on it. **(e) is this block.** Plan-022's Status is untouched on the CP-006-3/Plan-027 precedent — no Plan-022 task, phase, invariant, CP row, or Required ADR changes, and the implementation lands on the master-key custody ladder Plan-022 already owns at `bootstrap/daemon-key-store.ts` for CP-007-8; the byte-format specification is the genuinely new increment, and it has no other correct home. Plan-006's own Status is likewise unchanged (`approved`): this registers an obligation the shipping code already created rather than introducing a new one.

**Refs:** [Plan-022 §Preconditions](./022-data-retention-and-gdpr.md#preconditions) (the new checkbox carrying all three obligations) and [§Target Areas](./022-data-retention-and-gdpr.md#target-areas) (the `daemon-key-store.ts` line the sealer builds on), `Plan-006 §Cross-Plan Obligations` CP-006-11 (the minted bullet), [Plan-007 CP-007-8](./007-local-ipc-and-daemon-control.md#cp-007-8--daemonkeystore-interface-boundary-owed-to-plan-022-tier-5) (the reciprocal-keystore-boundary precedent, including its bidirectionality rationale and the `keytar` supersede), and `Spec-022 §Daemon Master Key` (the custody ladder the implementation plugs into).

**2026-07-27 — CP-006-12 minted: Plan-022 Path 1 bound to preserve the `session_events.pii_payload` ciphertext bytes, registered on Plan-022 ([§Preconditions](./022-data-retention-and-gdpr.md#preconditions)) and on BOTH [§3](../architecture/cross-plan-dependencies.md#3-inter-plan-dependency-graph) rows — Plan-006's and Plan-022's.**

Phase 2 closes a signed-but-unchecked binding by making `BLAKE3(pii_payload)` = the signed `pii_ciphertext_digest` an unconditional read-side check over every non-NULL `pii_payload` (I-006-2-11). Writing it with **no crypto-shred carve-out** is a claim about a mechanism Plan-022 owns, so the claim is registered as an obligation rather than left as an assumption. It is correct today: [Spec-022 §Shred Fan-Out](../specs/022-data-retention-and-gdpr.md#shred-fan-out) Path 1 destroys the `participant_keys` row and overwrites no column, and the [GDPR Manual Erasure Runbook](../operations/gdpr-manual-erasure-runbook.md) issues no `UPDATE` against `session_events`. A Tier-5 implementation that zeroes or NULLs the column instead flips every shredded row to `pii_ciphertext_digest_unbound` and pages on every GDPR erasure, so Plan-022 owes one of two things: preserve the bytes, or land the skip-condition amendment to [Security Architecture §Verification Rules](../architecture/security-architecture.md#verification-rules) rule 2 and to I-006-2-11 in the same change.

**Registered because the divergence was already latent, not hypothetical.** This plan's own Spec-006 surfaces — the `event.shredded` census row and the §Canonical Serialization Rules `pii_payload` bullet — both described the ciphertext as "zeroed" until this round, overstating Spec-022's mechanism in exactly the direction that would have made the carve-out look necessary. Both are corrected here. An unwritten rule would let the overwrite land silently and leave the column unbound; a written one forces the collision.

**Cross-plan touch rationale (Housekeeping Exception, [cross-plan-dependencies.md §Ownership Rule](../architecture/cross-plan-dependencies.md#ownership-rule)), criteria re-derived rather than carried forward from the CP-006-11 round earlier the same day:** (a) doc-only on the Plan-022 side with zero runtime or behavioral change there — the code shipped in this PR is Plan-006's, and Plan-022 has no Tier-5 code yet to alter; (b) first discoverable from the Plan-006 side, which minted the check and had to resolve the shred-mechanism question to write it; (c) sub-30-LOC and sub-day — one Preconditions checkbox plus two dep-map row clauses; (d) a one-time registration claiming no ongoing edit right on Plan-022, whose Path-1 design decision remains entirely its own. Status unchanged (`approved`) on both plans: this names an obligation Plan-006's shipping check creates rather than introducing new Plan-022 work.

**Refs:** [Plan-022 §Preconditions](./022-data-retention-and-gdpr.md#preconditions), [cross-plan-dependencies.md §3](../architecture/cross-plan-dependencies.md#3-inter-plan-dependency-graph), [Spec-022 §Shred Fan-Out](../specs/022-data-retention-and-gdpr.md#shred-fan-out), [GDPR Manual Erasure Runbook](../operations/gdpr-manual-erasure-runbook.md)

**2026-07-27 — `Spec-022 §Signature Safety Under Shred`'s stale shred mechanism corrected (`docs/specs/022-data-retention-and-gdpr.md`, an approved spec Plan-022 owns).**

That section's "Why This Matters" paragraph stated that after a shred "the ciphertext bytes are zero but the signature still exists on disk." The mechanism is false, and it is contradicted **inside its own file**: [Spec-022 §Shred Fan-Out](../specs/022-data-retention-and-gdpr.md#shred-fan-out) Path 1 destroys the per-participant `participant_keys` row and overwrites no column at all. The ciphertext is RETAINED; what a shred destroys is decryptability. The [GDPR Manual Erasure Runbook](../operations/gdpr-manual-erasure-runbook.md) agrees with the owner and issues no `UPDATE` against `session_events`, its Path-1 verification step spot-checking that a previously-readable `pii_payload` no longer DECRYPTS — a check that presupposes the bytes are still there.

**The provenance is a completeness failure of an earlier sweep in this same PR, not a newly introduced defect, and that is the useful signal for the next reader.** The 2026-07-27 CP-006-12 round corrected exactly this claim in `Spec-006 §Canonical Serialization Rules` and in `security-architecture.md` and did not sweep Spec-022 itself — the file it had just cited as the OWNER of the correct mechanism. The class is the one a canonicalization sweep exists to prevent: when a design-fact value changes, every form of it is swept repo-wide — including in the document the correction was sourced FROM, which is the instance a sweep driven by the consuming plan's own surfaces is most likely to skip. Two invariants now depend on the corrected mechanism being stated consistently everywhere — I-006-2-11's "no crypto-shred carve-out is needed" and I-006-2-12's "the stamp survives Path 1" both fail if a reader takes the stale sentence as the specification.

**Cross-plan touch rationale (Housekeeping Exception, [cross-plan-dependencies.md §Ownership Rule](../architecture/cross-plan-dependencies.md#ownership-rule)), criteria re-derived for this round rather than carried forward from the three entries above:** (a) doc-only, and narrower than any prior round — a factual correction of a mechanism STATEMENT to match the normative text in the same file, with zero runtime or behavioral change and no normative change on either side; Plan-022 has no Tier-5 code yet, and Spec-022's own §Shred Fan-Out is unedited because it was already right. (b) First discoverable from the Plan-006 side, and demonstrably so: Plan-006's read-side checks are what made the mechanism load-bearing enough to sweep for, and this round found the miss by re-deriving the previous round's own sweep rather than by anything on Plan-022's side changing. (c) Sub-30-LOC and sub-day — one paragraph in one section, in a single dispatch. (d) One-time correction — Plan-022 retains ownership of its spec, and Plan-006 claims no ongoing edit right on it or on the shred mechanism, which remains entirely Spec-022's to define; if Plan-022 later changes Path 1, CP-006-12 is the collision point, not this entry. **(e) is this block**, appended per this section's append-only rule. Status stays `approved` on both plans, and on Spec-022: correcting a consumer-facing restatement to match the normative rule beside it changes no requirement, no task, no invariant, and no CP row.

**Refs:** [Spec-022 §Shred Fan-Out](../specs/022-data-retention-and-gdpr.md#shred-fan-out) (Path 1 as the owner specifies it — key destruction, no column overwrite), [GDPR Manual Erasure Runbook](../operations/gdpr-manual-erasure-runbook.md) (the Path-1 verification step that presupposes retained ciphertext), `Plan-006 §Cross-Plan Obligations` CP-006-12 (the obligation both corrected surfaces feed, now covering `pii_payload` and `pii_participant_id`), and the CP-006-12 entry above (the same-PR round whose sweep this completes).

**2026-07-27 — CP-006-3's refusal count corrected on both mirror surfaces (`docs/plans/027-cross-node-dispatch-and-approval.md` §Preconditions, an approved plan Plan-027 owns, and `docs/architecture/cross-plan-dependencies.md` §3's Plan-027 row).**

T2.1's `assertNoToJsonOverride` took the shared `canonicalizeJson` entry point from two refusals to three this round. Both mirrors described the helper as "bounded by two refusals that differ in kind" / "carries two refusals of different kinds" — accurate when written, an undercount now. **The correction states two different numbers deliberately, and conflating them is the failure mode it exists to prevent:** the entry point carries THREE refusals; exactly TWO bind Plan-027; and Plan-027's reciprocal obligations stay at THREE, unchanged. A reader who took only "three" from the count would conclude a third registered rejection reason is owed. None is: Spec-024 bodies reach `canonicalizeJson` through `JSON.parse`, whose output can carry no function-valued member — a wire `{"toJSON":"x"}` parses to a STRING — so the predicate is unsatisfiable on parsed intake and the refusal is unreachable from a dispatch body. Both mirrors now say so in one clause. **The argument is scoped to the parsed-intake path**, and the residual is recorded rather than hidden: a body Plan-027 CONSTRUCTS instead of parsing sits outside it and would meet a live refusal — and Plan-027 has such a path, its caller-side construction step JCS-canonicalizing an in-process body to derive the BLAKE3 `request_body_hash` without ever parsing it. So the residual is concrete rather than hypothetical. What stays open is whether that body can present a `Date`, a `Buffer`, or any other `toJSON`-bearing value once Plan-027's Zod schemas have validated it — recorded in the same clause as a named question for the Spec-024 intake-surface census, deliberately unanswered from this side, since Plan-006 has no view of the construction site and only Plan-027 can enumerate its own intake surfaces.

**Cross-plan touch rationale (Housekeeping Exception, [cross-plan-dependencies.md §Ownership Rule](../architecture/cross-plan-dependencies.md#ownership-rule)), criteria re-derived for this round rather than carried forward:** (a) doc-only and strictly count-correcting — no Plan-027 task, phase, invariant, CP row, Required ADR, or reciprocal obligation changes, and Plan-027 is Tier 9 with no code to affect; the edit removes a false numeral and adds the clause that keeps the true one from being misread. (b) First discoverable from the Plan-006 side, necessarily so: Plan-006 T2.1 is what added the third refusal, and nothing on Plan-027's side moved — the mirrors could not have gone stale from there. (c) Sub-30-LOC and sub-day — one clause plus one explanatory passage in a single §Preconditions checkbox, and one clause in one dep-map row, in a single dispatch. (d) One-time correction; Plan-027 keeps ownership of its plan and of every rejection reason it registers, and Plan-006 claims no ongoing edit right. The identical pair of surfaces was touched for the identical reason by this plan's earlier 2026-07-27 round, which added the third reciprocal obligation to the same checkbox and extended the same dep-map row — this round is strictly smaller, changing a count where that one changed an obligation. **(e) is this block**, appended per this section's append-only rule. Status stays `approved` on both plans.

**Refs:** [Plan-027 §Preconditions](./027-cross-node-dispatch-and-approval.md#preconditions) (the corrected checkbox), [cross-plan-dependencies.md §3](../architecture/cross-plan-dependencies.md#3-inter-plan-dependency-graph) (the corrected Plan-027 row), `Plan-006 §Cross-Plan Obligations` CP-006-3 (the obligation both mirrors restate), `Plan-006 §Invariants` I-006-2-09 (the six-family refusal set the third refusal joins).

**2026-07-27 — CP-006-12's second column carried through to every surface that restates the obligation (`docs/plans/022-data-retention-and-gdpr.md` §Preconditions, an approved plan Plan-022 owns; plus the Plan-006 and Plan-022 rows in `docs/architecture/cross-plan-dependencies.md` §3).**

Widening CP-006-12 to cover `session_events.pii_participant_id` left this plan's CP row saying two columns while Plan-022's own precondition checkbox — "Path 1's crypto-shred MUST leave the `session_events.pii_payload` ciphertext bytes in place" — still named one. **That gap is the whole obligation, not a cosmetic lag.** The party who must HONOR the rule is a Plan-022 Tier-5 implementer deciding what Path 1 may touch, and they read their own §Preconditions, not another plan's Cross-Plan Obligations section. An obligation that is complete only in the document its beneficiary reads and incomplete in the document its duty-holder reads is, operationally, incomplete — and it goes live the moment this PR merges. The checkbox now names both columns and carries the reason the stamp half is sharper than the digest half: Path 1 destroys the key, so no decrypt can re-attribute the retained ciphertext, leaving the stamp the sole surviving evidence of whose data the row held and the thing `Spec-022 §Shred Fan-Out`'s own scope selector reads. It stays `[x]` — the box records that the precondition is registered and understood, which is still true and is now true of both columns.

**Deferring it was considered and rejected.** The earlier draft of this round booked it as wanting "the same one-clause widening in a later round," which names no round, no trigger, and no owner; an ungated deferral on a live obligation is how a corpus acquires exactly the kind of self-contradiction this same PR spent three entries correcting. Small, reversible, non-blocking work of this size lands in the round that creates the need for it.

**Cross-plan touch rationale (Housekeeping Exception, [cross-plan-dependencies.md §Ownership Rule](../architecture/cross-plan-dependencies.md#ownership-rule)), criteria re-derived for this round rather than carried forward:** (a) doc-only, and additive to a checkbox whose normative content is unchanged in KIND — Path 1 already overwrites no column, so preserving the stamp costs Plan-022 nothing and imposes no new work; no Plan-022 task, phase, invariant, CP row, or Required ADR moves, and Plan-022 has no Tier-5 code yet to affect. (b) First discoverable from the Plan-006 side, necessarily: I-006-2-12 was minted here this round and is what extends the obligation — nothing on Plan-022's side changed, so the checkbox could not have gone stale from there. (c) Sub-30-LOC and sub-day — one clause in the checkbox header plus one passage in its body, and one clause in each of the two dep-map §3 rows that restate the obligation, in a single dispatch. The dep-map rows are swept in the SAME pass deliberately: widening the CP row and its consumer checkbox while two graph rows still said one column would have rebuilt, one surface further out, exactly the gap this entry exists to close — and this PR has already corrected three separate instances of a sweep that stopped one document short. (d) One-time; Plan-022 retains ownership of its plan and of the shred mechanism, which stays entirely Spec-022's to define, and Plan-006 claims no ongoing edit right. **2026-08-03 — T1.12's touches on Plan-003-owned surfaces (PR #285): the five `RuntimeNode*Payload` declarations converted from `interface` to object type alias and a third `event.ts` → `event-core.ts` declaration-home attribution repointed, both in `packages/contracts/src/runtime-node.ts`; plus the `capabilities` payload gloss trued up in [`docs/plans/003-runtime-node-attach.md`](./003-runtime-node-attach.md) §Phase 2 T2.1.**

1. **The five `interface` → object-type-alias conversions.** T1.12 composes Plan-003's five `runtime_node.*` payload schemas into `SessionEventSchema` arms, and the Plan-003 emitter feeding them (`packages/runtime-daemon/src/node/node-event-emitter.ts`) widens the same payload TYPES to `AppendableEvent.payload`'s `Record<string, unknown>`. TypeScript grants an object type ALIAS an implicit index signature and grants an INTERFACE none, so while the five were interfaces that widening was reachable only through an `as unknown as` double cast — an assertion the compiler cannot check, at the one seam where a wrong payload shape becomes canonical bytes. `RuntimeNodeRegisteredPayload`, `RuntimeNodeOnlinePayload`, `RuntimeNodeOfflinePayload`, `RuntimeNodeCapabilityDeclaredPayload` and `RuntimeNodeCapabilityUpdatedPayload` are now `export type X = { … }` with member lists unchanged member-for-member. **Zero schema, wire, or public-API change:** each still annotates its own `z.ZodType<…>` schema export unchanged, `export type` and `export interface` present the same import surface, and no consumer relied on declaration merging (no second declaration of any of the five exists under `packages/` or `apps/`). The single downstream consequence is compile-time only and runs the safe direction — the emitter's `as unknown as` double cast collapses to one checked widening, and casts erase, so no emitted byte moves.
2. **The third declaration-home attribution.** The T1.12 Files cell recorded two in-file comments naming `event.ts` as a moved symbol's declaration home, repointed to `event-core.ts` alongside the import repoint. There were three: the `runtime_node.registered` `nodeVersion` field-type rationale also cites where `EVENT_ENVELOPE_VERSION_PATTERN` is declared. It was repointed in the same edit; the T1.12 Files cell and the [§2 Package Path Ownership Map](../architecture/cross-plan-dependencies.md#2-package-path-ownership-map) `packages/contracts/src/` Notes cell now both say three.
3. **The `capabilities[]` gloss.** [Spec-006 §Runtime Node Lifecycle (runtime_node_lifecycle)](../specs/006-session-event-taxonomy-and-audit-log.md#runtime-node-lifecycle-runtime_node_lifecycle)'s `runtime_node.registered` row glossed the payload extension as `capabilities[]` — array notation — while the shipped Plan-003 schema is `z.record(z.string(), z.unknown())`, a keyed map. This round trues the spec row to the record form (Plan-006 owns Spec-006, and correcting a gloss to match shipped code is not scope growth, so no Status moves) and sweeps the two surfaces that quoted the old gloss: `runtime-node.ts`'s field-type rationale, which recorded the divergence and now records the alignment, and Plan-003's T2.1 §Step, which restates the payload extension inline. Leaving a plan step quoting a spec line that no longer exists is the stale-claim class this PR has already corrected several times over.

**Cross-plan touch rationale (Housekeeping Exception, [cross-plan-dependencies.md §Ownership Rule](../architecture/cross-plan-dependencies.md#ownership-rule)), criteria re-derived for this round rather than carried forward:** (a) type-level and doc-only — the conversions change declaration FORM, not members, so every structural type Plan-003's Tier-3 code compiles against is identical; the attribution and gloss edits are comment and prose text. No schema, no runtime branch, no wire byte moves. (b) First discoverable from the Plan-006 side, necessarily: the implicit-index-signature requirement arises from composing these payloads into an `event.ts` union arm and from the emitter widening that composition made checkable — Plan-003 shipped its schemas without ever needing the index signature, so nothing on its side could have surfaced it; likewise the attribution, which only became wrong when T1.12 moved the symbol out of `event.ts`. (c) Sub-30-LOC and sub-day — five keyword changes, one comment path, and one quoted gloss in each of two files, in a single dispatch. (d) One-time correction — Plan-003 keeps ownership of `runtime-node.ts`, of its payload shapes, and of its own plan file; Plan-006 claims no ongoing edit right, and this entry closes the touch rather than opening a channel. Directly on point: Plan-009 T1.2's verbatim relocation of the `NodeId` cluster into `node-id.ts` was a strictly LARGER touch on this same owned file, under this same exception, for the same import-cycle class ([Plan-009 §Cross-Plan Amendments](./009-repo-attachment-and-workspace-binding.md#cross-plan-amendments)). **(e) is this block**, appended per this section's append-only rule. Status stays `approved` on Plan-003 and on Spec-006.

**The inbound reciprocal — the receive-append and compaction seam sanction ([Plan-008 §Cross-Plan Obligations](./008-control-plane-relay-and-session-join.md#cross-plan-obligations) CP-008-13), recorded here from the owning side.** The provenance amendment's write and preservation seams land as three Plan-008-authored edits inside this plan's shipped `events/` module — `canonicalizer.ts` gains the conditional `receivedFromNodeId` canonical-member emission, `event-log-service.ts` the optional received-origin append parameter that stamps the Plan-008-owned marker column, and `compactor.ts` the conditional `receivedFromNodeId` / `channelId` stub-projection preservation per [Spec-006 §Compacted Event Format](../specs/006-session-event-taxonomy-and-audit-log.md#compacted-event-format) — shipping with Plan-008 T-008r-4-14's Tier-5 code, not with any task of this plan, and scheduled rather than landed here (this delta stays doc-only). CP-008-13 registers the sanction from the consuming side under the [CP-003-5](./003-runtime-node-attach.md#cross-plan-obligations) per-file-crossing shape — additive, byte-governed by [Spec-006 §Canonical Serialization Rules](../specs/006-session-event-taxonomy-and-audit-log.md#canonical-serialization-rules) and §Compacted Event Format (an origin-authored append with no parameter stays byte-identical to today's output, and a stub of a row carrying neither conditional field projects byte-identically to today's), and scoped to exactly those three edits, everything else in the module staying consumed by import only — and the [§2 map's `events/` row](../architecture/cross-plan-dependencies.md#2-package-path-ownership-map) carries the extender registration. This paragraph is what lets a reviewer of this plan's `events/` module read the crossing as sanctioned rather than as an unregistered foreign edit.

**Cross-plan touch rationale (Housekeeping Exception, [cross-plan-dependencies.md §Ownership Rule](../architecture/cross-plan-dependencies.md#ownership-rule)):** (a) doc-only — governance text with zero live-code deltas (every surface this delta edits is governance text over unshipped Tier-4/Tier-5 work; of the shipped code surfaces named, AttachFlow is touched by NOTHING — its planned seam edit is retracted — and the `events/` pair's sanctioned edits are scheduled onto Plan-008 T-008r-4-14's future Tier-5 code, landing nothing here); (b) each edit is the mechanical consequence of the T4.10 re-specification and Spec-006 amendment this delta owns — no other plan's design is re-decided; (c) the deletion sweep is loud, enumerated here and at the §6 NS-58 node, never silent.

**Refs:** `Spec-008 §Peer History Backfill On Join (V1)` (both pins), T4.10 + T4.2 (the re-specified surfaces), CP-006-4 / CP-006-7 / CP-006-14 (the obligation records), I-006-4-08 / I-006-4-09 (the invariant pair), [Spec-006 §Canonical Serialization Rules](../specs/006-session-event-taxonomy-and-audit-log.md#canonical-serialization-rules) (the conditional member), [Plan-008 §Cross-Plan Obligations](./008-control-plane-relay-and-session-join.md#cross-plan-obligations) CP-008-13 (the inbound seam sanction).

**2026-08-12 — CP-008-15 store-lineage watermark accepted on the `daemon_signing_keys` table (Codex PR #323 round 3).**

[Plan-008 §Cross-Plan Obligations](./008-control-plane-relay-and-session-join.md#cross-plan-obligations) CP-008-15 registers a Plan-008-shipped additive extension of this plan's `daemon_signing_keys` table (T2.7's store, migration `0005-daemon-signing-keys.ts`): the PK widens `(session_id)` → `(session_id, node_id)` and `superseded_at_sequence INTEGER` (NULL = active) is added, one row per `NodeId` the store has ever been registered under, the watermark stamped at admission-time registration when a fresh `NodeId` takes over a carried-forward store — so `Spec-008 §Peer History Backfill On Join (V1)`'s serving selector durably labels origin-authored rows and derives `servesForOrigins` from lineage rows rather than guessing. Plan-008 ships the lineage migration and the selector; this plan's ownership of the table, its sealing model, and the rotation reservation are unchanged (the predecessor row's sealed private key is dead weight after carry — per-machine OS-keystore sealing is deliberately unrecoverable elsewhere; its `public_key` + watermark are the durable lineage record). Canonical DDL: [Local SQLite Schema §Audit Log Crypto Tables (Plan-006)](../architecture/schemas/local-sqlite-schema.md#audit-log-crypto-tables-plan-006); the [cross-plan-dependencies.md §1](../architecture/cross-plan-dependencies.md#1-table-ownership-map) entry carries the contested-extension note.

- 2026-08-26 — **Per-limit quota key (the Plan-029 CP-029-8 reciprocal, cross-plan §6 node NS-83; Status flipped `approved → review` and restored `approved` in the same swap).** `usage.rate_limit_update` gains an additive-optional `limitId` and the axis re-keys to `(providerAccountId, limitId)`. This is the **second** re-key of this event and the two close different collisions, which is worth recording so the pair does not read as one fix applied twice: 2026-08-18 closed a collision **across accounts** (two registered accounts of one provider overwrote each other's reading); this one closes a collision **within** one account, which the account-scoped key still admitted, because the pinned Claude surface publishes five limit identifiers of which **three share a 10080-minute window**. Keying on the window length therefore retained one of the three with the survivor decided by arrival order — the operator would be shown one weekly limit as their weekly standing, two others silently gone, and the survivor changing between restarts. `windowMins` stays on the payload as a property of the reading rather than part of its identity. The consumer obligations in the Event Type Summary row are scoped **per key** in the same pass: the drop-a-lower-reading rule now requires the same `limitId` as well as the same `resetsAt`, because a comparison spanning two limits of one account was never meaningful. Registration is a payload-member addition on an existing type — **no event type minted, no category moved, and the 156-type / 20-category censuses are unmoved**; the producer, the durable window store, and the renderer are Plan-029's and Plan-013's respectively.

- 2026-08-30 — **Durable encrypted home for machine-authored prose (§6 node NS-94).** Status flipped `approved → review` and restored `approved` in the same diff, jointly with Spec-006, by the targeted readiness-audit delta riding that diff. Assistant- and tool-generated prose had no durable home in the corpus — the shipped `packages/contracts/src/event.ts` registers 25 `SessionEventSchema` payload variants, none of them assistant or tool, so both driver normalizers resolve every one of those types `payload-variant-pending` and forbid envelope construction, and no variant could be registered because the payload shapes named no member carrying the body. This plan takes ownership of the fix, on the `pii_participant_id` precedent (Plan-006-created via its own additive migration, not Plan-001 forward-declared): `session_events.content_payload` is added to §Data And Storage Changes, specified in the new §Content Payload Column (Machine-Authored Prose) as the Encrypt-Then-Digest-Then-Sign order re-run under a **session**-scoped stored DEK with one embedded member instead of two, and built by the new **Phase 3B** supplement (T3.6, T3.7, T3.8) — a supplement because Phase 3 merged 2026-08-04 and a merged phase is not amendable in place (the Plan-016 / Plan-004 Phase-3B precedent). Invariants **I-006-3-05** … **I-006-3-08** and obligation **CP-006-15** (⇄ CP-005-13, mechanical via Plan-005 T3.20's `external_plan_phase_merged` gate) are minted; both §Preconditions boxes are re-opened scoped to that growth and re-checked in-diff. **Round-2 fold (Codex PR #383 round 1 — eight findings, all accepted).** The sealing key is re-designed from `HKDF-SHA256` over the daemon master key to a **stored, re-wrappable per-session DEK** in the new `session_content_keys`: the round-1 derivation would have been destroyed by [Spec-022](../specs/022-data-retention-and-gdpr.md)' rotate-on-shred on any participant's erasure, silently taking every existing body on the daemon with it, and rotation now re-wraps this table inside its **existing** `BEGIN EXCLUSIVE` rather than in a pass of its own. T3.8 is added for verify-on-read (the sixteenth mode `content_ciphertext_digest_unbound`, provenance-dispatched, with the received-row require-absent arm) and the `HydratedSessionEvent` read path that never splices a body into signed `payload`. T3.6 gains the five body-bearing `SessionEventSchema` variants — without which the column could be created and sealed but never written to — and T3.7 gains `content_payload` in both byte-accounting statements (without it a content-bearing session reaches ~12.5 GiB before the count trigger fires) plus the stub's surviving `contentLength` / `contentTruncated`. The column is confirmed **node-local**: never relayed, a peer's projection reporting `'turn_content_unavailable'` as a named residual whose closing swap owns the key-distribution leg — which a stored key makes possible and a derived one never could. **One census moves**: local SQLite **58 → 59** for `session_content_keys`; Postgres stays **26**; no event type or category is minted, so the Spec-006 taxonomy stays **159** across **20**; no wire method and no error code. One count moves and is re-derived by counting the union's members — `DeclaredLossKind` **five → six** for `'turn_content_truncated'`, mirrored into Plan-016's two count claims on a recorded no-flip. The migration ordinal is deliberately unpinned (`0NNN-content-payload.ts`) because `0012` is claimed by an in-flight branch and ordinals are taken in merge order.
